# ADR classification — the cold pass

**Generated. Do not hand-edit** — edit `CLASSIFICATION.tsv` and run
`uv run python tools/gen_adr_index.py`. Guarded by `tools/check_adr_classification.py`.

Prologue **pass 1** of the refactor loop (`docs/agents/refactor-loop.md`): every ADR gets a
**bucket**, an **owning system**, the ADRs it **constrains**, and a **status**. No ADR prose was
touched — rewriting happens per chunk in the loop, where the context to do it well exists.

**Buckets** — `engine` survives any rename and travels with its system into the addon ·
`seam` is a PSX compromise that defines where a conversion boundary goes · `content` is
FFT-specific and stays with the content pack · `method` is how the work is conducted, stays
in the host, and can be *closed* rather than superseded when the refactor ends.

**Span** — an ADR reaching two systems is answered three ways: `split` (the only defect),
`interface` (a seam already written down — the most valuable documents here), or `platform`
(promoted above every system).

`cited_by` counts files under `src/`, `assets/` and `addons/` carrying an `ADR-NNNN`
mention. It is **measured on every run**, not recorded — see this script's docstring for
what the hand-kept column was worth. `docs/` and `tests/` are deliberately outside the
scope: this number answers *what code is standing on this decision*.

| [0219](0219-a-cell-is-x-z-level-because-the-rom-bounds-checks-a-third-coordinate-we-drop.md) A cell is `(x, z, level)` — the ROM bounds-checks a third coordinate we drop on the floor | `engine` | interface | · | 33 | `0119`, `0164`, `0166`, `0192` |
| | | | | | The ROM resolves a tile as tile_ptr(x, z, level) @ 0x80183fb4 -- sltiu v0,a2,0x2 bounds-checks level < 2 and strides it by level << 8 -- and a live logging breakpoint caught 738 of 3,321 calls in ~2s passing level=1, all tile_ptr(4,2,1). We resolve (x, z), so DynamicTerrainBuilder.gd:49 reads level_0 only and grep 'level_1' over all .gd returns ZERO. Not one bridge: 202 selectable level-1 tiles across 44 of 119 exported maps. Adopts Vector3i(x, z, level) as the cell identity -- transcribed, not invented: the actor array (stride 0x440, NOT the 0x1C0 table at 0x801908CC, which was the wrong base) carries (x,z,level) as three adjacent bytes at +0x80, and only Algus of six live actors carries level=1, the unit the chunk's only Z=1 instruction targets. Amends ADR-0192 dec. 6 and ADR-0166 dec. 3 on ARITY ONLY: all four of their reasons (value equality, one type for one concept, staleness, one spelling for the key) hold for Vector3i verbatim -- nothing in either argument was about the number two. Axis order (x,z,level) is the ROM's own argument order and leaves 31 existing .x/.y sites correct unchanged. TerrainCell.NONE becomes Vector3i.MIN; current_cell becomes Vector3i; TerrainIndex._make_key(x,z)->String is DELETED not widened (a String key is a second spelling ADR-0166 dec. 3 forbids). Level LATCHES, never interpolates -- witnessed field-at-a-time retirement at +0x7C/+0x80 -- so a mover carries it, and inferring it from height is banned (that is what the deleted _op_walk_to comment did). Census: Walk To and Warp Unit take Z in {0,1} over 634 instructions corpus-wide, while Camera's identically-named Z has 406 distinct values and is a world coord. Rejects (b) parallel level field on ADR-0119 dec. 3's never-two-fields-one-axis rule, (c) flatten at export as PROVABLY wrong (MAP060 has 6 level-1 Bridge tiles over walkable StoneFloor h3), and (d) infer from a height band (no threshold separates MAP012's level-1 h15 from MAP083's h9). Deliberately does NOT settle map arrangements (exporter writes PRIMARY only of six -- suspected cause of MAP058) or which ROM routine writes the level byte. R: #789. |
| [0221](0221-a-cells-marking-is-slotted-and-the-highlight-publish-is-its-only-writer.md) A cell's marking is slotted, and the highlight publish is its only writer | `engine` | interface | · | 6 | `0164`, `0166`, `0194`, `0196`, `0218`, `0219` |
| | | | | | Tile.current_highlight_type is ONE slot with THREE writers and no arbiter: CursorController._set_active_tile, CursorController._on_camera_mode_changed, and TileHighlights.paint driven by StrategyPhaseManager / PlacementTileHighlighter. Both cursor writers save the displaced value into ONE member, _active_prev_type, captured when the cursor ARRIVED -- so a march pick landing on that tile is erased by the cursor's restore-on-leave, and the user-visible symptom is the pick going dark the moment the player moves to choose a destination. CursorController._process exists ONLY to re-assert CURSOR_ACTIVE every frame against the same collision, which is why the damage only ever showed on the square the cursor LEFT. ADR-0164 dec.1 measured the publish's external clients as src/strategy/ only, 14 lines, and that measurement is why this was invisible: two of the three writers are INSIDE the addon, so the census that established the publish never saw them. Slots the marking -- CURSOR > SELECTION > TERRAIN_SET, topmost occupied renders -- with the slot DERIVED from the kind so paint(cell, kind) keeps two arguments and src/strategy/ is relieved of arbitration rather than handed it in a new spelling. Derivation lives in TileHighlights, not on CellMarking: the schema names what a marking IS (ADR-0196 dec.6), the publish decides which of several a tile SHOWS. Table keyed Vector3i in TileHighlights (ADR-0219 dec.1 / ADR-0166 dec.3, the key both sides already hold); _bind DROPS it; an absent cell records NOTHING, because a table that accumulated cells the map lacks is a second divergent store of what terrain exists. Mints SELECTED as a sixth CellMarking.Kind -- the march pick was painting CURSOR_ACTIVE, so the two writers were writing the SAME value and no arbiter could have told them apart; it renders as the cursor dimmed, opaque blend 4 to stay on the in-scene path rather than routing through TileOverlayCompositor. Tile.set_highlight_type / clear_highlight are underscored to the ADR-0164 dec.2 addon-internal spelling with a static arm pinning TileHighlights.gd as sole caller; outside Tile.gd the tree holds exactly 11 executing lines on the three highlight members (8 CursorController, 3 TileHighlights) and the rest is prose, which is what makes the rule enforceable. CursorController acquires highlights in seed_from_map and paints through the publish; _active_prev_type, the whole _process override and the third writer are DELETED, a takeover clears the cursor slot and returning repaints it. kind_at is KEPT but re-justified: it answers about the RENDERED tile, not the table, and its principal caller is a test. Rejects (b) fixing the restore's TIMING (no capture instant survives the interleaving; _process is that family's last member and it patches the one square where the damage is invisible), (c) a separate cursor overlay node (fixes rendering, leaves the arbitration and leaves _active_prev_type alive for the placement writers), (d) the slot array on Tile (state where every writer can reach it IS the arrangement being replaced), and (e) a slot parameter on paint (wider interface, and the only new thing a caller could do with it is disagree). Leaves alone, named: StrategyPhaseManager:333's fallback repaint (it also refreshes claimed_cells and :295 has no repaint of its own), TileCursorIntegrationTest's masking lambda, and :295's missing repaint. Arms: CursorConfirmEndToEndTest's walk-away arm, seen RED on the parent commit reporting 1 (PLACEMENT_PLAYER), plus a scene-free TileHighlightsTest inside the addon over TerrainFixture (ADR-0218, ADR-0194). |
| [0250](0250-world-quad-is-about-billboarding-not-about-being-level-so-the-tile-decal-drapes.md) `world_quad` is about billboarding, not about being level — so the tile decal drapes | `engine` | · | · | 2 | `0009`, `0074`, `0191`, `0212`, `0247` |
| | | | | | WORLD_QUAD IS ABOUT BILLBOARDING, NOT ABOUT BEING LEVEL, SO THE TILE DECAL DRAPES. The routed tile highlights (green deployment zone, blue Move, red Attack) lay FLAT on sloped ground; on Gariland's zone that is tiles (5,2)/(6,2), a matched InclineEast/InclineWest pair at the zone centre. Cause: one expression in TileOverlayCompositor._append_tile had the four real world corners in hand and substituted the centroid Y for all four, defended by a comment ('slope dropped -- a slope-conforming decal would be Path 2') and asserted by a test. There is no Path 2. dec. 1: each corner keeps its OWN world Y plus DECAL_LIFT; on a Flat tile the bake is identical, so level ground is unchanged. dec. 2: geometry = world_quad constrains how the verts are PRODUCED -- baked ground positions, never expanded about a centroid at draw time (the ADR-0074 s4b billboard fix) -- and says nothing about sharing a Y; flattening was that fix over-applied, and ADR-0074 never asked for it. dec. 3: the DEPTH stays flat and is a DIFFERENT axis -- CUSTOM0 remains one shared lifted centroid, one OT depth per face (ADR-0009, the GTE's AVSZ4 model); the guard now locks the two halves AGAINST each other, because 'make the decal follow the slope' is exactly the edit that would make CUSTOM0 per-corner too. dec. 4: no new data and no new RE were needed -- terrain.json already ships four per-corner vertices per tile with decoded slope_type/slope_height, and the IN-SCENE highlight path always used them verbatim, so whether a highlight followed the terrain depended only on BLEND MODE: PLACEMENT_UNAVAILABLE (0) and CURSOR_ACTIVE (4) were already draping on the very tiles where PLACEMENT_PLAYER (1) was not. SOURCES: the ROM drapes a ground decal per corner -- vault/Unit Shadow Rendering.md [S.D.R] 3/3, vertex builder FUN_8007b9d0 @ 0x8007B9D0 (BATTLE.BIN), slopes drape per-corner via tile-shape byte tile[+4]; GaneshaDx TerrainTile.SetRenderVertices lifts a SlopeType-selected corner subset by 12+(SlopeHeight-1)*12 and 8 of the ROM's 9 cited slope codes appear in its 13-entry table; our exporter agrees numerically (MAP001 (0,0) InclineSouth h=2 -> corner Ys 3.38/2.90/2.90/3.38 = a 24-unit lift at 0.02/unit); the Blender addon keeps slope_type as the raw byte for byte-exact round-trip and never decodes it; MapGridOverlay is the in-repo precedent (same addon, same fold, per-corner Y with one shared centroid); and UnitShadow.gd already drapes. REJECTED: a 'Path 2' second path (it is one expression, the corners are already in hand); per-corner CUSTOM0; leaving the flatness assertion and adding a new one beside it (the old assertion was the defect written down, and two guards would disagree while the suite stayed green either way). BUILT: TileOverlayCompositorTest 19 -> 24, flatness assertion DELETED not relaxed; 2 seeded defects each reddening only its own arm. The guard asks the drape CORNER BY CORNER (emitted order 0,1,2,0,2,3), because 'the Ys differ' is also true of a decal draped over the WRONG corners. NOTE: the first research pass searched ~/Obsidian Vault, a near-empty decoy, and recorded the zero as a finding; the vault is vault/ IN THE REPO, 254 docs. |

| [0224](0224-the-gpu-battle-mover-addresses-a-cell-so-the-map-is-two-planes-and-the-unit-carries-its-level.md) The GPU battle mover addresses a cell, so the map is two planes and the unit carries its level | `engine` | interface | · | 8 | `0001`, `0164`, `0166`, `0192`, `0219` |
| | | | | | Battle PLACEMENT is level-aware and shipped; battle MOVEMENT is not, by explicit declaration, and the gap is a live defect: ScenarioPlacementSource seats an enemy at (x,z,1) from ENTD upper_level, GPUCombatPacker packs POS_X/POS_Z and DROPS cell.z while packing U_HEIGHT from terrain_at(cell) -- the level-1 height, correctly -- so the shader's get_tile_height(x,z) answers the LEVEL-0 height for the same column and a unit's own height contradicts the floor it stands on. Of 21 ENTD slots with upper_level=true, 12 are REACHABLE via scenarios.json (8 records, 6 maps: MAP012/057/060/063/070/083) and every one sits over a PASSABLE level-0 cell with a height gap of 7 to 15; the other 9 (records 144,207,208,210,273,472) have no scenario naming their ENTD index and are not a consumer. The seam is 6 accessor functions: exactly 8 lines do column arithmetic, all in combat_common.glslinc:759-802, behind ~100 call sites (44 get_tile_height, 22 get_distance, 16 is_tile_traversable, 15 get_tile_occupant, 3 is_tile_occupied) -- wide, not deep. 'Movement only' is NOT available: 27 of the 44 height reads are stage_attack/stage_spell/combat_combat range, arc and line-of-sight, so widening the accessor forces a targeting ruling. Adopts a DENSE two-plane identity level*(w*h)+z*w+x for map buffer, distance-field node numbering and every accessor; total_tiles stays the PER-LEVEL count and the matrix becomes (2*total_tiles)^2 -- 0.3MB to 1.0MB worst case (MAP125), negligible to negligible, so memory must not argue against the plane. Buffer is LEVEL-MAJOR ([L0: heights|traversable|cliff->L0 x4|cliff->L1 x4][L1: same], 20 planes, dec.2 as it now stands) because that keeps level 0 byte-for-byte where it is and lets the map buffer widen in a commit that CANNOT move behaviour; the distance field cannot widen inertly (its matrix STRIDE is total_tiles) so it stays one plane until the shader pass. Cliff bits go 4 to 8 per cell, one per (direction, target level); computing a cross-level verdict in the shader is refused because is_cliff_edge reads tile_vertices, which ADR-0164 dec.2 put on the port. Unit record gains U_LEVEL/U_DEST_LEVEL/U_PROPOSED_LEVEL (UNIT_SIZE 98->101, SHADER_VERSION 29->30), declared in the shader and GENERATED into GDScript per ADR-0001; three not one because DEST and PROPOSED persist across ticks at possibly different levels, and they alias pos_* exactly as the existing pairs do. DBG_NEXT gains none; dec.4 says AOE_CENTER gains none too and the 2026-09-05 amendment RETRACTS that -- the collapse reads the aimed cell, so AOE_CENTER gains a LEVEL. Adjacency is ADR-0219 dec.6 VERBATIM -- four column-to-column neighbours, every admissible cell of the target column a candidate low-to-high, climb gate decides, no fifth/sixth move -- because EventPathfinder does not CHOOSE a level, it expands both; the GPU analogue is 4x2 candidates minimising get_distance. Occupancy becomes level-aware (get_tile_occupant is a unit SCAN, not a plane, so it is one comparison) and a unit under a bridge stops blocking one on it; single-target resolves the target's U_LEVEL, empty-tile targets resolve to ground, AoE was ruled COLUMN-wide and is RETRACTED by the 2026-09-05 amendment -- the ROM's effect grid collapses the column against the aimed cell's own height and erases beyond `vertical` levels (324 of 368 abilities), so the falloff rule WAS measured; the cursor stays column-addressed (#795 deferred). Per-unit Jump is explicitly OUT and named: the field bakes ONE jump_range (3.0) for every unit, U_JUMP=11 already rides unused in the record, and the event mover's WALK_TO_CLIMB=3 is a ROM literal for {28} Walk To that matches 3.0 by coincidence. No feature flag -- a flag threads a branch through ~100 call sites to provide what git revert provides; instead the 74 level-0-only maps must come out BYTE-IDENTICAL and that ratchet lands FIRST. Writeback VALIDATES: GPUVisualBridge resolves the level through the lattice and falls back to ground_at with a logged ERROR, matching the null check on the line above it. Bounds do not move: 125 walkable level-1 cells across 35 maps (209 minted across 45, 84 impassable) and ZERO lie outside the ground walkable box. The one non-free cost is stage_pathfind.glsl:82 int visited[8] -- 256 bits, and MAP125 has exactly 256 columns -- which becomes visited[16] plus stack_l[32]. Rejects (b) interleaved two-slot column as NOT A SAVING (same slot count, and it forfeits the staging property), (c) a sparse side table (saves 0.7MB by adding an indirection to the innermost loop and making the byte-identity ratchet unstateable), (d) plane-major (tidier, but no commit's inertness could be proven), (e) a config flag, and (f) inferring level from height (MAP012 h15-over-h0 vs MAP057 h8-over-h1). States that the first half does NOT fix the defect. R: #816, #817. |
| [0235](0235-reconfigure-is-an-overlay-and-the-shader-write-set-classifies-the-fields.md) `reconfigure` is an overlay, and the shader-write set classifies the fields | `engine` | · | · | 7 | `0001`, `0002`, `0003`, `0016`, `0047`, `0224` |
| | | | | | GambitBattle keystone (#888). RECONFIGURE IS AN OVERLAY, AND THE SHADER-WRITE SET CLASSIFIES THE FIELDS. The design doc's SS2 asks for a 'lossless state <-> config round-trip' covering 'RNG stream position'; both clauses are false and the build says so. There is no return leg -- UNIT_CONFIG_SCHEMA partitions the 101-int unit block into 45 config-derived offsets and 56 hand-written init constants which ARE the live combat state, and _extract_unit_config reads a CPU-side Unit node, so no buffer->config direction exists anywhere in the tree; and rand_int is a STATELESS PCG hash of (battle_seed, unit_id, tick), so there is no stream cursor to capture. SS2's conclusion (build it first, gate it on bit-identity) stands. dec. 1: two primitives with two criteria -- snapshot_battle/restore_battle (bytes<->bytes) and reconfigure_unit (a selective write) -- both on the simulator/packer layer and on no host. dec. 2: schema MEMBERSHIP is the classification (ADR-0003); a parallel table would be a second drift surface. dec. 3: each row declares recompute|clamp|carry and a row without one is a BOOT ERROR, not a default -- clamp keeps the live value under the recomputed ceiling (SS4's clamp-never-scale; scaling makes toggling a +HP item a free heal), carry leaves it alone, and the 56 non-schema offsets are carry implicitly. 27/16/2 over 45 rows. dec. 4: THE FLOOR IS DERIVED FROM THE SHADER -- shader_written_unit_fields() scans write_unit() call sites out of src/gpu/shaders/ (57 fields) and overlay_behaviour_problems() errors if any of them is recompute, because a field the kernel writes is live state by definition. A hand-audit produced pos_* and status_flags_*; the SCAN added three more it had missed -- pending_heal_* and, the one that matters, wp/s_ev, which Break Weapon / Break Shield zero (combat_combat.glslinc formula 37), so recompute would have SILENTLY REPAIRED a broken weapon on any job change. One-sided on purpose: shader-written implies not-recompute, but a field the shader never writes may still be carry -- `height` is, since its extractor reads the stale CPU cell (and is read by NOTHING in the kernel since ADR-0224 dec. 6). dec. 5: wp/s_ev are carry, so a break is never silently repaired; whether an equipment change repairs one is #894's rule, not a classification. dec. 6: a snapshot is FOUR slices (battle, cooldown SSBO, gambit SSBO, results) and the ping-pong cursor is NOT one -- only the CURRENT half is captured and restore writes it into BOTH, because compute_unit_state opens every tick with copy_header_to_next + copy_unit_to_next, both total, so the write half carries nothing forward; capturing both halves would force the batch-global _current_buffer into a per-battle artifact and corrupt every other battle on restore. The gambit slice is readonly to the shader and captured precisely because it is what the PLAYER edits. dec. 7: restore_battle takes a TARGET battle id (restore_battle(k, snapshot_battle(0)) is SS7's fork) and the dict is plain data so a caller can edit the SEED before restoring, which is how SS7 spaces its CRN seeds. dec. 8: two test layers. GPUBattleSnapshotTest runs the gate on the seed-locked 4v4 RE-GAMBITED TO CAST -- every ability carries cooldown_ticks 300, so with attack-only gambits the cooldown slice stays zero and the arm covering it passes vacuously; the arms print each slice's nonzero count for that reason. ARM A IS STRICTLY WEAKER THAN ARM B AND THE SEEDED DEFECTS PROVE IT: with restore writing only half 0, A passes and B fails on cast_timer 90-vs-88; with the cooldown slice dropped, A passes again and B fails on aoe_pending_caster 5-vs--1 -- a no-tick restore-then-snapshot reads back the unrestored slice, which still equals the source. B's 181 ticks are ODD on purpose so the second run starts on the opposite half. A REAL BATTLE ONLY DIVERGES THE FIELDS IT HAPPENS TO TOUCH, so the exhaustive half is PURE: UnitEncodeSchemaTest arm 6 saturates all 101 offsets with a distinct sentinel and asserts the three behaviours plus every non-schema offset, including that the clamp actually BIT. |
| [0236](0236-the-turn-meter-is-a-gpu-unit-field-and-it-is-not-called-ct.md) The turn meter is a GPU unit field, and it is not called CT | `engine` | · | · | 6 | `0001`, `0002`, `0003`, `0235` |
| | | | | | GambitBattle turn clock (#889). THE TURN METER IS A GPU UNIT FIELD, AND IT IS NOT CALLED CT. The design's SS3 names the field CT; this kernel already spends that spelling on ability CHARGE TIME (AB_CHARGE_TIME, U_CAST_TIMER, get_ability_charge_time, AbilityView.ct, and the 'ct' key on all 512 ability rows), and it separately inherited FFT's OTHER CT in the UI -- UnitInfoPresenter's ct bar row is the 0..100 turn counter and has never had a producer (FormationScene passes has_ct false; every other caller hard-codes a number). dec. 1: the name is turn_meter / U_TURN_METER at offset 101, counting UP where every *_TIMER in the block counts down; the on-screen label stays CT because that is what the game prints. dec. 2: the kernel ADVANCES it and never READS it, so a GambitBattle turn cannot change what a GPUArena battle does by construction; it is on the GPU so it round-trips inside a battle snapshot (ADR-0235) for free, is correct inside every rollout, and is visible to the value function. dec. 3: READY IS A VALUE, NOT A FLAG -- the kernel clamps at TURN_METER_FULL so turn_meter >= FULL is readiness with no companion bit to disagree, and consume_turn subtracts 100 CARRYING the overshoot and REFUSES a non-ready unit (returns -1) because subtracting from a clamped-below value drives it negative. dec. 4: the seeded start is CPU-side, a GDScript mirror of the kernel's own pcg_hash, and a PARAMETER of _write_unit_data in team's position -- a per-slot value a live Unit cannot supply; mirrored not dispatched because the forecast is asked for at deployment, before the first tick. dec. 5: ONLY DEATH STOPS THE CLOCK -- the advance sits BEFORE the cinematic-pause gate, because a cinematic raises U_PAUSED on every unit EXCEPT its caster and gating there would hand the caster free meter every spell. dec. 6: the forecast is a CLOSED-FORM projection in src/gpu/TurnQueue.gd, one full round-robin deep, ordered meter-DESC then team then index -- the design fixes the tie-break and 'tie' means equal meter. dec. 7: max(1, speed) is load-bearing in BOTH halves and forecast()'s budget counts ITERATIONS not emitted entries, because a zero-gain unit spins while emitting nothing and an entry-counted belt would never fire. dec. 8: two layers, split as ADR-0235 split its own -- TurnQueueTest is pure and exhaustive over cases a battle cannot reach and checks the closed-form jump against a dumb one-tick-at-a-time oracle; GPUTurnMeterTest runs the real kernel at Speed 3..13 in a 3v3 so slots 6-7 are BOOT-DEAD spares (a unit that dies in combat has long since pinned at FULL, so 'did not move' would be vacuous). REJECTED: U_CT (the collision); turn_charge (charge IS the cast-time word); turn_timer (every *_TIMER counts down); initiative; a CPU accumulator; wrapping in the shader (the host reads back at frame granularity and cannot catch the edge); a separate U_TURN_READY flag; GPU-side init at tick 0; a UNIT_CONFIG_SCHEMA row (it is live state the shader writes). CONSEQUENCE: UNIT_SIZE 101->102, SHADER_VERSION 30->31, live-state count 57 not 56. RECORDED, NOT SETTLED: the tick-to-turn RATE. atb_speed carries FFT Speed, so a Speed-8 unit is ready every 13 ticks -- under half a second at 30 ticks/sec; SS3's lever is the between-turn playback rate and the call needs #891/#892 to exist. |
| [0237](0237-the-rollout-budget-is-bounded-by-the-horizon-and-the-unit-count-not-the-fleet.md) The rollout budget is bounded by the horizon and the unit count, not by the fleet | `engine` | · | · | 6 | `0001`, `0068`, `0235`, `0236` |
| | | | | | GambitBattle rollout budget (#890). THE ROLLOUT BUDGET IS BOUNDED BY THE HORIZON AND THE UNIT COUNT, NOT BY THE FLEET. The cost side of SS7's K x M x H and SS8's accuracy-vs-cost table, measured by tests/GPURolloutBudgetBench.tscn (a [NOT_A_TEST] rig) on an RTX 5090 over battles in {1,4,16,64,256,1024} x units_per_battle in {8,16,32}. It INVERTS the framing SS7 was written in. dec. 1: the fleet is nearly free -- 1024 battles cost 1.41x one battle at U=8, because _run_tick dispatches over EVERY battle in one compute list and a tick is 8 dispatches separated by 7 full memory barriers, so 8 threads take 113us and 8192 take 137us; saturation begins above ~2000 allocated slots (U=32,N=1024 is 2.3x N=1). dec. 2: units_per_battle is the EXPENSIVE axis -- U 8/16/32 costs 27.6/45.3/109.1 ms at fixed fleet (4x the units for 3.9x the time, against 1.4x for a 1024x FLEET), because four of the eight passes are one thread per battle looping over all U units, so U buys serial work where N buys parallel threads. dec. 3: H is the only lever with a SUPERLINEAR price -- timed in 8 chunks over H=800 the per-100-tick cost climbs 29.6/24.3/19.4/21.3/29.0/70.1/112.9/134.9 ms as units engage and die, so 400->800 costs 4.3x not 2x. dec. 4: SS7's degradation ladder (drop M, then K, never H) has NOTHING TO RECOVER -- halving M at the Gariland shape saves ~10 ms of a 65 ms beat and half of that is the score leg dec. 7 deletes; the ladder is kept for its reasoning, not its rungs, and the cap must be set so degradation is never the plan. dec. 5: the measured budget at the shipping shape -- Gariland's ENTD 388 holds 6 of 16 slots (ENTD records are always 16 wide), so padded U lands near 12, and K=64 x M=4 x H=300 is a 64.7 ms beat; H=300 is 5 s of simulated battle because CombatLoop ticks at 60/s. dec. 6: a rollout FORKED FROM A MID-BATTLE POSITION costs ~2x a fresh one (51.3 -> 105.1 ms of run at prefork=600), which is the real SS7 case, so every fresh-fork row is a FLOOR. dec. 7: scoring reads the RESULT buffer in BULK -- read_unit_column is a blocking readback per battle (33-51 ms for ONE column over 1024) while the whole result buffer is 0.11 ms, and stage_victory writes result/tick/per-team living HP EVERY tick, so the value function's features belong in that record and a scorer looping read_unit_column (or get_battle_result, which re-reads the whole buffer per call) is the shape to reject. dec. 8: NO SIZE CAP IS HARDCODED and no tunable is published -- CombatLoop.gd:312 still initialises ONE battle, so the multi-battle path has no production caller and a tunable now would ship a default with no reader; #897 owns it. dec. 9: MAX_UNITS_PER_BATTLE=8 stays vestigial and did not bound the sweep; the structural ceiling is the ENTD's 16 slots. dec. 10: the empty-fleet trap is real but only 22% (30.5 vs 39.1 ms at N=1024), and the FILL LEG ERASES IT -- restore_battle installs battle 0's header, result word and all, so the arm's first version measured nothing and the rig's own red warning was falsified by its own arm. dec. 11: roster composition is not a budget lever -- a CAST roster (witnessed by nonzero cooldown_ready_at) costs the same or less than ATTACK, because the horizon's work is movement and target search, not which action fires. CONSEQUENCE: cleanup() now frees _effect_timings_buffer, the one buffer _create_buffers made and cleanup never gave back (one leaked StorageBuffer per teardown, once per battle in a campaign). |
| [0239](0239-the-turn-director-gates-the-pump-it-does-not-own-and-stops-on-the-exact-tick.md) The turn director gates the pump it does not own, and stops on the exact tick | `engine` | · | · | 8 | `0018`, `0037`, `0065`, `0068`, `0083`, `0235`, `0236` |
| | | | | | GambitBattle turn director (#891). THE TURN DIRECTOR GATES THE PUMP IT DOES NOT OWN, AND STOPS ON THE EXACT TICK. SS1's component node, built: mounted on a CombatLoop, not a host and not CombatHost. The design left WHO PUMPS and HOW PRECISE THE STOP IS unsaid, and the corpus had already ruled on the first. dec. 1: the director GATES (combat_active) and adds no pump and no second freeze authority -- CombatLoop.tick() is already the one pump and both hosts merely CALL it, and ADR-0037 dec. 2 is 'One authority, no mirror'; the freeze is applied SYNCHRONOUSLY from inside the gate call and never from _process, because a child processes after its parent and node order is not a correctness argument. dec. 2: the stop is EXACT-TICK via an optional CombatLoop.turn_gate predicate consulted LAST in each tick-loop iteration -- a frame drains ~16 ticks at 15 fps and a Speed-8 unit is ready every 13, so a frame-granular stop overruns a WHOLE TURN and SS3's 'the world never moves except between decision points' is false at frame granularity; it breaks rather than returns, so the frame's per-frame work still runs and the accumulator remainder survives the resume. dec. 3: the gate costs NO READBACK -- turn_meter and flags join _read_tick_columns's single version-cached read, the predicate is an allocation-free scan over two PackedInt32Arrays, and the full TurnQueue ordering is paid ONCE on the tick it stops. dec. 4: the dead-bit filter is LOAD-BEARING, not defensive -- the kernel stops advancing a dead unit's meter (ADR-0236 dec. 5), so a unit that dies at or above FULL keeps that meter forever and an unfiltered gate would trip every tick while ready_now returned nothing, freezing and resuming in a loop with no turn to open. dec. 5: the boundary is signals + explicit commit()/cancel(), and the director NEVER classifies sides -- NavigatorMain composes team0 as owned units UNION ENTD-blue, so a guest sits on team 0 and team-based classification is wrong at the first real roster; team rides in turn_opened only because TurnQueue already carries it. dec. 6: CANCEL UNDOES THE EDITS, NOT THE TURN -- the snapshot predates consume_turn so restore puts the UNSPENT meter back and the SAME turn re-opens; a cancel that spent the turn would make Escape cost you a turn. Committing with no edits IS waiting; there is no separate pass verb. dec. 7: ONE SNAPSHOT PER TAKER, not per freeze -- two units can hold turns inside one freeze and a freeze-scoped snapshot would let a cancel of the second silently revert the first's COMMITTED decision, ADR-0235's invisible divergence. dec. 8: a freeze DRAINS the ready set, RECOMPUTED after each commit and never cached, because a cached list is a claim about a world a reconfigure_unit just edited. dec. 9: DEPLOYMENT is a state of this machine and the ONE state with no snapshot -- SS9's 'a turn with the clock stopped and the CT gate removed' opens through the same signal with taker == -1, and holds nothing to undo because nothing is ticking and the units are not in the GPU buffer yet (boot_battle is what puts them there), so placements are CPU-side and land in one write at commit. dec. 10: playback_rate is a VIEWING rate and that is TESTABLE -- an ADR-0068 static var (dec. 13: never a const, frozen at parse time) forwarded into CombatLoop.playback_scale, and because the drain is in whole TICK_INTERVAL steps and the stop is exact-tick, a between-turn stretch is a FIXED NUMBER OF TICKS, so no rate can change an outcome; ADR-0065's principle holding in the combat context ADR-0065 left untouched. Landed in _resume as well as _process, because the rate picked during a turn is the next stretch's rate and a hand-driven harness renders no frame. dec. 11: the name is TurnDirector, NOT GambitTurnDirector -- it directs turns, the gambit-specific parts mount ON it, and SS11 has NavigatorMain mounting the same object. dec. 12: the test mounts on a bare CombatLoop with no host scene AND NO UNIT NODES -- _check_state_changes and _read_tick_columns both clamp to units.size(), so zero units skips the whole CPU apply pump and leaves the sim, the pump and the gate; empty gambits make the meter the only field that moves. The cancel arm asserts the edit LANDED before cancelling it, or it would compare a buffer against itself. REJECTED: the director claiming the pump (a third thing able to advance COMBAT-owned bodies, the condition ADR-0083 dec. 1 expressed away); Engine.time_scale (ADR-0037 dec. 1 rules combat pause combat-scoped, and NavigatorGarilandVictoryTest already owns the knob); a frame-granular stop; a per-side strategy object (layerable later, but it would force the bare-mount test to build a double); a new word for taker. NOT RE-OPENED: ADR-0083's set-aside single clock authority. Its premise is 'a two-pump system' and this decision PRESERVES the count, so the set-aside stands unamended; folding CombatHost/GPUArena/NavigatorMain/ScenarioVM into one authority would make #891 a pump refactor. Recorded as fog on map #886. CONSEQUENCE: CombatLoop grows three things (playback_scale, turn_gate, two more lean columns on a read it already performs); neither host changes and with no director mounted behaviour is unchanged. The gate is a general per-tick stop hook, so Active mode is a policy change inside the predicate rather than a rewrite. STILL OPEN: the tick-to-turn RATE -- at 60 ticks/sec a Speed-8 unit is ready every 0.22 s, which no playback rate makes readable; needs #892 to exist. |
| [0242](0242-the-gambit-host-boots-from-one-integer-and-the-gpu-battle-does-not-exist-until-commit.md) The gambit host boots from one integer, and the GPU battle does not exist until commit | `engine` | · | · | 10 | `0018`, `0042`, `0048`, `0137`, `0180`, `0219`, `0235`, `0239` |
| | | | | | GambitBattle host scene + deployment (#892). THE GAMBIT HOST BOOTS FROM ONE INTEGER, AND THE GPU BATTLE DOES NOT EXIST UNTIL COMMIT. SS10's scenario_id boot and SS9's deployment, built as a THIN third CombatHost subclass beside GPUArena. The design left three things to the build: WHEN the GPU battle comes into existence, WHO the player may command (ADR-0239 dec. 5 ruled the director must never say), and whether the deployment DECISION is new state even though the SCREEN is not new UI. dec. 1: GambitBattle is a third CombatHost subclass, thin, beside GPUArena and not a mode flag on it -- three subclass tests read arena state and a second rules model behind a flag forks all of them; CombatHost carries accessor mechanics and no policy (ADR-0018) so the third subclass costs nothing there and buys the pump, and this host adds none of its own. dec. 2: THE GPU BATTLE DOES NOT EXIST DURING DEPLOYMENT and the simulator is sized by the SQUAD -- GPUArena boots its sim before placement because its march runs ON it (ADR-0042); here the zone's max_squad_size is smaller than the roster (Gariland 5 of 7), so the cast that boots is not the cast that fights, and sizing at boot would either allocate for units that never take the field or force the assignment to be final before it is made. combat_loop.gpu_simulator is NULL for the whole of deployment, the mechanical form of ADR-0239 dec. 9, so CPU-side placement is by CONSTRUCTION and not by discipline; the test asserts the null directly and a seeded early setup_gpu_simulator reds exactly that one assertion. dec. 3: the deployment DECISION is a pure editable model, DeploymentAssignment, and clear() is its only undo -- it holds no snapshot and offers no cancel, because TurnDirector.cancel refuses DEPLOYMENT and there is nothing to restore. Scene-free, so the rules are testable under exactly the condition deployment runs under: no map, no GPU. dec. 4: DeploymentPlan is ONE assignment computed, DeploymentAssignment is the assignment the player EDITS; auto_fill delegates to the plan so the debug path and the arena's no-strategy path cannot drift. dec. 5: the ATTACK.OUT flags bit 0 mandatory rule is honoured BY CONSTRUCTION -- auto_fill hoists mandatory units to the front rather than trusting DeploymentPlan's 'Ramza first' claim about what owned_units() happens to return; the test puts the mandatory unit LAST in roster order, which is the arm that distinguishes the two, and a seeded drop of the hoist reds 3 assertions. dec. 6: the cap bounds the SQUAD, not the EDITS -- a sixth body is refused, moving an already-placed unit is always legal; the cheap version forbids dragging a unit around a zone you have filled. dec. 7: the ROM places everyone except your squad and places them EXACTLY -- the ATTACK.OUT zone table is player start tiles only, so enemies and ENTD-blue guests stand on authored ENTD coords including upper_level (ADR-0219), placed before the player is asked anything, and no ROM-placed unit stands inside the player's zone. dec. 8: THE DEPLOYMENT ASSIGNMENT IS WHAT SAYS WHICH UNITS YOU MAY STEER -- ADR-0239 dec. 5 refuses side classification because NavigatorMain puts guests on team0; the host answers with the assignment itself, so the units you deployed are the units you command, which is the ROM's own rule at Gariland (Delita is ENTD-blue on team0, fights beside you, takes no orders). A team-based test hands him to the player; an is_player_controlled test is a second source of truth for a question the player already answered. dec. 9: the scenario cast is ONE SEAM, ScenarioCast, MOVED out of GPUArena rather than copied -- ADR-0180 retired the rosters so the arena would stop being a separate combat universe and a copying second host rebuilds it; the same argument folds _build_encoded_gambits into GambitEncoder.encode_for_units, already hand-mirrored once on NavigatorMain and about to be a third time. The navigator's per-unit spawn TAIL is deliberately NOT folded (special_name, HP across a walk, SCENARIO clock) -- the disagreement UnitSpawn's docstring warns against swallowing. dec. 10: NO NEW VERBS -- Enter commits the open turn (commit with no edits IS wait, ADR-0239 dec. 6), Backspace cancels, Space commits the deployment; the ADR-0137 Amendment 4 mapping GPUArena already uses, with the PHASE supplying what acting means. REJECTED: a mode flag on GPUArena; booting the simulator at _ready and merely hiding the bench (units_per_battle becomes a function of the roster, and the CPU-side guarantee becomes a convention); a DeploymentScreen class (that class IS the second UI SS9 forbids); a snapshot for deployment so cancel looks symmetric; auto_fill in roster order; classifying commandability by team or by is_player_controlled; copying the cast composition. MEASURED: GambitBattleTest 55/55 and DeploymentAssignmentTest 47/47, both green, with FOUR seeded defects each reddening only their own arms. THE TICK-TO-TURN RATE, which is what #892 existed to make feelable: 244 turns over ~1,650 ticks at Gariland = A TURN EVERY ~0.11 s, and map #886's fog note (13 ticks / 0.22 s) is the PER-UNIT period -- what the player sits through is that divided by the number of living units, so it gets FASTER as the roster grows and the playback rate alone cannot be the lever, since it stretches the gaps and cannot reduce how many stops there are. The whole battle runs in 12.6 s wall clock. OBSERVED, NOT FIXED: the scenario-booted cast has EMPTY gambit lists (UnitSpawn mints a GambitList and nothing in the boot path authors into it), so all eleven Gariland units run on ADR-0048's injected safety net -- not new here, the arena's cast is the same cast, but newly visible because #894 opens its editor onto an empty list. CONSEQUENCE: GPUArena loses a cast region and gains two call sites; NavigatorMain loses its gambit-encode mirror; ProceduralMapMountTest 114 -> 116 and CombatCameraMountTest 112 -> 114 for the host scene and its rig. dec. 7 (added after the first build, settled by the user in a grilling session 2026-09-06): A MANDATORY UNIT IS NEVER PLACED FOR YOU AND THE FIELD HOLDS A SLOT OPEN FOR IT -- the effective cap for a non-mandatory unit is max_squad_size less the mandatory units NOT YET PLACED, so Gariland is 4 until Ramza is down and 5 after, and a mandatory unit is never held back by its own slot. The argument is where the refusal LANDS: without it a player fills all five tiles with cadets, presses START and is told 'Ramza must be deployed' over a full zone with no indication of whom to bench -- legal all the way to the commit and then refused at it. problems() stays as the backstop; the placement that would strand him is refused when it is MADE. REJECTED with it: commit-only enforcement (not wrong, refused at the wrong moment) and AUTO-PLACING him (where Ramza stands is a real decision the screen must not make for you). auto_fill is unaffected -- its hoist discharges the reserve on the first placement. Witnesses: DeploymentAssignmentTest 73/73 (arm 2b, and dropping the reserve reds 10 assertions), GambitBattleTest 63/63 (arm 3's reserve half runs on the REAL Gariland cast, not stand-ins). |
| [0245](0245-a-turn-nobody-can-take-is-spent-where-it-opens.md) A turn nobody can take is spent where it opens | `engine` | · | · | 0 | `0037`, `0082`, `0083`, `0236`, `0239`, `0242`, `0244` |
| | | | | | A TURN NOBODY CAN TAKE IS SPENT WHERE IT OPENS (#898, design S11). S11 is a CHECK, not a feature: ADR-0239 put the director on the CombatLoop and not on CombatHost because NavigatorMain extends no host, and this is where that either costs one line or is exposed. It costs two -- TurnDirector.mount(_combat_loop) and TurnQueueHud.mount(camera, director) -- with no host change, which also discharges ADR-0244's soft spot ('the HUD rides the camera, not the host' was an argument and not a demonstration). THE HARD HALF IS THAT THE WALK'S BATTLE IS PLAYED BY NOBODY: it runs itself to annihilation while the walk waits on CombatLoop.victory, and there is no adjustment UI (#894), no rollout driver (#897) and no player, so a director that froze would hand the first crossing to no one and the walk would stop there forever. dec. 1: NavigatorMain mounts the same director on its bare loop, in _build_frozen_combat_loop right after boot_battle creates the simulator it reaches through; the director is a child of the LOOP, which is the property the placement was chosen for. dec. 2: THE STOP IS A POLICY ON THE DIRECTOR -- `stops_the_world`, WAIT by default -- and the walk mounts the non-stopping one: the turn is announced and spent where it opens, the gate returns false, the drain carries on through the same tick, the turn costs ZERO ticks. Design S3 already named the shape ('Active mode is the same machine that simply does not stop'); this is its smallest form and claims nothing else -- no shot clock, no player who may interject. REJECTED: an auto-commit driver in the host (GambitBattle's call_deferred('_pass_turn') shape) -- turn_gate returning true BREAKS the frame's tick loop, so a deferred commit caps the sim at ONE TURN PER FRAME and the walk's Gariland battle spends 225 turns; committing synchronously inside the emit instead re-enters _open_turn through commit's own drain. REJECTED: mounting nothing (S11's check is never made and the strip's claim stays an argument). dec. 3: A TURN NOBODY HOLDS TAKES NO SNAPSHOT -- nothing can cancel a turn spent inside the call that announced it, so the pre-turn image is dead weight (four GPU slice readbacks x 225 turns) and cancel() correctly refuses. dec. 4: THE WALK'S BATTLE IS DIRECTED THOUGH NOBODY PLAYS IT, and consuming the turns is the point: with nothing calling consume_turn the kernel stops advancing a crossed meter and every living unit sits permanently ready, so TurnQueue.forecast degenerates to 'everyone, now' forever. The kernel writes the meter and never reads it (ADR-0236), so no outcome changes -- the walk's battle is the battle it was, with a clock now legible. dec. 5: NavigatorMain._combat_active KEEPS ITS SINGLE WRITER, the deliberate answer #891 asked for: the mirror is the PUMP gate (_process ticks only while true) and not the freeze authority, and under this policy the director never touches combat_active at all. #897 IS WHERE THAT CHANGES -- a driver that HOLDS a turn needs stops_the_world true, and then a freeze under a player-paused mirror resumes a world the player paused. dec. 6: the strip is freed with the battle (it is a child of the CAMERA, which outlives every battle in the walk); the director is not, being a child of the loop. FOUND: 'THE QUEUE MOVED' IS NOT EVIDENCE THAT A TURN WAS SPENT -- dropping the consume_turn call passed BOTH rigs (20 headful arms green, unit test green; the only trace was wall clock 3.0s -> 17.0s, unasserted), because a meter stops advancing at its crossing and each unit crosses carrying its OWN overshoot, so an unspent ready set keeps growing and each larger overshoot takes the head; deaths do the same at walk scale. The arm that catches it READS THE BUFFER (arm 6C: announced at meter 108, reads 108, expected 8). FOUND: A FREEZE ARM GATED ON combat_active CANNOT SEE A FREEZE -- a freezing director writes combat_active false, so the sample was skipped on exactly the frames that mattered and the seeded hang was reported by the mirror arm instead. FOUND: a freed Object compares EQUAL to null in GDScript, so a post-hoc `!= null` cannot tell 'never mounted' from 'mounted and torn down' -- the rig latches booleans at first sighting. MEASURED: NavigatorTurnDirectorMountTest (headful, real GPU, the real Gariland walk) 20 arms green, 225 turns over 11 units, the strip repainting on every turn, 3.0s against a 3.7s undirected baseline; TurnDirectorTest arm 6 drives the SAME fat frame under both policies and then reads the buffer; three seeded defects redden only their own arms (the walk that stops -> 8, the spend that never lands -> 6C, the strip left on the camera -> 2). GambitBattleTest, TurnQueueHudTest, TurnQueueTest, NavigatorGarilandVictoryTest green unchanged. SOFT SPOTS: the walk's battles are now turn-ordered and nothing looks at the order until #897; the forecast costs one GPU readback per turn and the coalescing fix is deliberately not built on a cost nobody has observed; and stops_the_world is a boolean where S3's Active mode is a larger thing. |
| [0246](0246-the-rollout-fleet-is-the-whole-batch-and-the-only-thing-the-cpu-prunes-is-what-cannot-change.md) The rollout fleet is the whole batch, and the only thing the CPU prunes is what cannot change | `engine` | · | · | 3 | `0023`, `0047`, `0048`, `0068`, `0235`, `0236`, `0237`, `0242` |
| | | | | | THE ROLLOUT FLEET IS THE WHOLE BATCH, AND THE ONLY THING THE CPU PRUNES IS WHAT CANNOT CHANGE (#895, design S7). ADR-0237 measured what a thinking beat COSTS; this is what it IS -- RolloutHarness (snapshot -> fill -> run -> bulk read -> restore), RolloutCandidates (the K, pure, no GPU) and RolloutPlaybook (authored postures). dec. 1: THE FLEET IS EVERY BATTLE SLOT, THE LIVE ONE INCLUDED, which is why the restore is load-bearing and not tidy -- step_tick encodes ONE compute list over the whole batch (ADR-0237 dec. 1) with no per-battle active mask, so a horizon carries the LIVE battle forward too; the live battle is candidate 0 / seed 0's substrate and restore_battle(0, pristine) puts it back, making the rollout ADR-0235's keystone's second consumer exactly as S7 predicted, and making a shader change unnecessary. CONSEQUENCE: after a beat slot 0 no longer SHOWS its candidate (its result record was read before the restore and is valid, the slot is not) -- asserted in both directions. dec. 2: A CANDIDATE IS AN ENCODED GAMBIT IMAGE, NOT A GambitList -- the mutation domain is the buffer (GAMBITS_PER_UNIT ints, the slice snapshot_battle hands back), because a Gambit->encoder->packer round trip inside a beat lets every candidate FAIL to encode (ADR-0023's skip) with nothing to do about it mid-beat, whereas an image edit is definitionally installable; authored postures are the exception and go through the ONE encoder, so an inexpressible posture is dropped with ADR-0023's own verdict. Two properties fall out and are guarded: no operator touches slot MAX_USER_GAMBITS (ADR-0048's terminal candidate) and CANDIDATE 0 IS THE UNMUTATED INCUMBENT (without a leave-it-alone arm the AI must change its gambits every turn, re-planning a working posture the moment every mutation scores below it with nothing carrying its score). dec. 3: THE LEGALITY PREFILTER IS STATIC-ONLY -- S7 says it reuses spell_pre_validate / cooldown_pre_validate, which live in stage_compute.glsl and read CURRENT MP and a live cooldown clock, so a CPU copy would be a second implementation of a runtime rule and the two would disagree (#894's own objection to a cleverer imperative predicate); the CPU prunes only an ability the unit lacks and one whose mp_cost exceeds its MAX MP, both read from the SAME table the shader indexes, i.e. a ceiling test on the rule's inputs and not a copy of the rule. An illegal candidate is not waste: it falls through in-shader and scores like the plan it degrades to. dec. 4: CRN SEEDS ARE SPACED BY THE RNG'S OWN INPUT WIDTH -- rand_int hashes battle_seed + unit_id*1000 + tick, so a battle at U units over H ticks draws on [S, S+(U-1)*1000+H-1] and two seeds are provably disjoint at a separation of (U-1)*1000+H; aliasing is not a crash but the variance reduction turning into shared error. The M seeds ARE reused across all K candidates and that aliasing is the point. dec. 5: THE PLAYBOOK IS KEYED BY UnitRole, NOT BY JOB ID -- S7 says per-job, a per-job table is ~130 rows of invention with no data behind them, and JobDatabase.get_job_role is an existing tested job->archetype map covering every job via its HYBRID default; a job-keyed override in front of it later changes no caller. dec. 6: SCORING IS ONE BULK READ (read_all_results, raw ints) and this harness does NOT score -- ADR-0237 dec. 7 measured read_unit_column at 33-51 ms for one column across 1024 battles against 0.11 ms for the whole result buffer, and a harness that ranked would make #896's H-sweep unable to vary the scorer. dec. 7: IMPERATIVE-ISSUE CANDIDATES ARE OUT, with a reason -- a lock-on must name ONE unit and no encodable target does (TARGET_THEM is the unit the CONDITION matched, SPECIFIC_UNITS is in ADR-0023's UNSUPPORTED set); #894 owns the charge economy and the encoding it needs, and the sixth family lands then. dec. 8: NO FLEET BY DEFAULT AND NO SIZE TUNABLE -- CombatLoop.rollout_fleet_size defaults to 0 meaning one battle, ADR-0237 dec. 8's reasoning, #897 publishes the ADR-0068 static var. REJECTED: a widened 1+K simulator with a per-battle active mask (S7 names it for Active mode; it buys nothing while the turn is frozen and costs a shader change plus a mask every stage must honour); a CPU mirror of the shader's pre-validates (prunes more, every prune on an invisible disagreement); compacting the gambit list on a delete (the shader SKIPS a disabled slot, so a compacting delete is a delete AND a reorder presented as S7's one-step operator); taking the first K in family order (spends every slot on swap and reaches no posture at all -- the families are interleaved so a small K drops each family's tail, S7's own bias-is-worse-than-variance reasoning). FOUND: A CANDIDATE FAMILY CAN BE ENUMERATED AND NEVER OFFERED, AND THE OBVIOUS TEST CANNOT SEE IT -- families() enumerates and FAMILIES interleaves, two lists; deleting 'playbook' from FAMILIES makes that family unreachable through generate() while families() still produces it, and the first version of the test walked FAMILIES to check coverage, SO THE SEED DELETED ITS OWN CHECK and the run was green. The enumeration's keys are now the oracle in both directions and the interleave arm demands EVERY family at K = 1 + family count. FOUND: the transposed-fill seed (slot m*K+k for k*M+m) left the bit-identity arm green (the restore still ran) AND the CRN-identity arm green (a 2x2 fleet transposes symmetrically); only the arm that reads each slot's header seed and gambit image back caught it. MEASURED: RolloutCandidatesTest, RolloutCrnSeedsTest and GPURolloutHarnessTest green; a beat on an 8-battle x 4-unit fleet at H=60 costs fill 0.06 ms, run 3.40 ms, read 0.07 ms, restore 0.02 ms. Seven seeded defects, six reddening only their own arms on the first try (missing restore -> 5 arms, transposed fill slot -> 10, off-by-one separation -> 6 including the ground-truth intersection arm, no MP ceiling -> the prefilter arm plus ~15 candidate arms, no incumbent -> 1, editable safety net -> many) and the seventh (dropped family) exposing the test gap above. SOFT SPOTS: the playbook's postures are plausible and encodable but NOT calibrated -- nothing can calibrate them until #896 has a value function; there is no production caller, so 'the beat is invisible to the battle it forks from' is proved on a rig and not yet in a game; and the CRN detector is conservative by construction (below the separation two windows OVERLAP, and whether an overlap yields a shared draw depends on H against the 1000-wide unit stride). |
| [0247](0247-the-deployment-picker-is-the-roster-grid-over-the-map-and-the-pick-is-a-latch.md) The deployment picker is the roster grid over the map, and the pick is a latch | `engine` | · | · | 5 | `0084`, `0088`, `0137`, `0164`, `0196`, `0206`, `0219`, `0221`, `0239`, `0242` |
| | | | | | THE DEPLOYMENT PICKER IS THE ROSTER GRID OVER THE MAP, AND THE PICK IS A LATCH. ADR-0242 built the deployment MODEL and left the half that decides WHO fights as benched()[0]; at Gariland the cap is 5 against a 7-unit roster, and #940's own note recorded that benched()[0] agreed with the mandatory-unit reserve BY ACCIDENT OF ROSTER ORDER. The ticket's own two gaps: the map-hosted Formation screen has NO roster grid and answers 'who is selected' FROM THE TILE CURSOR, and a benched unit stands on no tile; and 'the pick LATCHES' is a statement about what the MAP shows, not about what the host remembers. THE FIRST BUILD GOT THE PICKER WRONG AND THE USER CORRECTED IT IN REVIEW -- it walked the bench ONE UNIT AT A TIME on the map host's Status panels, reading the ticket's tension as 'that screen has no grid, so the picker cannot be one'; the user's answer is that the Formation screen SHOWS YOU ALL YOUR UNITS, which is what makes it a picker rather than a scroll. dec. 1: THE PICKER IS THE ROSTER GRID, raised over the battlefield for the length of a pick -- owns_roster_grid() gated only the BUILD, so build_roster_grid()/free_roster_grid() make it something the host raises and drops (the same three calls _ready makes, reversibly), and while it is up the grid answers 'who is selected' (selected_character defers to FormationScene's answer-by-cell) and takes the roster's own d-pad routing that this host otherwise suppresses. dec. 2: it is the MAP HOST WITH ITS GRID SWITCHED ON, not the ROSTER host mounted over a battlefield, and paints_own_backdrop() is why -- the roster host paints a cobble floor and pillarbox bars that would cover the very thing you are deploying onto, and the map host answers false through the whole pick. THE FIRST BUILD'S REJECTION OF THIS IS WITHDRAWN: 'it would put a second selection UI over a map the cursor is already driving' is false, because the pick takes the camera (begin_hold) and that freezes the cursor -- the real obstacle was ONE PREDICATE and the screen already had a switch for it. dec. 3: THE PICK OPENS THE MAIN MENU, NOT THE ACTION MENU, so the host-rows check is stated on BOTH dispatches -- a plain grid has no detail screen under it and open_action_menu() refuses without one, so the door is open_main_menu(); FormationDetailTransition.action_rows swaps the rows on either door and routes every choice out through action_row_chosen, because both dispatches are keyed by INDEX and a check on only the detail path would have sent row 0 into enter(State.EQUIP). The IDLE guard that refuses a MAIN menu on the map host stays, narrowed by one clause: its reason was 'a map has no plain roster', and during a pick it has one. dec. 4: ROWS_DEPLOY is ONE row and the first row set in this port with no ROM behind it -- FFT's deployment screen has no action menu, and the user's ask was to keep the RE faithful AND roll our own rather than choose; rows has been a host-swapped var since §15.23 so ROWS stays byte-untouched. One row because the screen already shows one unit and the tile was chosen before it came up. LOC_DEPLOY's rect is DERIVED from the three proven homes (each is rows*16+16 tall, each one's frame art ends at display x244) and is LABELLED derived -- an invented oracle citation is worse than none. dec. 5: THE PICK IS A LATCH ON A TILE, NOT A CARRY OF A UNIT -- O on an occupied tile lights CellMarking.Kind.SELECTED (whose own docstring already said 'Picked and LATCHED, the cursor has moved on') and frees the real cursor, so two tiles are lit and the player sees both ends of the move; keyed by TILE because move/swap/refuse are all questions about tiles and unit_at is one call away. The _carried variable it replaces was INVISIBLE: the state lived in the host and the map showed nothing. dec. 6: a misfire OUTSIDE the zone refuses and STAYS LATCHED -- the second press is the one that spends the selection, so a bad aim must cost nothing; the only refusal in these verbs that does not reset state. dec. 7: the deployable tiles stay GREEN -- PLACEMENT_PLAYER renders green and BLUE is PLACEMENT_ENEMY, i.e. 'you may NOT place here'; re-colouring touches a TileOverlayConfig GPUArena shares. dec. 8: X IS A CURSOR INTENT, CursorRig.cursor_cancelled, not a host keybind -- ACT and LOOK were signals while BACK was a ui_cancel test in GambitBattle._unhandled_input, two thirds of one intent published and the rest re-derived per host, which is the road unit_inspect went down (three host actions on one key because no port owned the intent). It rides the built-in ui_cancel name (Backspace here) so the battlefield does not back out on a different key from every screen. dec. 9: FACING IS A PURE FUNCTION OF THE ASSIGNMENT, recomputed for every deployed unit on every _show_assignment from the nearest authored enemy ENTD tile by column distance -- covers both units in a swap for free and cannot drift out of step with the layout. No rotate control in this ticket. dec. 10: A REFUSAL IS AUDIBLE -- _report_refusal printed, which is not feedback; the system bank already carries invalid (5) / unit_removal (9) / set (10) plus confirm_selection / cancel_selection for the latch, so this adds cues and not a cue system. dec. 11: THE PICKER OFFERS WHAT THE TILE WOULD ACTUALLY TAKE -- the bench is filtered by effective_cap, so with four of five Gariland tiles held by cadets the list is exactly one name long; offering a unit the confirm would then refuse is the 'legal all the way to the commit and then refused at it' shape ADR-0242 dec. 7's reserve exists to end, and a picker is where it would have come back. dec. 12: GambitBattle mounts the map-hosted Formation screen ONCE and can_open is the exact NEGATION of _cursor_confirm_is_mine -- the screen and the host both listen to cursor_confirmed, and one predicate read by both sides makes that a DISPATCH rather than a race. REJECTED: walking the bench one unit at a time on the Status panels (the first build -- it is a SCROLL, not a picker); mounting the ROSTER host over the battlefield (it paints its own backdrop); a DeploymentPickerHost subclass (inherits a whole screen to re-answer one question, and the grid it wants is a lazy build not a class); painting occupied zone tiles PLACEMENT_UNAVAILABLE ('Already claimed' -- the ticket notes the kind exists, but an occupied zone tile is a fully LEGAL second press, it SWAPS, and the kind shares a slot with PLACEMENT_PLAYER so it would replace the green rather than layer over it); enabling the 4x2 roster grid on the map host (owns_roster_grid is false because a battlefield is not a grid; it would put a second selection UI over the map the tile cursor also drives); cycling the bench with repeated O and no screen (refused by the user twice -- choosing who fights wants the unit's stats on screen); rebuilding the DetailScene per pick step (its three set_*_view calls are a REBIND, a teardown would replay the O-press transition on every left press); a second 'Cancel' row (X already cancels everywhere; a row that duplicates a button can disagree with it); latching by holding the UNIT (identical while nothing else moves, wrong the moment something does). CONSEQUENCE: CursorRig publishes FIVE signals now, not four -- its class docstring still says four about the MEASURED union that closed ADR-0164 dec. 4 criterion 1, and that measurement stays true of what it measured. WIDEST BLAST RADIUS: ui_cancel is now SWALLOWED BY TileCursor in every cursor-bearing scene, safe for its twins' reason (_input_allowed is false during a camera takeover, which is exactly when a screen wants X for itself) but the first thing to look at if a back-out stops working somewhere with a map in it; the action is exempt from check_addon_install arm 2 on the ui_ prefix, so the addon owes no new provide. GambitBattleTest arm 3 is rewritten around the latch and gains the facing and zone-paint arms, and now ends by restoring the canonical auto_fill layout because arms 5 and 6 play the battle the assignment produces. The picker's own rig, GambitDeploymentPickerTest, drives the arrows, TRIANGLE and O as PHYSICAL KEY EVENTS through the real InputMap -- the routing is where 'the key does nothing' lives, and setting selected_cell directly would skip the action lookup, the frozen cursor's swallow and the host's dispatch. A MISSING GRID IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY QUESTION EXCEPT ONE, which is why has_roster_grid() exists: selected_character() reads the injected roster by INDEX and _cell_has_unit falls through to grid-bounds nav when nothing is populated, so a host that never built a grid selects, navigates and reports a character exactly like one that did -- seeded that way the rig passed EVERY assertion but one, in a different arm. Six seeded defects on the picker (no grid, eligibility filter dropped, lands on the cursor tile, arrows not routed, main menu ignores host rows, grid never freed) each redden only their own arms. |
| [0252](0252-the-adjustment-turn-holds-the-undo-not-the-redo-and-steerability-is-not-ownership.md) The adjustment turn holds the undo, not the redo, and steerability is not ownership | `engine` | · | · | 3 | `0005`, `0037`, `0048`, `0068`, `0137`, `0180`, `0235`, `0239`, `0242`, `0245` |
| | | | | | THE ADJUSTMENT TURN HOLDS THE UNDO, NOT THE REDO, AND STEERABILITY IS NOT OWNERSHIP. SS4's editing window, built (#894). ADR-0239 built the freeze and ADR-0235 the two GPU primitives; neither could build the half only a HOST can see -- TurnDirector mounts on a bare CombatLoop with no roster, so its snapshot is the GPU's three buffers, while the formation screen's Equip/Ability/Change-Job flows write straight through to the durable Character (ADR-0005), which is on the CPU and in no SSBO. Before this, reconfigure_unit_from had ZERO production callers: a mid-battle equip or job change mutated the Character and the kernel never heard, and a cancel restored a GPU that had forgotten the edit while the Character still remembered it. dec. 1: THE PRE-TURN IMAGE HAS TWO HALVES and the host owns the second -- AdjustmentTurn is pure and scene-free (DeploymentAssignment's shape, ADR-0242 dec. 3), wired to the director's three signals; it never sees the director and the director gains NO API, resting on orderings the director already documents (turn_cancelled before the re-open, turn_committed before a drain whose ready list is recomputed rather than cached). dec. 2: EDITS ARE MADE EAGERLY ON THE CHARACTER AND LAND LATE ON THE GPU -- staging them means every edit flow writes into an overlay instead of the Character, i.e. the fork of the formation screen #894 forbids in as many words; holding the UNDO costs one to_dict() per turn and touches none of them. A constraint acting on the design, not a preference. dec. 3: STEERABILITY IS NOT OWNERSHIP and the words stay apart -- ownership is a property of the UNIT (ADR-0137, an enemy gets the same screen read-only), steerability of the MOMENT; selection_is_steerable() is the conjunction and _apply_ownership asks it. One flag for both is wrong for whichever case it was not written for. dec. 4: THE GATE IS ON THE EDIT, NEVER ON THE LOOK -- ADR-0137 Amendment 6 rules a LOOK is never refused, so the turn disables the action ROWS through the mechanism that already exists for an enemy; a non-taker's screen mid-turn is exactly an enemy's: complete, readable, inert. dec. 5: THREE PHASES, THREE ANSWERS -- DEPLOYMENT the whole owned squad (no GPU battle exists yet, ADR-0242 dec. 2, so an edit lands at commit by construction); TURN_OPEN only the taker (a wider rule makes your fastest unit a universal remote and every other CT bar decoration); RUNNING nobody (the turn IS the reconfiguration). dec. 6: THE COMMIT WRITES TWO BUFFERS because the state is in two -- reconfigure_unit_from for the unit block, set_unit_gambits for the gambit SSBO which is not in the unit block at all; either alone lands half an edit. dec. 7: the push is SKIPPED on an untouched turn as a COST gate and not a correctness one -- ADR-0235's no-op identity arm proves an unedited reconfigure is bit-exact, so unconditional would still be correct; skipped because enemy/guest turns are the common case and a battle spends hundreds. dec. 8: THE PRUNE DROPS ONLY STALE ABILITY ROWS AND RETURNS THEM -- ATTACK/MOVE/WAIT are job-independent verbs, the usable set is primary skillset UNION secondary (AbilityLoadout's ROM model), and it returns the dropped rows rather than a bool because SS4 requires the loss to be SHOWN: a list that looks armed and is not is worse than refusing the job change or leaving a no-op slot. dec. 9: the usable set is NOT filtered by learned_abilities -- a gambit naming an unlearned ability of a job you are still in is a slot the player is working toward; the question is 'can this JOB do it', not 'can it do it yet'. dec. 10: Character.restore_mutable_from restores IN PLACE, ALL THE WAY DOWN -- three holders point at the Character so from_dict's fresh instance leaves them looking at the state being undone, AND THE SAME RULE APPLIES ONE LEVEL DEEPER because a Unit binds unit_progression and gambit_list DIRECTLY at spawn and never re-reads them; fields are copied from the property list rather than a hand-written list, which is a restore that silently stops covering the newest field. REJECTED: a staged edit model (it is the fork); putting the CPU image in TurnDirector's snapshot (the director deliberately runs with no host, no roster and no Unit nodes, and the walk's non-stopping director would carry an undo for turns nobody holds); refusing TRIANGLE on a non-taker (refusing a LOOK, and it makes the mode's own inspector unreachable for the whole battle); extending can_open per-column (it gates O only, TRIANGLE is deliberately ungated, so it covers one door of two and not the one the design uses); pruning at the job-change moment (better feedback, but the Change-Job screen has no writeback yet so there is no edge to hook -- moves there when one exists); refusing the job change or silent no-op slots. MEASURED: AdjustmentTurnTest 51/51 with five seeded defects each reddening only their own arms. THE ARM THAT CHANGED THE BUILD: the unit test is STRUCTURALLY unable to see whether the host wires the class to anything -- a commit never called and a commit on an untouched turn look identical from inside it -- so GambitBattleTest gained arm 4b, which edits a real Character mid-battle and reads the answer back out of the GPU buffer (brave, because it extracts straight from the progression with no derived unit_stats in the way). IT CAUGHT A REAL DEFECT ON ITS FIRST RUN: the in-place restore preserved the Character and swapped its COMPOSED objects, the Unit kept the old ones, and a cancelled edit (brave=13) was pushed to the GPU by the NEXT commit where 77 was expected -- dec. 10's second half is that finding. Two further seeds on the host wiring (commit unwired, cancel unwired) redden only their own assertions. GambitBattleTest is 101 assertions and still ends in annihilation. NOT HERE: SS5's imperative gambits (one-shot lock-on, finite charges, watchdog, cancel refund) are a separate ticket -- independent of the turn's gambit edit by the design's own rule, and coupled to this file only in that the refund rides dec. 1's cancel. AND THE SURFACE THE TURN OPENS ONTO IS STILL EMPTY: UIGambitEditor3 has had no home since ADR-0137 Amendment 2 hid UICombatManager and GPUArena freed the arena's copy outright, so equipment, job and ability slots are editable through the adjustment turn TODAY and GAMBITS ARE NOT, because there is no screen for them. That is the follow-on, and where SS7's seeded per-job playbook has to land -- ADR-0242 already recorded that a scenario-booted cast has empty gambit lists, so the editor will open onto nothing until something authors into them. |
| [0253](0253-the-value-function-is-a-probability-fit-against-played-out-battles-and-the-horizon-is-priced-against-it.md) The value function is a probability fit against played-out battles, and the horizon is priced against it | `engine` | · | · | 4 | `0001`, `0068`, `0132`, `0237`, `0246` |
| | | | | | THE VALUE FUNCTION IS A PROBABILITY FIT AGAINST PLAYED-OUT BATTLES, AND THE HORIZON IS PRICED AGAINST IT (#896, design S8). ADR-0237 measured the cost axis and ADR-0246 built the machine; this is the ACCURACY axis and the joint (H, f) choice. dec. 1: a corpus battle is a GPU FLEET battle and a faithful game, not a model of one -- the shader IS the gambit AI (stage_compute evaluates the rows, CombatLoop only drives the clock), and CombatLoop's 60/s puts a thousand battles at a day against ADR-0237 dec. 1's 1.4x for 1024; a finished battle stops costing, so to-completion is far cheaper than ticks x battles suggests. 1,536 battles, 31,991 samples, 0 dropped. dec. 2: SAMPLING ON A FIXED TICK GRID makes ONE corpus serve the whole sweep -- any grid state is reachable as D+H from the grid state H earlier, so stride=100 covers H in {100,200,400,800} with no second run, and terminal_tick - tick is the stratifier exactly; only LIVE states are sampled because a decided battle's record stops being rewritten. dec. 3: THE FEATURES LIVE IN THE RESULT RECORD, 4 ints -> 14 -- ADR-0237 dec. 7's decision, first consumer; the record is write-only from the shader so a new field touches no combat rule, and it stops being THREE hand-written copies of one number (GD-only RESULT_SIZE=4 opposite a literal battle_id*4 in stage_victory.glsl plus RolloutHarness's own offsets) -- gen_gpu_layout.py now emits GPUCombatPacker.RESULT_SIZE and ResultField from the shader (ADR-0001). dec. 4: THE ENGAGEMENT TERM IS A CENTROID DISTANCE, and O(U^2) nearest-enemy was REJECTED ON A MEASUREMENT: 1.9x the run leg at U=16 fleet 256 (56.3 -> 105.0 ms) and 2.2x at U=32 (209.4 -> 455.2) against a ~7% repeat spread, in a pass the LIVE game pays every tick; the O(U) centroid reproduces ADR-0237's U=8 fleet-256 row to 0.2% (33.81 -> 33.89), which makes the residual real -- the widened record costs 15-24% of the run leg at U>=16 and ADR-0237's table is that much optimistic from here. dec. 5: EVERY FEATURE IS PER TEAM because f is read from a PERSPECTIVE -- a battle-wide scalar says how decided a battle is but never who is winning, and mirrored rows drive its coefficient to zero; the first engagement term was battle-wide and had to be split. Coefficients come out exactly antisymmetric, intercept 0, six free parameters not twelve, and f(s,team0)+f(s,team1)=1 is an identity the design rests on. dec. 6: S8'S FOUR FEATURE FAMILIES WERE NOT SUFFICIENT and only the STRATIFICATION could see it -- fit alone they scored 0.7210 in the early stratum against a base-rate 0.6931, WORSE THAN A COIN FLIP and confidently so, because a fraction normalises absolute power away (at full health a 262-HP and a 1690-HP team both read hp_frac=1.0, leaving only the standing count, so the model learned 'the bigger team wins'); adding HP-share and power-share pairs takes that stratum to 0.6495 and the held-out loss 0.3492 -> 0.3214. S8's own rule: the fix is A TERM, NOT A WEIGHT. dec. 7: EVERY HORIZON IS SCORED ON THE SAME HELD-OUT DECISION POINTS -- independent pairs change the population underneath the table (a long horizon only pairs a point far from the end), so losses differ for two reasons at once; under that error the sweep looked FLAT (0.3451 -> 0.3353 across a 16x cost rise), and on a common row set the horizon plainly buys accuracy (0.4234 at H=0 -> 0.3073 at H=800). H=0 is in the table as S8's reference end or it cannot say what H BUYS. Splits are by BATTLE never by ROW (one battle's samples share a label and a roster). dec. 8: THE KNEE IS A RULE ABOUT THE RATE, under a cap on the FORKED beat -- 'last horizon that improved at all' selects the largest H in any monotone table and picked H=800 off a step buying 0.0022 for 241 ms, 22x worse than the step before; the rule is the last step returning >=20% of the best step's log-loss-per-ms, and the cap uses ADR-0237 dec. 6's ~2x fork because the AI forks mid-battle. dec. 9: THE MEASURED ANSWER at U=12 fleet 256 with the widened record -- H 0/100/200/400/800 -> log-loss 0.4234/0.4092/0.3955/0.3688/0.3073 at beats -/18.9/34.7/69.1/310.5 ms (forked -/37.8/69.5/138.2/620.9); KNEE UNDER A 200 ms FORKED CAP IS H=400, and H=800 without the cap. dec. 10: THE HORIZON BUYS NOTHING IN THE EARLY GAME -- late 0.2832 -> 0.1392 and mid 0.4723 -> 0.3464 from H=0 to H=800, but early 0.6495/0.6561/0.6597/0.6652/0.6444; 800 ticks is a small fraction of a 3,500-tick battle. The endgame stratum is absent BY CONSTRUCTION (the common row set needs a landed state at D+800). dec. 11: THE ARTIFACT SHIPS THE ROUNDED FIT and everything downstream reads those numbers -- a fixture built from the RAW fit cost 6.4e-7 of cross-language disagreement under a 1e-6 tolerance, passing while eating the budget the tolerance existed to spend on real errors; rounding once gives 4.95e-10 and a 1e-8 tolerance. dec. 12: NO FLEET SIZE AND NO HORIZON TUNABLE published here -- ADR-0237 dec. 8 and ADR-0246 dec. 8 hold, #897 publishes the ADR-0068 static var and knee_horizon is an INPUT to that. REJECTED: a tournament of weight vectors (scores the whole AI, cannot separate f from H from the mutation set); a hand-weighted score with a terminal win/loss bonus (no principled value exists -- too small and the AI trades the win for damage, too large and every non-terminal difference is noise; a probability needs no bonus); nearest-living-enemy distance (dec. 4); a HEAL in the corpus roster -- ability 8 is REGEN and make_spell_gambit targets the nearest ENEMY, so the roster regenerated the people it was fighting, 19% of battles never reached a verdict and 48% of samples were dropped, and that drop rate looked EXACTLY like a tick cap set too low; scoring a draw as 0.5 (a third outcome smuggled into a binary fit -- 174 of 63,982 rows dropped and counted); returning 0.5 when the scorer cannot score (every value in [0,1] is a legitimate probability, so an error returning one ranks a broken candidate at the median -- score returns -1.0). FOUND: a LATENT NON-IDEMPOTENCE in gen_gpu_layout.py with no symptom until a second enum reused a member name -- preserved comments were keyed by BARE MEMBER NAME so BattleHeaderField.RESULT and the new ResultField.RESULT collided, the bled comment was scraped back next pass, and --check reported STALE immediately after a successful regeneration (reads as 'somebody forgot to run the generator', not 'the generator cannot finish'); keys are now scoped by enum and the test asserts IDEMPOTENCE against the real tree, because a comment assertion passes on pass one and leaves the loop open. FOUND: the CROSS-LANGUAGE PIN is the only thing that can see the defect it exists for -- RolloutValueFunction.feature_vector and fit_value_function.py's features_from are ONE FUNCTION WRITTEN TWICE and a divergence fails invisibly (every score stays a probability, every candidate still ranks, nothing goes red, the AI just plays worse than its calibration claims), so the fixture carries input rows and expected SCORES rather than feature vectors. MEASURED: 8 seeded defects all caught; the EIGHTH broke the mirror in BOTH languages and refit, so the cross-language pin AGREED (arm 1 green at 4.9e-10) and only the perspectives-sum-to-1 arm went red -- the case where both sides are wrong the same way, and why arm 2 is not a restatement of arm 1. CONSEQUENCE: assets/gambit/ is a new store named for its reader (ADR-0132); the corpus rig lives in tools/ not tests/ per #896's scope note but still costs ProceduralMapMountTest 119->120 and CombatCameraMountTest 116->117, which walk res:// and do not care which directory a scene sits in; ranking averages over the CRN seeds (what the seeds are FOR) and a tie falls to ADR-0246 dec. 2's incumbent. SOFT SPOTS: the corpus roster is RANDOMISED, not real -- no battle in it is an ENTD encounter, and S8's owed refit after the AI ships is the intended correction; the engagement coefficient is small (+0.0495 per tile) and its feature is the one cheapened for cost, so the sweep cannot tell a weak term from a well-priced one; the early stratum still barely beats a coin flip and H does not rescue it -- an opening may genuinely not predict its outcome, but dec. 6 showed one apparent floor there was a MISSING TERM; and there is still no production caller, so the scorer is proved on fixtures and a corpus, not in a game. |
| [0256](0256-the-thinking-beat-spends-its-cap-by-prediction-and-the-degradation-ladder-bottoms-out-in-a-refusal.md) The thinking beat spends its cap by prediction, and the degradation ladder bottoms out in a refusal | `engine` | · | · | 2 | `0068`, `0173`, `0235`, `0237`, `0239`, `0242`, `0246`, `0253` |
| | | | | | THE THINKING BEAT SPENDS ITS CAP BY PREDICTION, AND THE DEGRADATION LADDER BOTTOMS OUT IN A REFUSAL (#897, design S7). ADR-0246 built the machine, ADR-0237 measured it, ADR-0253 fitted the scorer; this joins them and owns the one question none of them could -- how big a beat is allowed to be. dec. 1: THE CAP IS SPENT BY PREDICTION AND THE STOPWATCH IS AN INSTRUMENT -- #897 asks for a hard wall-clock cap AND 'no wall-clock-dependent truncation that changes the answer between runs on the same position', and a beat that watched a clock would honour the first and destroy the second (on this box a sibling session's suite is running most of the time, so it is the normal case, not a race). plan(cap,U,fleet,K,M,H) is a PURE function; the beat times itself and WARNS, never re-plans. dec. 2: THE COST MODEL IS THE ADRs' OWN MEASURED ROWS, separable in three axes x ADR-0237 dec. 6's 2x fork -- horizon knots from ADR-0253 dec. 9 (linear between knots, because the knots carry the 4.5x per-tick climb), units linear in U (dec. 2: 4x the units for 3.9x the time, serial work inside a thread), fleet linear in LOG2(N) (dec. 1: 1024 battles for 1.41x, dispatch-bound). Separability is those ADRs' own claim, not a convenience; at the reference shape it reproduces dec. 9's forked column exactly. dec. 3: THE FLEET AXIS IS READ THROUGH A MONOTONE ENVELOPE -- the measured row at 4 battles is 0.02 ms BELOW the row at 1, repeat noise (~7%) on a quantity that cannot fall, and read literally it makes the ladder a HILL CLIMB where dropping M raises the predicted cost. dec. 4: THE LADDER IS S7's ORDER (M, then K, never H) and the model reproduces ADR-0237 dec. 4's finding independently -- halving M moves the prediction ~4 ms of 138, the WHOLE ladder recovers 25 of 138. Rungs are taken one at a time and each reports what it recovered, so a degraded line re-measures dec. 4 every time it fires.
