The capacity squeeze runs the same rungs, so the order is not a coincidence of which limit bit first. dec. 5: THE LADDER'S FLOOR IS A REFUSAL, and that is the decision #897 really owns -- it cannot shorten H (biases toward immediate damage; bias is worse than variance) and cannot shrink units_per_battle (the scenario fixes it), so a plan that does not fit refuses and the turn is spent unchanged, which is exactly 'wait' (ADR-0239). Running anyway makes the cap decoration; running at K=1 spends the whole horizon to compare the incumbent against nothing. The floor is K=2, the first K at which 'search' means anything, and a refusal is LOUD and names the two tunables that could move. dec. 6: THE CAP IS 250 ms AND IS PRICED AS A FROZEN FRAME, not as a cinematic -- the beat runs synchronously inside turn_opened so its cost is a hitch the player sees; 250 buys H=400 (138 ms predicted at U=12) with the ladder never firing at Gariland or at U=16. Deliberately NOT the budget a cinematic focus beat would justify: there is no focus beat in the tree, and when one lands the compute hides behind an animation and THAT is the argument for raising it. dec. 7: H=400 IS THE ARTIFACT'S KNEE AND THE DRIVER CHECKS THAT IT STILL IS -- a static var cannot read an asset at parse time so the number is written twice, and check_horizon_against_artifact compares them at mount; nothing else in the tree would notice a refit that moved the knee. dec. 8: FIVE TUNABLES LAND HERE because this is the first reader (ADR-0237 dec. 8 / ADR-0253 dec. 12 both deferred them) -- rollout.cap_ms 250.0, rollout.horizon 400, rollout.candidates 64, rollout.seeds 4, rollout.fleet_size 256, registered from RolloutDriver's own _static_init (ADR-0173) and PULL-READ (R5) once per beat rather than pushed, because the driver re-reads on its own and there is no Node to scope an on_update to. fleet_size is read ONCE at boot, so plan treats what the simulator ALLOCATED as the ceiling rather than the slug -- a beat that refused because a slider moved is dec. 1's wall-clock dependence in different clothes. dec. 9: CombatLoop.rollout_fleet_size KEEPS ITS ZERO DEFAULT and GambitBattle sizes the fleet -- 256 battles is ~8 MB at U=12, not the VRAM problem, but GPUArena and NavigatorMain never run a rollout and a default on the loop would charge them for a fleet nothing reads. dec. 10: THE PERSPECTIVE IS READ FROM THE PACKER'S OWN BYTE and the midpoint does NOT answer it -- CombatLoop documents team0 as [0, U/2) but set_battle_units seats the rosters CONTIGUOUSLY, so team1 begins at team0.size() and the two agree only when team0 fills exactly half; GambitBattle sizes U as 2*max(t0,t1), which holds that only while the player's side is larger, so a 5-deployed/6-enemy Gariland puts unit 5 on team 1 BELOW the midpoint. Scoring from the wrong side ranks every candidate backwards while every score stays a well-formed probability. A disagreement with the announced team refuses the beat. dec. 11: THE CRN BASE SEED IS A FUNCTION OF THE POSITION -- hash(battle seed, tick, taker), all three out of the snapshot the beat forks. Reproducible AND decorrelated across turns; a constant base hands every beat in a battle identical luck, a bias averaging over M cannot see because it is the same M. Kept inside int32, which crn_seeds' (U-1)*1000+H spacing makes a real constraint. dec. 12: THE WINNING EDIT LANDS THROUGH snapshot/restore (so ADR-0235's bit-identity test covers it) AND A HELD DECISION IS NOT APPLIED -- when candidate 0 wins (ADR-0246 dec. 2's incumbent, which a tie also falls to) nothing is written, so 'changed its mind' and 'held' stay distinguishable at the one place that could tell. REJECTED: a stopwatch cap (dec. 1); a latched degradation that remembers an overrun for later beats (deterministic given a history, but two runs of the same position from different histories would differ -- dec. 1's requirement, lost more subtly); running over cap at the ladder's floor (makes the cap decoration); K=1 at the floor (a beat that cannot produce a decision is a wasted frame, not a cheap decision); shortening H at the floor (S7 forbids it and cost cannot overrule the bias argument); deriving the team from the units_per_battle midpoint (dec. 10). MEASURED: RolloutDriverTest reproduces ADR-0253 dec. 9's forked column within 1% at every knot, and the ladder recovers 25 ms of 138 across its whole length. GPURolloutDriverTest: two beats on one position choose identically down to the full ranking. CONSEQUENCE: the AI REFUSES above about U=30 under a 250 ms cap (H=400 predicts 338 ms forked with the ladder fully spent) -- the cap being honest rather than the AI being broken, and RolloutDriverTest arm 5 reds the moment a change to cap_ms or horizon breaks the no-degradation property at U=12/16; GambitBattle gains ai_enabled so a test that turns the AI off has to write the claim down; the candidate context is built by the HOST and its ability set is learned UNION named-in-gambits, SORTED (an ENTD enemy need not carry a learned-ability table, and Dictionary.keys() is insertion order -- a property of how a Character was BUILT); ADR-0253's owed refit is now collectable because there is a production caller; ProceduralMapMountTest 120->121 and CombatCameraMountTest 117->118. SOFT SPOTS: the cost model is calibrated on ONE GPU, so on a slower card the cap is spent optimistically and only the beat's post-hoc warning says so -- deliberately not a feedback loop, so a mis-calibrated box hitches rather than degrades; the ladder's rungs have never been exercised in a game (U=12 and U=16 both fit with room); the K<2 refusal is scrub-only today; and one ply means the AI cannot see its own next turn -- the value function carries everything past tick H. MEASURED BY WIRING IT (2026-09-07, Gariland U=12 fleet 256 H=400): 330 beats at 37/54/68/87 ms min/median/p90/max against a 138 ms prediction and a 250 ms cap, NO rung taken and no refusal -- the model is ~2.5x conservative here and in the SAFE direction, because FORK_FACTOR is ADR-0237 dec. 6's LATE-battle fork and Gariland's turns open long before tick 600. The AI thinks and its choices are real: 86 re-plans against 244 holds, with margins that are not noise (0.088 -> 0.303 on one beat, 0.369 -> 0.934 on another). dec. 13: A WON BATTLE LATCHES FROM ANY STATE, NOT ONLY FROM IDLE, and wiring the AI is what found that -- stage_compute.glsl checked 'enemy team dead -> celebrate' at the BOTTOM of its state machine, in the IDLE fall-through, and a unit with a live 'attack the nearest enemy' gambit and no living enemy never reaches idle (the gambit re-evaluates, the unit re-enters WALKING toward a target that no longer resolves, timer restarting every tick). stage_victory reports a win only when EVERY survivor is CELEBRATING, so one stuck unit holds an already-decided battle open forever. The check moves above the state dispatch, and TWO details are load-bearing, both found by getting them wrong first: it must sit AFTER the per-tick clears (returning above U_PENDING_ACTION_TYPE = ACTION_NONE leaves last tick's ACTION_PATHFIND_MOVE standing for stage_pathfind to re-run, writing WALKING back over the CELEBRATING just set, every tick, invisibly), and it resets the proposed position (the reset above it deliberately skips the walking states); and DYING is EXEMPT (a unit playing out its death as the last enemy falls is still dying). NOT an AI defect -- any authored attack gambit reaches the same loop; Gariland ended before this only because a scenario-booted cast has EMPTY gambit lists (ADR-0242) so its survivors sat in IDLE. MEASURED: AI off, 282 turns to annihilation in 15 s; AI on BEFORE dec. 13, 1,734 turns to the 12,000-frame bound with alive=[2,0] and team1_hp=0 -- team 1 annihilated and the battle never LATCHED, one survivor CELEBRATING and the other state=WALKING target=<itself> timer=48->47 across 800 frames. With dec. 13, Gariland ends by annihilation WITH THE AI ON in 385 turns. The seeded-defect check restored the pre-fix shape exactly (celebrate back in the IDLE fall-through) and reddened GPURolloutDriverTest arm 5 with winner=-1 while nothing else moved. THE ARM ASSERTS THE LATCH, NOT THE PER-UNIT STATE, and that is not a weakening: once stage_victory declares a winner every stage opens with `if (result != RESULT_ONGOING) return;` (ADR-0237 dec. 10) INCLUDING the per-tick copy_unit_to_next, so the ping-pong halves stop being synchronised and get_battle_unit_states alternates between a frozen pre-victory half and the post-victory one -- a first cut asserted state == CELEBRATING and read WALKING/CELEBRATING/WALKING on successive ticks while the result already said TEAM_0_WINS. is_battle_finished is the same trap (the battle HEADER is in the ping-pong slice); get_battle_result reads the single-buffered results SSBO and is the stable answer. The SHAPE of what f asks for is still worth stating because it is what the owed refit is for: mp_frac_self +2.23, hp_frac_self +2.05, engage_self +0.0495 PER TILE OF DISTANCE, so over a 400-tick horizon it rewards spending no MP, taking no damage and standing off -- coefficients that CORRELATE with winning in a corpus where nobody was optimizing them, and that become INSTRUCTIONS the moment something maximizes them. Exactly the failure S8 predicted, i.e. ADR-0253 dec. 12's owed refit, which now has a production caller to produce its positions. GambitBattleTest plays the WHOLE Gariland fight with the AI ON as dec. 13's end-to-end guard, and its arm 4c reads beats_taken to prove the host actually calls the driver -- both unit tests are structurally unable to see that, because a decide never called and a decide that held leave the gambit buffer identical (#894's lesson). SOFT SPOTS: dec. 13 pays is_enemy_team_dead on every unit every tick in every state rather than only for IDLE units, in the pass the live game pays every tick, and that delta was not separately measured; ai_enabled defaults true and nothing sets it false, so its off-path has no user. |
| [0258](0258-the-march-is-retired-and-two-enums-lose-a-member-without-renumbering.md) The deployment march is retired, and two enums lose a member without renumbering | `engine` | · | · | 14 | `0042`, `0043`, `0119`, `0137`, `0166`, `0180`, `0192`, `0196`, `0242`, `0249`, `0251`, … |
| | | | | | THE DEPLOYMENT MARCH IS RETIRED, AND TWO ENUMS LOSE A MEMBER WITHOUT RENUMBERING (#942). The march -- auto-fill onto spawn tiles, then walk on the GPU mover to a procedurally-scattered 'contested' objective with no FFT equivalent -- was this game's FIRST answer to how a unit takes the field; #892 built the second and #941 made it playable, and the precondition was discharged by OBSERVATION (the user played GambitBattle headful and signed off), not by inference. dec. 1: retired OUTRIGHT, no debug path and no deprecation window -- a deletion cannot be proved by seeding a defect away from it, because the PR IS the seed, so a staged retirement would have left six tests exercising a system already declared dead. dec. 2: GPUARENA KEEPS ITS JOB AND IS NOT SCHEDULED TO DIE, stated positively because #942 exists over an IMPLIED death and leaving the arena's future unsaid would reproduce that defect one level up; it is run/main_scene, the --stress-units perf rig, and a substrate for three GPU subclass tests -- and it ALREADY placed FFT-accurately without the march (_place_units_at_defaults: zone + DeploymentPlan, ENTD slot tiles), so that branch simply became the only branch and NOTHING HAD TO BE BUILT. dec. 3: GambitBattle keeps sole ownership of the interactive deployment; the arena gets no picker (the screen fork ADR-0252 refused). dec. 4: CellMarking.Kind drops PLACEMENT_CONTESTED leaving a HOLE at 3, never a renumber -- and the hole is only safe because of the repair it forced: TileOverlayConfig.param_slug was Kind.keys()[type], the NAMES array indexed by the VALUE, correct only while the values are DENSE, so under a hole keys()[4] returns PLACEMENT_UNAVAILABLE while 4 is CURSOR_ACTIVE and every surviving tunable slug re-points at the wrong marking SILENTLY. Moved to Kind.find_key. A LATENT DEFECT THE DELETION MERELY EXPOSED. dec. 5: PlacementTileGenerator -> PlacementPolicy, 81 of 293 lines; the procedural board (flood-grown islands + Poisson contested sampling, ADR-0043's retained fallback) had the march as its only consumer, and what survives is ADR-0192 dec. 6's water rule. dec. 6: src/strategy/ does NOT lose its reason to exist -- the ticket's fourth question rested on a false premise, since #892 put DeploymentAssignment and DeploymentPlan there. dec. 7: CombatLoop.deploy_active dies and the tick gate is combat_active alone again; the whole ADR-0042 surface (set_deploy_move, grant_deploy_jump, restore_combat_jump, clear_unit_gambits, is_unit_idle_at, is_unit_idle) had StrategyPhaseManager as its ONLY caller, and GPUBatchSimulator.set_unit_jump went with it. THE BOOT/ARM SPLIT OUTLIVES ITS OWN AUTHOR: boot_battle + arm_combat_gambits stay two calls because NavigatorMain uses them separately. dec. 8: DebugOverlay.Category drops STRATEGY, hole at 6, values written out in ALL THREE copies -- CAUGHT MID-BUILD, NOT DESIGNED: deleting it from the implicitly-valued BaseDebugPanel copy ALONE renumbered ROSTER..STORY in that copy only, routing every panel below the hole to the wrong tab with nothing to say so; TAB_NAMES was a positional Array with the same coupling and is now a Dictionary. dec. 9: ADR-0166's holder count RE-MEASURED -- deleting PlacementTileSet deletes TWO of its holders (2 get_tile_ownership, 3 claimed_tiles), taking Battle's spellings of occupancy 4 -> 2, which is exactly ADR-0166 dec. 5's two-claims-two-arbiters shape reached by DELETION rather than collapse. dec. 10: the glossary retires the dead terms with NO tombstone (docs/context/ models what the game IS; the ADR is where history belongs) -- Deployment march and Placement tile deleted, Deployment zone and ENTD lose their march tails (spawn -> START tiles), Deployment plan COINED for DeploymentPlan which had no entry and is now the arena's whole placement. Also removes ONE OF THREE MEANINGS OF 'MARCH' in this tree (march-idle and ScenarioApply.march 0x80 remain, unrelated to it and to each other). MEASURED: 63 files, 433 insertions, 2,999 deletions. SIX tests booted through the march, not the THREE the ticket named -- four set use_strategy_phase explicitly, CursorConfirmEndToEndTest deliberately left the DEFAULT, StrategyPhaseTest drove the controller directly. REJECTED: retiring GPUArena entirely (moves run/main_scene, the smoke test and three perf scripts onto a host with no --stress-units lever); keeping the march as a debug path; preserving the walk-in as a visual (its cost is the whole CombatLoop deploy surface plus booting the simulator BEFORE placement, and GambitBattle deliberately does the opposite -- an arrival animation belongs on a CPU-side FormationPickIn gesture and a new ticket); keeping either enum member as reserved (code with no observable consequence); renumbering either hole; A RATCHET GUARD that the retired names can never return -- it ships with an EMPTY POPULATION and can only fire on a resurrection nobody is attempting, where numbers are falsifiable by re-measurement (the counter-argument is RECORDED: ADR-0251 dec. 3 praised a burn-down seeded empty, but a burn-down guards a live invariant over a live population and this would guard an ABSENCE); deleting GPUSeedReproTest's placement arm and ScenarioPlacementDataTest's can_source arms (deleting a test because its subject moved is how coverage evaporates silently -- both re-aimed, and ScenarioPlacementDataTest's replacement is STRONGER: it walks the whole corpus asserting every non-zero deployment idx resolves, because there is no fallback left). FOUND: config/tune_overrides.json carried a persisted simulation.skip_march for a slug nothing binds. SOFT SPOTS: the arena's boot is unbranched so an unresolvable zone warns and spreads onto an invented row, and the new corpus arm checks the DATA not the resolution to a live tile; and dec. 2 is a DECISION, not a measurement -- nothing enforces that GPUArena stays the boot scene. |
| [0259](0259-an-imperative-is-a-lead-entry-not-a-fifth-slot-and-its-charge-is-the-third-half-of-the-pre-turn-image.md) An imperative is a lead entry, not a fifth slot, and its charge is the third half of the pre-turn image | `engine` | · | · | 1 | `0005`, `0023`, `0048`, `0068`, `0137`, `0173`, `0235`, `0239`, `0242`, `0247`, `0252`, … |
| | | | | | AN IMPERATIVE IS A LEAD ENTRY, NOT A FIFTH SLOT, AND ITS CHARGE IS THE THIRD HALF OF THE PRE-TURN IMAGE (#1006). SS5 is settled design -- a one-shot top-priority lock-on that self-removes, removed on CombatLoop.action_committed or by a watchdog, NO refund on expiry, finite charges per unit per battle, independent of the turn's gambit edit -- and SS4 adds the fifth rule: cancel must refund any charge spent that turn or cancel becomes a trap. What could not be executed before was the word TOP. dec. 1: an imperative is a LEAD ENTRY in the encoded buffer and costs no slot -- the shader walks slots ascending and takes the first match (evaluate_gambits_up_to), so priority is a POSITION and no shader changes; the room already existed, GambitList.VISIBLE_SLOTS 4 vs GPUConstants.MAX_USER_GAMBITS 5 with ADR-0048's safety net after both. dec. 2: it rides the commit's SECOND write as a PARAMETER, not a third write -- AdjustmentTurn.commit gained a `lead` defaulting to null and GambitEncoder.encode_for_unit(unit, lead) is the one place that knows a lead goes first; a host pushing it separately would write that buffer twice per commit with only call ordering deciding what the kernel reads. dec. 3: the charge is a THIRD HALF of the pre-turn image and is neither of the other two -- it is in no SSBO for the director's snapshot and on no Character for AdjustmentTurn's image, so ImperativeGambits is wired to the SAME three director signals. dec. 4: charges are NOT on the Character, and that is a rule about PERSISTENCE -- the Character is durable and saved (ADR-0005), so a stored lock-on would still be armed next battle and a stored charge would be refunded as a side effect of restore_mutable_from rather than by a rule anyone wrote. dec. 5: re-aiming is a NEW order and costs a charge (a free re-aim makes the first issue a draft rather than a decision). dec. 6: cancel refunds ALL of the turn's charges and restores the order that STOOD -- not one charge (two issues on one turn is a re-aim and both were paid for) and not a bare clear (an order from an earlier turn must survive a later cancelled one). dec. 7: the watchdog is a deadline in TICKS and a pool-emptiness test and there is no third clause -- SS5 rules out reachability, LOS and affordability by name because each duplicates shader logic on the CPU and the copies will disagree. dec. 8: TICKS and not turns, because the world is frozen for the whole of a turn (ADR-0239); default 120, about eight of a Speed-7 unit's own turns at speed-per-tick to TURN_METER_FULL 100. dec. 9: SS5's 'target dead/removed' is the target POOL running out, because the GPU cannot express a named target -- TargetSelector.PoolType.SPECIFIC_UNITS is in GambitEncoder.UNSUPPORTED_POOL_TYPES (ADR-0023), so an order claiming to lock onto one unit would encode as something else and the screen would be LYING about what it armed; an imperative locks onto a RULE. dec. 10: action_committed is the right removal edge BECAUSE A MOVE DOES NOT RAISE IT -- it is keyed off the GPU's cast_step_id bumping, one per ACTION (ability or pure attack), so an order to attack across the map survives the approach and is spent by the blow. dec. 11: neither removal edge refunds. dec. 12: the watchdog rides _process, the FIRST thing GambitBattle has added to the pump -- it cannot go on a turn edge because the world runs BETWEEN turns, which is exactly the stretch an unconsumed order is exposed for. dec. 13: the surface gains a LEVEL, not a form -- the payoff ADR-0255 dec. 2 was written for: a fifth SLOT-level row that is not a slot, opening Level.IMPERATIVE, three strings and a _show_level case on the same generic GambitSurfaceMenu. dec. 14: the order is composed on a DRAFT and the draft is NOT a slot -- _gambit() hands out the draft while an order is in flight, so choices_for's applies are written once and the CHOICE level never learns which it serves. dec. 15: the order asks TWO questions (Do, To) and its To is a WHOLE AIM spanning BOTH selectors (condition_target picks the pool, action_target acts on whoever it picked) -- an order that matched on one unit and acted on another is not a lock-on; no When and no If, because an imperative is unconditional by construction and a condition would make it a rule, which is what the four slots already are; 'Them' is dropped because with no standing trigger there is nothing to have been triggered by. dec. 16: composing is FREE, only Issue costs, and the charge count is printed on the Issue row so the cost sits next to the press that pays it. dec. 17: the new arms went into the two tests that ALREADY HAD THE SETUP (charter clause 13) -- AdjustmentTurnTest 51 -> 118, GambitSurfaceTest 45 -> 94, ZERO new processes and ZERO census bump. REJECTED: a fifth GambitList slot (ensure_fixed_size pads to four, the list is what the durable Character persists, and it would not be top-priority without a sort); a priority flag plus a shader sort (position already IS priority, so the flag is a second encoding of the same fact); naming a specific target unit (unencodable, so the screen would lie); refunding on watchdog expiry (SS5 in as many words); charges shared across the squad (one lock-on for the whole squad -- and it is the implementation that passes every arm about a SINGLE unit, which is why arm 8 asks about the neighbour); taxing the turn's gambit edit as well (SS5: charges are already the scarcity); a 'stand down' row (the turn's cancel is the undo, and a stand-down at any time lets the player dodge 'a wasted lock-on is a real mistake'); a separate ImperativeSurfaceTest scene (re-pays a full Gariland boot and joins two census ratchets); awaiting real frames for the watchdog (the sweep only acts while RUNNING and a commandable turn opening mid-wait freezes it -- a HANG, worse than a failure because nothing names it). CONSEQUENCE: GambitEncoder grew encode_for_unit + authored_gambits and LOST A DUPLICATED FILTER -- 'what has this unit actually authored' is now one answer. AdjustmentTurn.commit gained a fourth parameter and every existing caller is unchanged (its 51 pre-existing assertions passed on the new signature before an imperative arm was written). Two ADR-0068 static-var tunables: gambit.imperative_charges (2) and gambit.imperative_watchdog_ticks (120) -- SS5 names the first one's successor itself (job-derived later), still map #886's fog. MEASURED: five seeded defects, each reddening only its own arms -- cancel refunding nothing reds 4 while cancel()'s RETURN stays green (the arms separate the report from the effect); the lead appended last reds exactly the 2 positional assertions; dropping the watchdog's pool clause reds 2 and leaves the deadline arm green; _gambit() never handing out the draft reds 5 including 'slot 1 is UNTOUCHED', the only arm that can see the player's own rule being overwritten; deleting _sweep_imperatives() from _process reds exactly 1 (arm 13), which is why arm 11 also asserts action_committed.is_connected outright. FOUND MID-BUILD: arm 13's first version cranked ONE hand-called frame and failed -- CombatLoop.tick deliberately KEEPS its accumulator remainder across a turn-gate break (ADR-0239), so one frame can drain it, open a turn, and leave the sweep correctly gated out. NOT HERE: the lists are still EMPTY (#892), so on Gariland an imperative is the first and only rule most units have and ADR-0048's safety net is the rest -- SS7's seeded per-job playbook is map #886's own fog. Gambit.enabled stays unexposed and the imperative does not use it: an order is REMOVED from the buffer, not disabled in it, because a disabled slot is a slot and an imperative is not one. |
| [0260](0260-the-turn-meter-is-a-dwell-clock-and-the-dwell-is-two-ability-cooldowns.md) The turn meter is a dwell clock, and the dwell is two ability cooldowns | `engine` | · | · | 3 | `0001`, `0068`, `0236`, `0239`, `0242`, `0253`, `0256` |
| | | | | | THE TURN METER IS A DWELL CLOCK AND THE DWELL IS TWO ABILITY COOLDOWNS (#939, design S3). ADR-0236 built the meter and did not set its rate; ADR-0242 measured the unset rate (244 turns to annihilation, a stop every ~0.11 s) and handed it to a ticket. The answer rests on a PREMISE CORRECTION, not on arithmetic: dec. 1: A TURN IS A RECONFIGURATION, so what this number sets is DWELL -- how long a gambit config stays committed before the player may change it -- not an action budget. Every earlier attempt priced it as 'how often should a unit act' and produced a number that felt arbitrary, because NOTHING HAPPENS ON A TURN: the world freezes, an invisible priority list changes, the world resumes. The reframe supplies the failure condition the action framing could not: a config re-tunable faster than it can express itself is a turn that means nothing. At FULL=100 a unit was re-aimed ~18 times per action (17-tick dwell vs a 300-tick cooldown) -- one decision, 385 times, before anything could change the answer. dec. 2: THE DWELL IS 600 TICKS, TWO ABILITY COOLDOWNS -- all 512 rows of effects.json carry cooldown_ticks 300, so the action cycle is a 5-second CONSTANT OF THIS GAME and the only non-arbitrary unit of measure available; dwell is quoted in cooldowns so the decision survives abilities ceasing to share one. One cooldown is a twitch (the config has not yet had the chance to be WRONG); three is most of a Gariland fight. dec. 3: THE LEVER IS THE METER'S WIDTH (100 -> 3600 = dwell x base Speed 6), NOT A TICK DIVISOR -- a divisor reaches the same ratio and advances every unit on the SAME tick boundaries, quantising crossings into clumps, i.e. it fixes the first half of the report ('turns come up way too quickly') by re-manufacturing the second ('they keep happening all at the same time'). dec. 4: SPEED STILL MODULATES THE RECONFIG TEMPO (gain = max(1, U_SPEED)) -- self-evident under the old framing, a NEW FICTION under dec. 1 ('agile units take new orders more often'), chosen deliberately to keep a ROM stat load-bearing; currently INERT at Gariland where every unit measures Speed 6. dec. 5: THE RATE IS PER-UNIT AND NEVER NORMALISED BY LIVING COUNT -- rejected not on fidelity grounds but because it would make the clock a function of the death toll, so a rollout's meter would depend on kills INSIDE the rollout and the AI would reason about a clock it can accelerate by killing. dec. 6: THE OPENING STAGGER WIDENS FOR FREE and stays full-width -- ADR-0236 dec. 4's seeded start is pcg_hash % TURN_METER_FULL, so [0,100) becomes [0,3600) with no second edit. THE STAGGER WAS NEVER MISSING, IT WAS ONE TURN INTERVAL WIDE: spread over a meter that filled in 17 ticks, uniform-in-[0,100) meant every unit was ready within a fifth of a second of every other, indistinguishable from lockstep. dec. 7: a GENERATED CONST, not an ADR-0068 tunable -- a live slider means promoting it to a battle-header field or uniform that every rollout then carries, real plumbing to save ~30 s of rebuild. dec. 8: SHADER_VERSION does NOT move (no offset changed; bumping it would spend the one signal that a packed record has gone stale). REJECTED: TurnDirector.playback_rate as the lever (it is the between-turn VIEWING rate -- it stretches the gaps between stops and cannot reduce how many stops there are); a fractional/fixed-point gain (arithmetically identical, changes what the field means); shipping the enemy-turn marker in the same PR. MEASURED: GambitBattleTest, Gariland to annihilation with the AI on -- 385 turns BEFORE, 8 AFTER, over a ~1,779-tick battle. U_SPEED read off the GPU is 6 for all eleven living units (the ticket's arithmetic had been quoting 8 from the test rigs, and a gain of 1 -- which the low turn count first suggested -- would have made dec. 4 vacuous). THE STOPS-PER-BATTLE WAS PROJECTED AT ~30 AND MEASURED AT 8: the projection assumed a full roster and Gariland ends 2-of-11 alive, so ATTRITION, not the meter, sets the count -- the dwell is the decision and the stop count is a consequence. TWO TESTS FIXED, NEITHER BROKEN BY THIS: GambitSurfaceTest arm 5 and GambitBattleTest arm 4b both asserted `not adjustment.is_open()` after a commit, described as 'the window closed with the turn' -- a stronger claim than commit can honour, since TurnDirector.commit DRAINS and a second unit ready on the same tick has its window opened inside the same call, so the assertion really tested 'nobody else was ready on this tick', a property of the SEED. Measured at the new width: unit 1 committed at meter 3602 while unit 4 sat ready at 3601. Confirmed LATENT rather than introduced by re-running at FULL=100, where it passes with one unit ready and the seeded meters [17,20,19,0,19,38,...] show units 2 and 4 sharing a crossing tick anyway -- IT PASSED ON LUCK AND THE WIDER METER RE-ROLLED THE LUCK. Both now assert the invariant commit owns: the window is not open ON THE COMMITTED TAKER. CONSEQUENCE: ADR-0259 dec. 8's imperative_watchdog_ticks default is INVALIDATED and re-derived 120 -> 600, because that default was derived FROM THE METER ('eight of a Speed-7 unit's own turns' at FULL=100) -- at FULL=3600 120 ticks is a fifth of a turn and under half of one 300-tick cooldown, so an order would expire before its unit could act on it once and expiry does not refund (ADR-0259 dec. 11); re-priced in the ACTION CYCLE it actually races (only an action spends an imperative, ADR-0259 dec. 10) at two cooldowns, so it lapses exactly when the player may re-issue it and does not move again if the dwell does. H=400 is unchanged but now covers ~2 fleet-wide stops instead of ~90, which is healthier for a one-ply search (the rollout finally sees the consequence of an edit before the next edit lands) and makes ADR-0253's owed refit doubly owed, since f was fitted on a corpus generated at the old tempo; thinking beats per battle fall by the same factor so ADR-0256's 250 ms cap is no longer under pressure from turn FREQUENCY; and design S3's 'cinematic focus beat' for enemy turns is downgraded to a BRIEF STOP WITH A MARKER, since a reconfiguration has nothing to show -- shipped as its own ticket so the dwell can be felt alone. SOFT SPOTS: 600 ticks is a first FELT value, not a measurement, and only a person playing the mode can refute it; the dwell is priced against Gariland's ~1,750-tick length and nothing holds the count stable across scenarios (deliberately, per dec. 5); and THE DECISION COUNT IS BOUNDED BY BATTLE LETHALITY, which this ADR cannot reach -- eight stops is what a 30-second fight that loses 9 of 11 units can carry at a meaningful dwell, and the lever that would buy more decisions is a longer, less lethal battle, which is nobody's ticket yet. |
| [0266](0266-a-turn-opens-on-a-taker-that-has-committed-to-nothing.md) A turn opens on a taker that has committed to nothing | `engine` | · | · | 0 | `0224`, `0235`, `0258`, `0264` |
| | | | | | `Battle`, not `Battlefield` or `UI`: the reported defect was a camera travelling to an empty tile, but the rule is about WHEN a turn may open against the GPU kernel's movement-step semantics (logical position is the destination for the whole step). |
| [0274](0274-the-search-maximises-a-named-objective-and-the-terminal-verdict-takes-the-endpoints-instead-of-an-invented-weight.md) The search maximises a NAMED objective, and the terminal verdict takes the endpoints instead of an invented weight | `engine` | · | · | 5 | `0068`, `0237`, `0246`, `0253`, `0256` |
| | | | | | THE SEARCH MAXIMISES A NAMED OBJECTIVE, AND THE TERMINAL VERDICT TAKES THE ENDPOINTS INSTEAD OF AN INVENTED WEIGHT (#1109, rebalancing map #1101). ADR-0253 chose P(victory) because a hand-weighted score needs an invented terminal bonus and no principled value for it exists; this ships a hand-written objective anyway and does not pay that price. dec. 1: THE OBJECTIVE IS NAMED ON THE INTERFACE -- RolloutObjective is the supertype, RolloutValueFunction (fitted-logistic-v1) and ProvisionalObjective (provisional-attrition-v1) its two members, every decide() returns the id, because a corpus whose objective is unrecorded is unattributable later; rank_candidates, the CRN averaging and ADR-0246 dec. 2's incumbent tie-break live on the supertype because all three are properties of the SEARCH, not of what it maximises. dec. 2: TWO MIRRORED SHARES, EQUAL WEIGHT, ZERO FREE PARAMETERS -- v = mean(hp_share_self, alive_share_self), shares not fractions so no roster size is needed, two terms because HP share alone cannot tell six wounded units from three corpses and three untouched ones. dec. 3: THE TERMINAL VERDICT TAKES A BAND AT EACH END AND ONGOING IS CLAMPED STRICTLY INSIDE (TERMINAL_BAND = 0.001; wins in [1-band, 1], losses in [0, band], mutual wipe 0.5, ongoing clamped to [band+1e-6, 1-band-1e-6]) -- a win is not WEIGHTED above an ongoing state, it is OUTSIDE the band one can reach, so the 50/50 between the two ongoing terms only ever orders ongoing states against each other. dec. 3a: A TERMINAL VERDICT IS A BAND AND NOT A POINT, AND THAT WAS MEASURED, NOT REASONED -- the first cut returned exactly 1.0/0.0, and instrumented at Gariland the FIRST beat came back result=TEAM_0_WINS on all 64 candidate rows with t1_alive=0, so all 64 tied at 0.0 and the beat held by tie-break, while f scored the same rows 0.001127 and 0.002705 and could still rank them; an objective flat across lost positions makes the AI stop trying once a battle is decided, which SHORTENS the battle -- the exact quantity #1101 exists to measure, so the instrument would have biased its own reading. A terminal verdict now orders inside its band by the winner's remaining hp_frac (a fraction, not a share: at a latched battle the loser's HP is 0 and every share is 1 or 0), and both perspectives read the same `spent` so they still sum to 1. dec. 4: DELIBERATELY CRUDE, and the ranking key stops claiming to be a probability -- p_victory/p_incumbent become value/value_incumbent and the log prints 'v' rather than 'P(win)', because a key name is a claim and only one objective can honour that one. dec. 5: NO MP TERM, NO STANDOFF TERM, NO POWER-SHARE TERM -- the first two are exactly the fitted coefficients that became perverse instructions (mp_frac_self +2.23, engage_self +0.0495/tile), the third is constant across a battle and therefore across the K candidates of one beat. dec. 6: IT READS result AND result's ZERO IS A LEGITIMATE VERDICT -- RESULT_TEAM_0_WINS == 0, so an unwritten record spells a team-0 win; stage_victory writes tick+1, so ticks <= 0 is the refusal. dec. 7: ONE SELECTION AND BOTH CALLERS READ IT -- RolloutDriver.objective_id, deliberately NOT a Tune slug (a mode whose change invalidates every corpus written under the other one is not a scrubbable number), and make_objective REFUSES an unknown id rather than falling back to f. dec. 8: THE HORIZON GUARD HAS THREE STATES AND ONLY AGREEMENT IS SILENT -- check_horizon_against_artifact returned true whenever an artifact declared no knee, which is exactly how a hand-written objective would have walked through it; under the provisional objective H=400 is a COST number (ADR-0256 dec. 6) and not a knee, so #1109's warning about pulling the refit back into scope does not fire. REJECTED: a minimal refit pulled into scope (needs a corpus at the settled tempo, which does not exist, and would be thrown away); hand-picked coefficients in the existing logistic artifact (the zero-code-change drop-in -- it fakes a probability, is_loaded() would report a hand-edited file as a fit, and the horizon guard would pass in silence); the game on f with only the referee on the stand-in (the rig would referee a game nobody plays); an alive_frac term (the result record carries no per-team slot count and reaching past it costs 33-51 ms/feature, ADR-0237 dec. 7); scoring the draw as a loss (breaks the perspective complement). MEASURED: RolloutValueFunctionTest arms 6-10, in the SAME process (charter clause 13) -- arm 6 seeds the clamp away and reds on terminal dominance while every value stays in [0,1] -- and it only reds on its SATURATED fixture, because a merely crushing position scores 0.944 and stays under a win with the clamp gone, which is how the FIRST cut of that arm went green against its own seed; a third seed collapses the terminal band to a point and reds the lost-cheaply/lost-dearly ordering, i.e. the defect dec. 3a measured live; arm 7 is a CONTROL ARM proving both halves on the same rows (the provisional objective scores full-MP and spent-MP identically, the fitted f does not); arm 8 seeds the ticks <= 0 refusal away and a zeroed record scores a perfect 1.0 for team 0. SOFT SPOTS: the 50/50 weight is still a weight, bounded but unmeasured; the objective is blind to position, MP, status and action economy, so everything past tick H is carried by the horizon alone; nothing has validated it against a played corpus because none exists at this tempo (#1110, #1111); RESULT_DRAW at 0.5 collides with a level ongoing state and the incumbent tie-break resolves it; GPUBatchSimulator.get_battle_result reads result off the same possibly-unwritten slot with no such guard. |
| [0275](0275-the-gambit-lab-is-a-cell-a-mirror-and-a-verdict-the-kernel-writes.md) The gambit lab is a cell, a mirror, and a verdict the kernel writes | `method` | · | · | 11 | `0023`, `0048`, `0051`, `0069`, `0158`, `0223`, `0242`, `0264`, `0265`, `0268`, `0270` |
| | | | | | THE GAMBIT LAB IS A CELL, A MIRROR, AND A VERDICT THE KERNEL WRITES. evaluate_gambits_up_to has TEN failure points and ONE stamps a reason (REASON_GAMBIT_FAILED, overwritten by each later attempt); check_gambit_conditions discards WHICH condition failed and what it measured -- so rule group B is debugged by inference and no number of fixtures fixes it, because the reason is computed on the GPU and thrown away. A development instrument, permanently; it never ships. dec. 1-2: the primitive is a CELL (one actor, ONE dummy, one condition, one boot), never a tableau -- a crowded scene's answer is joint over a population; every positive cell auto-generates its MIRROR and a pair that fails to flip is a defect regardless of expectation; a LADDER cell requires each earlier slot to decline for the PREDICTED reason. dec. 4-5: the kernel writes a per-slot verdict into GM_RESERVED_14/15 -- two free ints per slot per unit per battle, ZERO buffer growth; int A packs {failure_point, condition_index, opcode, evaluated_mask}, int B is the MEASURED PAYLOAD, because 'actual 62%, threshold 50%' ends an investigation and a fine per-opcode enum restates what you knew. GambitData LOSES readonly and that cost is stated out loud. dec. 6-7: layout is FLEET-SHAPED (battle,unit,slot) because the rollout runs the SAME kernel entry point, but GATED to one actor -- gating is cheap, layout is not. Ships always-on; threshold fixed BEFORE the number exists: GPURolloutBudgetBench interleaved A/B/A/B/A/B, gate if the run leg regresses >5% (rollout predicts ~138ms against a 250ms cap). A spec-constant variant is rejected up front -- two compilations of the AI kernel is a guaranteed divergence bug. dec. 8-10: a HAND-AUTHORED straddle table, one row per encodable opcode, guarded; generic +/-1 perturbation rejected (manufactures false negatives silently); an unstraddleable opcode produces the positive cell only, LABELLED; an unplaceable cell REFUSES and is counted, NEVER nudged -- a nudge fails PRODUCTIVELY. dec. 11: MEASURED -- MAP042 (67 of 84 fixtures) CANNOT host a controlled-distance experiment: largest fully-flat Manhattan disk radius 1, and at 10 wide the cardinal margin maxes at 4 so r=8 is GEOMETRICALLY impossible. MAP116 is 11x11, 121/121 walkable, uniformly height 0, flat rings to r=10 -- the only map of 119 with a flat disk above radius 3. Existing fixtures replay on their OWN maps: 46 of 84 use plain ATTACK, which is height- and LOS-sensitive, so re-hosting changes the experiment. dec. 12: throw is straddled by setting the ACTOR'S SPEED (speed/2+1, unbounded) -- it DEFINES the quantity rather than corrupting it. dec. 13-14: two pieces on the fleet/live line -- a hostless fleet sweeper in tools/ beside rollout_corpus.gd, and a thin CombatHost in src/scenes/ on ADR-0069's standalone-window precedent. ADR-0264 DOES NOT REACH THIS HOST and the argument is recorded: 0264's whole rationale is the three battle-entry invariants (opener framing, deployment idle tick, ENTD facing) and a synthetic two-unit cell reproduces NONE of them. A mode on GambitScenarioRunner rejected -- 84 fixtures read behaviour off it. dec. 15-17: the sweep is an INSTRUMENT and never joins the suite (GambitScenarioRunnerTest is already the 4th-slowest test at 171.28s, and a sweep would be red on day one for reasons that are FINDINGS); 'promote a subset later' rejected because the frozen expectation would be generated from current behaviour, ratcheting today's bugs into the guard; report is a gitignored artifact, INPUTS are committed; fixtures are READ-ONLY, no save-as-fixture button. dec. 18: the lab proves it can fail -- ~10 seeded cells, one per failure point. Trusting the kernel is the trap: a write that never fires leaves a zeroed field INDISTINGUISHABLE from 'nothing declined'. dec. 19-20: confounds are LABELLED never suppressed (ADR-0270 already made the safety net a dim visible row, so it is free; slot index is MAX_USER_GAMBITS, derived); the live arm mounts the REAL UIGambitEditor3 because a panel routes around GambitEncoder and editor->encoder drift is a bug class this exists to catch. dec. 21: #1105's status census is CONSUMED not rewritten (artifact on PR #1115, open); the formula census is a committed list plus a static-guard, not a runtime GLSL parser. dec. 22: unreachable opcodes are REPORTED, a raw-opcode injection path REJECTED -- it would exercise battles that cannot exist. The gap is TWO conditions and TWO selectors as of #1114, which landed COND_DISTANCE_LESS/GREATER, COND_IS_DEAD, COND_IS_ALIVE and TARGET_NEAREST_ALLY_OR_KO WHILE THIS ADR WAS BEING WRITTEN -- the number is a measurement with a date, not an invariant. dec. 23-24: kernel write + bench FIRST (the only thing that can kill the design), then the verdict guard, then the live arm; v1 is done when the lab EXPLAINS the existing XFAILs, not when a sweep completes (breadth before correctness) or when it boots (a demo is not an acceptance). FINDING REPAIRED: ADR-0264 contradicts ADR-0242 dec. 1 without naming it in prose (grep -c 0242 -> 0); CLASSIFICATION.tsv DID record the edge, so it is a prose gap not a data gap -- but a session reads the ADR, not the TSV. Marker added to 0242. REJECTED: twenty more fixtures (fails on MECHANISM -- a fixture records what fired, the reason is discarded); a CPU mirror of the range predicates (tests the transcription, and gambit-rules D6 forbids two copies of the arithmetic); widening RESULT_SIZE (per-battle, cannot express per-slot); bumping UNIT_SIZE (no padding, U_* is 0..101 contiguous -- costs a SHADER_VERSION bump plus a wider copy_unit_to_next every unit every tick forever); a player-facing sandbox; wayfinder (it maps OPEN decisions and this closes thirty). SOFT SPOTS: S1 the 5% threshold has no positive control -- the bench has never REJECTED a change. S2 possible vertical-tolerance UNITS BUG, untraced: terrain height is documented in FFT HALF-STEPS and packed raw while ability vertical is compared raw, so vertical checks may run at half their intended reach. S3 PR #1115 is open and conflicting. S4 79 of 84 fixtures do not account for ADR-0048's injected net. S5 the corpus never exercises separation beyond 10 and 39 of 84 sit at <=2, which is WHY dec. 11's measurement went undiscovered -- coverage of the range-8 abilities is ZERO. |
| [0277](0277-the-lever-set-is-a-strict-partition-baked-at-load-and-a-hollow-lever-is-an-error.md) The lever set is a strict partition baked at load, and a hollow lever is an error | `engine` | · | · | 5 | `0003`, `0047`, `0068`, `0235` |
| | | | | | THE LEVER SET IS A STRICT PARTITION BAKED AT LOAD, AND A HOLLOW LEVER IS AN ERROR (#1106, the keystone of build map #1101; unblocks #1107, #1108, #1119, #1122). Charting left five questions open and four measurements killed the premises of three of them: the config buffer is THIRTEEN INTS so a ~30-row table was never going to ride it; there is NO WEAPON TABLE on the GPU at all (a weapon reaches the kernel flattened into per-unit fields at pack time) while abilities do have one, so 'how does a factor reach the GPU' has two answers; and the three candidate ability partitions DO NOT COMPETE -- `formula` is present on exactly the 368 Normal records and absent on all 144 others (zero overlap), `ability_type` puts 367 of 512 in one bucket, and skill sets are NOT A PARTITION (224 of 355 covered abilities sit in more than one set, 157 of 512 in none). dec. 1: the vocabulary -- lever set (the file) / lever (one entry) / factor (the number) / global-category-record (the tiers) / effective factor (their product) / LEVERED (a value that has been through the layer, because a unit's wp is levered and the item's wp is not). dec. 2: tiers 2 and 3 are BAKED AT LOAD into whichever buffer already owns the quantity -- the ability buffer, or the packer's extract -- and the fork property the ticket calls load-bearing survives MORE strictly than on the config buffer, because a rollout copies the buffers a bake lands in. dec. 3: THE PACING PAIR IS A SEPARATE LAYER and is not in the lever set; neither move_time_scale nor damage_scale has a per-record form because no record has a `damage` field, so the 'same quantity at three resolutions' model does not hold for the two levers that already exist. dec. 4: the quantity enum is CLOSED at six -- wp, weapon_range (CAPABILITY, floor 1 when the ROM value was >= 1, because Nagrarock is wp 1 and a 10% nerf would disarm it) and cooldown_ticks, charge_time, mp_cost, w_ev (COST, floor 0); identities like formula_id / element / inflict_mask are deliberately absent, and a factor is bounded to [0.05, 20.0] because deletion is not a balance operation. dec. 5: every lever names its namespace explicitly ({by, key}) and the tiers MULTIPLY (rom x global x category x record); replace-semantics rejected because it makes the category tier decay as you use it. dec. 6: a non-empty `why` AND an evidence class (measured / rom-faithful / placeholder), so the rig can report 'N of M factors are still placeholders' as a number. dec. 7: THE CATEGORY KEY IS A TAGGED UNION OVER A STRICT PARTITION -- formula where present, ability_type where not, item_type for items, one category per record -- so {by: ability_type, key: Normal} matches NOTHING and is a guard error; the escape hatch for 'all Normal x0.9' is {by: all} plus nine ability_type counter-rows, not 82 formula rows. dec. 8: reachability is DERIVED, never listed -- MAX_COOLDOWN_ABILITIES is scanned out of combat_common.glslinc so #1108 moves the numbers without touching this ADR, AND AN EMPTY SCAN IS AN ERROR because an unread ceiling must not read as 'everything is reachable'. dec. 9: HOLLOW IS AN ERROR, PARTIAL IS A REPORTED NUMBER, and errors abort the MEASURING tools while the game only warns -- a silently-inert factor corrupts a measurement without failing it, and a typo in a balance file should not stop somebody playing. Measured: 60 of the 91 category classes sit entirely above the 128-ability cooldown ceiling and 14 STRADDLE it (formula 8 would move 25 of its 41). The guard also reports the worst REALISED factor, because wp 3 x0.9 rounds to 3 (a realised 1.0) and 29 of 127 weapons carry wp <= 5 -- rounding half away from zero beats truncation (which would give 2, a 33% cut from a 10% lever) but hides the resolution rather than fixing it. dec. 10: THE DIGEST HASHES THE OUTPUT, PER SAMPLE, AND MAY CHANGE MID-FILE -- a hash of the composed effective factors plus the pacing layer's live values, one column on every corpus row; output because an input hash moves on a reworded `why` and stays still on a loader change; per sample because read at write time the column would be constant BY CONSTRUCTION and could never witness the mid-run scrub it exists to catch. dec. 11: the reload affordance is a separate ticket and it REFUSES WHILE A BATTLE IS LIVE, because wp is BEHAVE_CARRY and weapon_range/w_ev are BEHAVE_RECOMPUTE, so a mid-battle reload would leave one unit under two lever sets. dec. 12: the lever set SHIPS EMPTY and each of the ten guard arms seeds a defect AND re-asserts the corrected control, so an arm cannot pass by rejecting everything. REJECTED: a per-category table on the 13-int config buffer; a parallel U_*_FACTOR_Q8 unit field the shader multiplies at use (right answer if oracle diffing ever needs both numbers at once); ability_type alone as the category (367 of 512 in one bucket); skill set as the category (not a partition -- and an earlier pass wrongly reported skill sets ABSENT from the tree, a grep run from the wrong directory; assets/abilities/skill_sets.json holds 176); a matching rule instead of a strict partition; replace-semantics; a dense file (~126 rows of mostly 1.0 trains readers to skim); config/levers.json (config/ is machine-scoped, tune_overrides.json is rewritten by the F3 panel, and the lever set is content the shipped build reads); blocking a partially-applying lever (#1108 is removing the ceiling that makes them partial); hashing the file's bytes; a hand-listed reachable subset. RENUMBERED 0276 -> 0277 mid-rebase: main landed its own 0276 (a-sensible-default-is-the-aim) while this branch was verifying, after a scan of every worktree AND every ref had reported 0275 as the high-water mark. SOFT SPOTS: S1 the lever set is EMPTY so no shipped path has ever baked a factor other than 1.0 -- #1107 is the first end-to-end proof. S2 dynamic coverage is decided and not built (#1110's rig), so today the instrument can only report the half that cannot see #1121's 5,103 weaponless slots. S3 w_ev is levered by the right-hand weapon's record and _get_evade_breakdown aggregates. S4 LeverSet.PACING_SLUGS is a COPY of GPUBatchSimulator's two slug constants (cycle avoidance), reconciled by arm 8 but still two registers. S5 the realised-factor report has no positive control on the shipped tree. |
| [0279](0279-the-attack-period-is-the-animations-and-a-lever-buys-recovery-not-a-faster-swing.md) The attack period is the animation's, and a lever buys recovery rather than a faster swing | `engine` | · | · | 1 | `0001`, `0032`, `0047`, `0235`, `0236`, `0277` |
| | | | | | THE ATTACK PERIOD IS THE ANIMATION'S, AND A LEVER BUYS RECOVERY NOT A FASTER SWING (#1107, build map #1101). THE TICKET'S SECOND FINDING IS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT: a per-weapon-type attack period ALREADY EXISTS and is written -- setup_attack_animation ends on U_TIMER = max(MIN_ATTACK_DURATION, total_frames_seq) and compute_unit_state returns early while timer > 0, so the SEQ length IS the rate limit. Measured from the shipped SEQ and item data: 38 ticks for all nine melee types, 40 unarmed, 44 Gun, 66 Crossbow, 76 Bow and 100 Gun-at-range-8, plus a max(dist*10,15) flight tail the firer waits out in AWAITING_IMPACT (one of the five states both brakes call mid-action). A bow at its native range 5 is 2.0x a sword. So 'a bow is a sword with more reach' and 'the downside does not exist to be scaled' are both wrong; what the period lacked was an AUTHOR. dec. 1: attack_period is the SEVENTH lever quantity (domain item, kind cost) -- ADR-0277 dec. 4 closed the enum so a seventh would be a decision, and this is it. dec. 2: its ROM base is the SEQ period and NOT zero, which is why the quantity is `attack_period` and not `attack_recovery` -- a lever multiplies and never replaces, so a base of 0 could not be authored at all. dec. 3: a levered period is realised as RECOVERY, a tail after the animation, never as a longer animation -- ADR-0032 defines ACTING as SEQ playback ONLY, so stretching its timer would have the playback state claim ticks in which no SEQ is playing; the tail is U_TIMER on the IDLE edge, the idiom TICKS_GAMBIT_REEVAL already uses at four sites, so NO new LOGICAL_ACTIVITY, no animation mapping, no brake-list member, no UI. dec. 4: THE BASE IS THE WHOLE PERIOD AND NOT THE ANIMATION -- rom_period = max(attack_duration, damage_frame). For a sword those are the same number; for a RANGED weapon they are not, because damage_frame is projectile_frame + max(dist*10,15) and the firer waits that tail out in AWAITING_IMPACT before it may choose again. Scaling only the animation would make x2.0 mean 'x2.0 except on the three weapon types where it is x1.68, varying with how far away the target stood'. THIS WAS THE FIRST VERSION'S BEHAVIOUR and every melee arm of the test passed while it was wrong -- the ranged arms exist because of it. dec. 5: THE UNLEVERED PERIOD IS THE FLOOR -- recovery = max(0, levered - rom_period), so a factor below 1.0 buys nothing: no shorter SEQ, no faster arrow. dec. 6: the packer bakes the Q8 FACTOR into the unit row rather than the levered value, which NARROWLY REVERSES one of ADR-0277's rejected alternatives ('a parallel U_*_FACTOR_Q8 unit field the shader multiplies at use') -- scoped to this one quantity and for a reason 0277 did not have: all six original quantities have a HOST-SIDE base off the item or ability record, and this one does not (it is SEQ frame data in the anim-timings buffer, reachable in GDScript only by mirroring get_attack_anim_id, which #1147 has just established is WRONG). 0277 dec. 2's purpose survives: baked at load, not live-scrubbable, riding the unit row, so a rollout that forks the unit buffer inherits it. dec. 7: no host-side realised-factor report for this quantity, a NAMED soft spot -- building a host copy of the base to report on it would reintroduce the duplication dec. 6 refuses. dec. 8: ZERO FACTORS AUTHORED, because #1101's destination rules the final numbers out of scope and the measurement removed the evidence that would have justified one. REJECTED: stretching the ACTING timer (ADR-0032); a new LOGICAL_ACTIVITY_RECOVERING (needs an anim mapping, two brake-list rows and a NAMES entry to express what IDLE-with-a-timer already expresses); baking the value and mirroring get_attack_anim_id in GDScript (two copies of a predicate #1147 is about to change); giving attack an ability id and reusing the ADR-0047 cooldown (ability id 0 is a real ability and MAX_COOLDOWN_ABILITIES would become attack's ceiling -- the ticket warned about this itself); fixing #1147's unbounded bow arm in the same change (disjoint blast radius, 36 weapon records' animations); authoring a first factor to discharge 0277's S1. VERIFIED static AND dynamic, agreeing to the tick: TYPE1_MELEE_SWING is 38 frames statically, and AttackPeriodTest -- five arms across both teams IN ONE BATTLE ON ONE TICK CLOCK, each differing from its OWN control only in attack_period_factor_q8 -- measures modal gaps sword 40 / 78 (+38) / halved 40, and bow-at-distance-3 59 / 115 (+56). +38 is the SEQ length exactly; +56 is max(52, 26+30), the PERIOD and not the 52-frame animation, which is dec. 4's whole claim. THREE seeds RUN, each redding a different arm: a no-op consume_attack_recovery collapses every arm onto its control; rom_period = timer_value leaves every MELEE arm green and reds only the bow ('added only 52 ticks against a control gap of 59'); removing the Q8 clamp lets a composed 20.0x20.0 reach the kernel as 102400. THE INSTRUMENT NAMES ITS OWN SUBJECT, because once it did not -- the first version seated FIVE attackers on team 0, a battle is 4v4, the fifth was dropped with no error, and slot 4 belongs to the dummy, so the test read the dummy's 40-tick melee gaps as a bow's and reported them confidently; every arm now asserts its slot's weapon_type and attack_period_factor_q8 before any timing assertion runs. UNIT_SIZE 102->104, SHADER_VERSION 32->33. U_ATTACK_RECOVERY is BEHAVE_CARRY and a GUARD is why -- written RECOMPUTE first and the schema check refused it, correctly: a unit mid-swing still gets a reconfigure turn (ADR-0236 design S3), which would re-pack 0 over the recovery. SOFT SPOTS: S1 no host-side realised factor (#1111 owns the verdict). S2 the lever set is still empty -- only the test bakes a non-identity factor. S3 the ANIM KEY still picks which base applies, so until #1147 lands a Polearm's base is the bow's 52 and a lever on item_type Polearm would scale the wrong base. S4 melee is one bucket -- the tier can distinguish Knife from Knight's Sword by item_type but their BASES are identical, so it can only express differences it invents. |
| [0282](0282-the-cooldown-is-the-abilitys-own-period-and-the-ceiling-was-the-index-not-a-budget.md) The cooldown is the ability's own period, and the ceiling was the index not a budget | `engine` | · | · | 0 | `0047`, `0068`, `0237`, `0277`, `0279` |
| | | | | | THE COOLDOWN IS THE ABILITY'S OWN PERIOD, AND THE CEILING WAS THE INDEX NOT A BUDGET (#1108, build map #1101). Amends ADR-0047 dec. 1, 2 and 6; its dec. 3, 4 and 5 stand. Every one of the 512 abilities carried cooldown_ticks 300 -- the ONLY hand-authored number in an otherwise ROM-faithful balance corpus. ADR-0047 dec. 2's JUSTIFICATION FOR THAT IS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT: the charge period is ct*30 (GPUAbilityLoader), so it exceeds a 300-tick floor only above ct 10, which is SIX abilities -- the flat floor therefore BINDS 123 of the 144 CT-bearing abilities (72 of the 87 below the old ceiling and actually gated), adding +30..+240 ticks on top of FFT's own cadence. It did not merely fail to differentiate, it OVERRODE the differentiation the ROM already had. Also corrects the charted facts: ct is THREE populations, not two -- 224 present-and-zero, 144 present 2..20, and 144 with NO ct FIELD AT ALL (ids 368-511, a 20-field record; absent is not zero). dec. 1: a CT-bearing ability's cooldown IS its charge period (ct*30) -- not zero, because effective spacing is max(charge, cooldown) so at factor 1.0 it paces exactly as FFT paced it while the lever layer still has a non-zero base to scale, and a 0 base is unleverable and reads as a hollow lever under ADR-0277 dec. 9. dec. 2: a free-and-instant ability is priced by JP mapped onto FFT's OWN ct scale (virtual_ct = jp_cost/50, then *30) -- MP CANNOT CARRY IT, 209 of the 224 ct==0 records cost 0 MP; JP_PER_CT_UNIT=50 lands the observed JP range 10..900 on the range ct already occupies. dec. 3: what the ROM does not price keeps the bare floor -- 127 unlearnable monster skills at 0 JP AND 0 MP, plus the 144 with no ct field; only 3 of those 127 sat below the old ceiling. dec. 4: the 300-tick floor is UNCHANGED and is a floor not a default -- #92/#93 established it empirically, the original 60 left no fall-through window for a ct==0 ability like Secret Fist whose cast animation is itself ~60 ticks of ACTING. dec. 5: THE CEILING IS DELETED, NOT RAISED -- MAX_COOLDOWN_ABILITIES is now the whole ability table (512 == MAX_ABILITIES), because of the 384 ids above 128, 240 ARE ORDINARY Normal ABILITIES, not the summons the old comment assumed: an ungated tail wider than the gated head. dec. 6: MEASURED BEFORE RAISING, AND THE MEASUREMENT CONTRADICTED THE TICKET'S OWN WORRY -- VRAM 4 MiB -> 16 MiB on the default 1024x8 fleet, and the per-beat rollout fork rides RolloutHarness.fill_ms which the budget bench recorded at 0.73 ms of a 318.87 ms beat (0.23%), far inside ADR-0237's 250 ms cap; per tick it costs NOTHING because ADR-0047 dec. 4 kept the buffer out of copy_unit_to_next. dec. 7: the constant stays a LITERAL INTEGER in the shader because ADR-0277 dec. 8 regex-scans it and an unread ceiling is an ERROR there -- spelling it = MAX_ABILITIES would silently break the scan -- so GPUBatchSimulator REFUSES TO INITIALIZE if MAX_COOLDOWN_ABILITIES != MAX_ABILITIES, drift being exactly the un-gating the old ceiling did on purpose. dec. 8: SHADER_VERSION 33 -> 34, the cooldown SSBO and the snapshot image both change width. VERIFIED: ADR-0277 dec. 8's 'derived, never listed' claim was TESTED not assumed -- the ceiling moved with NO edit to LeverSet.gd and none to ADR-0277, LeverSetTest's own summary line reads 'cooldown ceiling 512', and tools/probe_cooldown_reach.gd re-measures coverage through LeverSet's own predicate rather than a Python replica of its regex: of the 91 ability category classes, full-reach 17 -> 86, no-reach 60 -> 4, straddling 14 -> 1, and 421 of 512 records now reach cooldown_ticks. The derivation lands on 17 DISTINCT VALUES where there was 1, 15 of them below the old ceiling; 123 abilities faster, 17 slower, 372 unchanged. The regeneration diff was ATTRIBUTED BEFORE IT WAS TRUSTED -- the parser was first run UNCHANGED into a temp dir and came back BYTE-IDENTICAL to the committed effects.json, so the only field that moves is cooldown_ticks (140 records) with key order untouched. The guard is DIRECTION-TESTED: LeverSetTest arm 9 rides the existing process (charter clause 13), and collapsing derive_cooldown_ticks back to a flat default reds exactly 9a and 9b with the message recomputing '123 of 144' from INSIDE the running game; 9c/9d/9e stay green under that seed so they are independent, and 9e is the control that the reachability predicate can still say NO (it refuses 91 ids), without which '1 straddling' would be indistinguishable from a blind yes. REJECTED: raising the ceiling while keeping the flat 300 (gates 240 more abilities at a rate the ROM never chose -- the two halves of #1108 are not independent); RE-KEYING the SSBO to a small per-unit (ability_id, ready_at) table, which nearly shipped because a unit can only gate the abilities in its own gambit slots and MAX_GAMBITS is 6, so action_id (sole producer get_gambit_action_id, stage_compute.glsl:636) takes at most six values per unit and the dense 512-wide row is >98% dead weight -- rejected ON dec. 6's MEASUREMENT, because the cost argument motivating it does not exist and it would be a correctness-neutral rewrite of the kernel's indexing plus a snapshot-shape change bought with elegance alone; copying ct straight in (different quantities, and it leaves the 224 ct==0 abilities at zero); deriving the ct==0 price from MP (209 of 224 cost 0); treating an absent ct as 0 (prices abilities ADR-0277 calls unreachable); lowering the 300 floor so JP differentiates further down (re-opens #93 -- the floor would have to be the ability's own ACTING length, SEQ frame data not reachable host-side, the same wall ADR-0279 dec. 6 hit). SOFT SPOTS: S1 the ct==0 set differentiates only above 500 JP, so 97 learnable abilities produce 7 distinct values and the middle of the range is still flat. S2 JP_PER_CT_UNIT=50 is the one invented constant, principled but not measured against play. S3 nothing has played a battle at these rates and 123 abilities got FASTER while the map's thesis is that battles should get longer -- unmeasured until #1110's rig runs. S4 the 91-refusal control proves the predicate is not blind, not that it is right about any particular ability. S5 TICKS_PER_CT_UNIT=30 is a SECOND REGISTER mirroring GPUAbilityLoader's ct*30 with nothing holding them together. |
| [0285](0285-the-target-column-names-a-pool-and-a-depth-and-nearest-was-the-word-that-was-lying.md) The Target column names a POOL and a DEPTH, and `Nearest` was the word that was lying | `engine` | · | · | 5 | `0023`, `0048`, `0118`, `0139`, `0268`, `0275`, `0276`, `0278`, `0283` |
| | | | | | THE TARGET COLUMN NAMES A POOL AND A DEPTH, AND `Nearest` WAS THE WORD THAT WAS LYING. The player asked for three rows per team -- 'I want all 3 options. Ally, Nearest, Weakest' -- after objecting 'it can't be nearest and any'. THE OBJECTION IS CORRECT AND THE LABEL WAS LYING: today's `Nearest Ally` row encodes TARGET_NEAREST_ALLY, and evaluate_gambits_up_to's Pass 2 RETRIES that type at ranks 1,2,3, so the row has always meant 'an ally, nearest first' while the word on it said 'the nearest ally'. Of the three rows asked for, TWO ARE RENAMES OF THINGS THAT ALREADY WORK (`Ally` is today's TARGET_NEAREST_ALLY byte for byte; `Weakest Ally` is TARGET_LOWEST_HP_ALLY, never retried) and ONE IS NEW KERNEL CAPABILITY -- strict depth did not exist: Pass 2's retry decision reads cond_target_type AND NOTHING ELSE, there is no per-slot flag and GAMBIT_SIZE 16 has no spare int (0-13 host-written, RESERVED_14/15 are ADR-0275's kernel-written verdict+payload). The renames are the dangerous half -- a save that reads back under a re-meant word is a rule the player never wrote. dec. 1: DEPTH IS A RESOLUTION, NOT A NEW FIELD -- one APPENDED ResolutionStrategy member (appended never inserted; to_dict/from_dict serialise the int), so no new almanac member, no schema row, no save migration, and the ADR-0118/0139 schema-admission gate is dodged entirely. dec. 2: NEAREST IS RENAMED NEAREST_FIRST or the defect is rebuilt one layer down -- after the split the member called NEAREST is the one that is NOT the nearest, and leaving that name re-creates the label-vs-behaviour defect in a layer no screen test looks at; the int position does not move so the rename is free. dec. 3: TWO GPU TYPES WHOSE VALUE IS THEIR ABSENCE FROM PASS 2'S GUARD -- TARGET_NEAREST_ALLY_ONLY=9 / TARGET_NEAREST_ENEMY_ONLY=10 call the SAME find_unit_by_criteria(mode=0) as the non-strict pair (identical search, identical metric, identical self-skip) and are simply not listed in the `target_type !=` condition, so they take the existing VERDICT_NOT_RETRYABLE branch: no new verdict code, no new clause. GPUConstants is generated from the shader so it follows; SHADER_VERSION 34 -> 35. dec. 4: THE FAMILY SEED TAKES THE POOL ROW, AMENDING ADR-0278 dec. 2 RATHER THAN FOLLOWING ITS WORDS -- when the player said 'healing defaults to nearest friendly' the only ally row on the screen WAS the pool search, so seeding the NAME would have narrowed every healing and buff ability on the tick the rename landed: a behaviour change riding inside a relabel, the exact shape this ticket removes. family_aim_name returns `Ally`/`Foe` and every seeded slot behaves as it did yesterday. dec. 5: `Nearest` MOVES OFF NEAREST_FIRST IN PROSE TOO AND LANDS ON NOTHING -- resolution_word answered 'Nearest' for the pool search, so 'Cure Nearest Ally Unit when Nearest Ally Unit HP < 50%' claimed a depth the rule did not have; a resolution that narrows the pool by nothing contributes NO WORD and both sentence builders already drop an empty one. dec. 6: FOES SPLIT TOO and the `To` list now SCROLLS -- 8 rows (Self, Them, Foe, Nearest Foe, Weakest Foe, Ally, Nearest Ally, Weakest Ally) against VISIBLE_ROWS=5; order is load-bearing (the cursor rests on the head, Self keeps it, each team's pool row leads its group) and PIXELS COST NOTHING -- `Ally`/`Foe` are 14 px against a 48 px cap the existing widest rows already fill. dec. 7: GambitOptions.nearest_foe() IS RENAMED foe_pool() -- it builds NEAREST_FIRST, and a constructor named after a depth it does not have is how a reviewer confirms the wrong thing in one glance. dec. 8: THE WITNESS IS WHICH SLOT COMMITTED, ON ONE UNIT IN ONE BATTLE -- the two selectors differ in one field and produce the SAME rank-0 candidate, ability, target and reach, so no observable OUTSIDE the slot can tell them apart; scenario B10 puts the strict row at slot 0 and the pool row at slot 1 on one Healer whose rank-0 ally is healthy and reads first_commit.slot, which is not two fixtures compared across two runs. B10c is its POSITIVE CONTROL (same fixture, the 80 HP moved onto rank 0, strict fires from slot 0) because 'the strict slot never fired' is otherwise satisfied by a type that cannot fire at all. REJECTED: packing a STRICT bit into COND_TARGET_TYPE (recommended to the player FIRST and measured to be worse -- it grows the field's meaning and needs pack/unpack mirrored across the GPU boundary, buying nothing two dense offsets in an already-0..8 range do not); a per-slot flag in GambitField (no spare int; taking RESERVED_14/15 blinds GambitVerdictReader and GambitVerdictCellsTest -- an instrument traded for a feature that needs no buffer); offering the free fourth combination (`Weakest Ally` non-strict = 'the weakest ally who is under 50%', falls out for nothing; they asked for three); seeding `Nearest Ally` because ADR-0278 dec. 2 says 'nearest friendly' (those words named a row that has since split in three); keeping NEAREST and documenting the discrepancy (a comment does not survive the next reader; the NAME is what the encoder, the prose layer and twelve string literals read). CONSEQUENCES: existing saves untouched BY CONSTRUCTION (NEAREST_FIRST is int 0, unmoved, and still encodes to TARGET_NEAREST_ALLY); `Nearest Ally` still EXCLUDES THE CASTER and a lone unit's row resolves to nobody (VERDICT_NO_CANDIDATE) exactly as today's does -- strict depth changes what happens AFTER rank 0 fails, never whether rank 0 exists; the (verb x aim) census walks 8,992 cells where it walked 6,744 (aim axis 6 -> 8) at sensible 7,249 / no_op 5 / forbidden 1,247 / ignored 27 / unnamed 184 / never_resolves 280; GambitEncoderTest's rank-walk arm now grades BOTH directions off one table (pool rows retryable, depth rows not) because either arm alone is satisfied by a build that collapsed the distinction in one direction, which is how the label started lying; GambitProseTest's resolution table is EXHAUSTIVE over the enum and asserts its own size, so a seventh strategy is a red rather than a string nobody has read; KO-inclusive strict is UNSUPPORTED not approximated (no TARGET_NEAREST_ALLY_OR_KO_ONLY, so the encoder refuses under ADR-0023 rather than dropping half the ask). VERIFIED: THE SEEDED CONTROL IS THE ARGUMENT AND IT IS ONE LINE -- TARGET_NEAREST_ALLY_ONLY was added back to Pass 2's `target_type !=` guard, the single edit that would re-mean the row, and EXACTLY ONE verdict went red (B10) with exactly the two assertions designed to (gambit_fired_at_slot slot=1 saw slot 0; no_commit_at_slot slot=0 caught a commit at tick 182); B10c stayed green and so did the other 91 scenarios, AND THE HEALING ASSERTIONS ALSO STAYED GREEN UNDER THE SEED, which is what proves a heal on rank 1 is produced by BOTH readings and is therefore not the discriminator. Clean-tree: GambitScenarioRunnerTest 93 scenarios green (85 PASS / 5 XFAIL / 1 pre-existing XPASS, the same set ADR-0283 recorded); GambitEncoderTest PASS at 8,992 census cells; GambitProseTest PASS 63 strings; GambitSurfaceTest PASS; GambitCellSynthTest PASS with NO UNDECLARED COVERAGE GAP (where the two new GPU types prove they are REACHED and not merely declared); GambitVerdictCellsTest 80/0; GambitSafetyNetTest, GambitEncodeSchemaTest, AdjustmentTurnTest PASS; project-wide parse sweep clean. A FIXTURE WAS MOVED FOR A REASON WORTH RECORDING: B10 first put rank 1 two tiles out, and it failed in the WRONG SHAPE -- one commit at tick 61 and no heal on ANYONE, rank 0 included (asserted, so not B7's hijack); every distance-1 cast in the suite lands (B10c 249, I2 246-253) and both distance-2 attempts gave a lone commit and no cast, pointing at the cast-position search. The allies are now equidistant, separated by C1's stable unit-id tie-break. SOFT SPOTS: S1 B10's ranks come from a TIE-BREAK not a distance, so a change to C1's ordering reds it for an unrelated reason. S2 the distance-2 cast failure is UNDIAGNOSED -- two fixtures showed it, the hijack reading is ruled out, and it is left inside B6/B7's open territory, written down so the next person placing a target two tiles from a caster does not spend the same hour. S3 nobody has PLAYED a battle with a strict row in it -- kernel, encoder and screen are each witnessed, but what strict depth feels like is unmeasured, which is dec. 4's whole argument for not shipping it unasked. S4 the `To` list SCROLLS now and no capture was taken of it doing so; the pixel budget is a WIDTH argument and the eight rows past VISIBLE_ROWS=5 are a HEIGHT one that was reasoned, not photographed. dec. 9: `Them` LEAVES THE `To` LIST AND THE GRADE IS WHAT REMOVES IT -- ADR-0283 kept the row because the fourth column NAMES what Them forwards to, and THAT REASONING DOES NOT SURVIVE THE PRESS: landing Them re-mirrors the subject, mirror_of refuses to copy a TRIGGERING aim and falls back to the ACTOR, and the other branch is no different because with a two-row subject catalogue 'not a copy of the aim' can only mean `My` which is the actor already -- so BOTH paths end with the subject on the caster and the row the gate called SENSIBLE produces `Self` under a word that says otherwise, which is #1125's own opening complaint manufactured by the row kept to answer it. aim_verdict now grades EVERY Them aim AIM_UNNAMED (circular pair still NEVER_RESOLVES first) and targets_for withholds it as a CONSEQUENCE not a special case -- the offer list and the grader stay ONE decision (ADR-0276), which is the guard that caught the first attempt. Them STAYS in targets() because that catalogue is the READBACK and _target_text prints the literal 'Self' for any selector it cannot name; the safety net, the imperative and #895's operators all build TRIGGERING aims. THE IMPERATIVE HAD TO CHANGE WITH IT: _order_target_choices graded (verb, ability, Them, row) with Them as the AIM, so the new rule emptied the order's To list outright -- the two were never the same question (a slot asks 'may this verb be aimed at a pool it forwards to', an order asks 'may this verb be aimed at THIS pool'); the verb-and-ROM half is identical because aim_class(Them,row) forwards to aim_class(row), so it now grades the row directly with the caster skip restated explicitly. dec. 10: THE `If` PRESS MUST NOT TOUCH THE SUBJECT -- reported from play, 'when I enter gambits left-to-right I can't choose my or their'. The If apply called _seed_subject, which re-derives the subject from the aim, so a player authoring in READING ORDER (Do, To, Subject, If) had the Subject press overwritten one column later and BOTH choices came out `Their`: the column INERT in exactly the order the four-column layout asks the row to be read, working only if If was authored BEFORE Subject. Invisible as well as discarded -- with a blank condition subject_label returns BLANK, so the press showed no change to confirm or deny. Nothing needs seeding: the subject is already right by the mirror invariant ADR-0283 dec. 3 maintains everywhere else (_clear_condition mirrors, the To press re-mirrors when it was mirrored, _seed_aim re-mirrors on a verb press), so seeding could only overwrite the PLAYER's value because the default is what seeding recomputes. _seed_subject DELETED, not left unused -- its own docstring claimed 'the subject is its own part, so one press of O on it overrides this', the opposite of what it did. VERIFIED (dec. 9/10): DIRECTION-TESTED -- putting the If re-seed back reds EXACTLY ONE assertion (the `My` case) while the `Their` case and the never-pressed default stay green, which is the asymmetry the defect has by construction (it always produced `Their`, so an arm pinning `Their` alone passes ON the bug); that is why the guard asserts the two choices DIFFER and carries the default as a third row. Reproduced before and confirmed after through capture_gambit_surface --author='Attack|Foe|My|HP<50%' (Their before, My after; --then=Subject:My correct in both, which named the PRESS rather than the value). Them's removal was caught by ADR-0276's own guard on the first attempt -- withholding in targets_for alone reds GambitEncoderTest with 'grades sensible but the To list WITHHOLDS it' -- which is why the rule moved into aim_verdict. The To list was PHOTOGRAPHED, not merely asserted: Self / Foe / Nearest Foe / Weakest Foe / Ally. GambitSurfaceTest 266/0, GambitEncoderTest PASS, GambitProseTest, GambitCellSynthTest, GambitSafetyNetTest PASS, parse sweep clean. S5: dec. 10 removes a seed and leans on an invariant instead -- 'a blank condition leaves condition_target mirroring the aim' must now hold on every path that writes either field, where the If press used to paper over any path that broke it; three writers maintain it and the never-pressed default arm guards it, but a fourth writer added later would fail quietly rather than be corrected. |
| [0292](0292-the-render-clock-is-not-the-tick-clock-interpolate-the-remainder-behind-never-ahead.md) The render clock is not the tick clock: interpolate the remainder, BEHIND and never ahead | `engine` | · | · | 1 | `0018`, ` 0239` |
| | | | | | THE RENDER CLOCK IS NOT THE TICK CLOCK: INTERPOLATE THE REMAINDER, BEHIND AND NEVER AHEAD (#1206). CombatLoop drains whole TICK_INTERVAL steps out of an accumulator and the visual path then recomputed from the resulting INTEGER tick, so any rendered frame banking less than one tick redrew every walking unit bit-identically. MEASURED on Gariland: zero-tick frames 66/66 duplicate (100.0%) vs ticked frames 227/1552 (14.6%); after, 5/60 (8.3%) vs 230/1598 (14.4%) -- the arms AGREE, which is the pass. SEVERITY IS SET BY THE MONITOR: a 144 Hz frame is 6.944 ms against a 16.667 ms tick = 0.4167 ticks/frame, so 58.3% of frames banked ZERO, a permanent 60 Hz strobe under a 144 Hz presentation -- which is why it was reported as 'the whole game jitters' and not as a movement bug. AND IT GETS WORSE AS FRAMES GET CHEAPER, so optimising measures the judder increasing. The remainder was already being kept; only the renderer could not see it (ADR-0239's turn gate breaks out mid-drain and keeps it on purpose). dec. 1: CombatLoop.tick_alpha is the accumulator as a share of one tick, refreshed at the visual seam, a READ and never a write or reset -- clamped because the turn gate can leave more than a whole tick banked; playback_scale already scaled the delta going IN. dec. 2: INTERPOLATE BEHIND -- _render_timer = timer + (1-alpha), because timer counts DOWN; the tick firing and alpha resetting CANCEL exactly, which is what makes it continuous across the boundary rather than merely finer-grained. Costs ~16.7 ms of latency. THE USUAL OVERSHOOT ARGUMENT IS WEAKER HERE THAN IT LOOKS AND IS NOT THE REASON: every path already clamps to the move's span and the trajectory is committed at NEW_STEP, so ahead-sampling would evaluate a known curve, not predict one. The real reason is that a step can be CANCELLED mid-flight (dbg_conflict_blocked, retry/wait) and behind can never draw a position the sim did not authorise. dec. 3: calculate_position/get_activity/_resolve_leg take float; MovementLeg IS GPUMovementVisualizer (preloads itself) so one signature covers both levels; update_visual_positions defaults tick_alpha=0.0 so every test driving the bridge keeps per-tick behaviour. dec. 4: the guard extends GPUVisualBridgeInterpolationTest rather than adding a scene (the charter prices tests in PROCESSES) -- three deterministic arms that hold the sim STILL and move only the render clock; the run's real zero-tick frames are PRINTED as diagnosis and never gate PASS, because at 60 Hz only ~4% of frames bank none and under suite load fewer still. dec. 5: arm 3 samples ONLY the plain adjacent walk -- cliff JUMPING holds start_pos and LANDING holds end_pos, plateaus that are STILL BY DESIGN (an indiscriminate sampler read 3998/4359 and all 361 others were legitimate), so it asks the one shape with no licence to stand still and demands 1655/1655. dec. 6: AnimationClock.advance_frame() deliberately UNTOUCHED -- FFT sprite frames are authored at a discrete cadence and are meant to step, not blend.
