| | | | | | Turn queue forecast HUD (#893). A TURN QUEUE ENTRY IS A TURN AND NOT A UNIT. Design S6 settled the DEPTH -- one full round-robin deep -- and that sentence only parses if the queue's element is a turn, which then decides the data structure, the redraw policy and what the strip can say. dec. 1: one card per forecast ENTRY, so a unit fast enough to act three times before a slow one acts once contributes three cards and the PILE in front of the slow unit IS how its wait is shown; measured at Gariland the strip is exactly 11 cards over 11 living units, [4,10,2,8,5,1,0,9,7,6,3], head 4:Squire3 = the director's own taker. Rejected: one card per unit with a 'next in N' number, which is a roster with a countdown and silently collapses the repeat that carries the information. dec. 2: NO NUMBERS -- `ticks_from_now` rides in the data and is never drawn, because a wait shown as a number is the IMPLIED form S6 rejects, and it is the unstable one (the number moves 60x/sec while the order does not, so it is a flicker or a per-frame text rebuild bought for information already on screen). dec. 3: `forecast()` lives on TurnDirector, not the HUD -- loop -> simulator -> battle slice -> TurnQueue rows is exactly what `_open_turn` walks and what #895/#897 want, and a view walking it itself is a second copy of the director's plumbing in ui3, free to disagree about which slice it meant; it returns [] while gpu_simulator is null, which is deployment (ADR-0242 dec. 2) and is what hides the strip with no phase check in the view. dec. 4: TWO EDGES AND NOT A POLL -- `turn_opened` + `resumed` is COMPLETE, because commit and cancel each end by opening the next turn or resuming; rejected a `_process` poll (a GPU readback per frame for a queue that moves at turn granularity, and CombatLoop drains ~16 ticks in ONE frame so a frame-granularity view is coarser than a turn, #891) and `turn_committed` (a second readback a millisecond before the first). dec. 5: the redraw gate is keyed on the ORDER and its witness is a COUNTER -- node identity is VACUOUS here, a queue of the same LENGTH reuses its frames whether the gate fires or not, so `draws()` is the arm (TunablesRegistryViewTest's shape) and identity is kept only for a resize that rebuilds a strip that did not change size. dec. 6: built on UIPortraitFrame and NOT UIRosterBar -- the bar templates every frame at once and a queue interleaves the teams entry by entry, and the bar's ASSEMBLY (CombatUI.tscn + UICombatManager) is what ADR-0137 scoped for demolition, not the card underneath. dec. 7: the two teams FACE EACH OTHER, the enemy card mirrored per entry (CombatUI.tscn's own EnemyRoster convention), the only side-marking the strip carries. dec. 8: GambitBattle frees the inherited legacy CombatUI exactly as GPUArena does -- captured BEFORE the fix, unbound it draws eight empty white 'Unit' cards down both margins, the exact real estate a battlefield HUD wants, and a child of an instanced scene cannot be removed per-consumer (ADR-0204); THE THIRD COPY OF THOSE FIVE LINES IS THE SIGNAL TO DO ADR-0137's GLOBAL DEMOLITION. dec. 9: truncation is reported ONCE on the edge (max_shown 16), because a silently short strip reads as 'the queue ends here' and a warning per refresh is a log nobody reads. REJECTED ALSO: a per-unit CT gauge column (S6's implied form; UnitInfoPresenter's `ct` row stays, a different view for 'how close is THIS unit'); emphasis on the acting card (position is the language dec. 2 committed to); mounting the HUD on the host scene (NavigatorMain would replicate it -- it mounts on the CAMERA, so #898 inherits it in one line); a vertical FFX-style strip, rejected on measurement -- 11 cards fill this frustum's ~315 virtual px of height edge to edge and occupy ~800 of 1024 across. FOUND: A UI3 WINDOW HOST LEFT AT THE CAMERA'S OWN ORIGIN DRAWS PERFECTLY AND IS INVISIBLE -- UIWindowHost writes only x and y, so z stays 0, on the near plane; CombatCamera.tscn gives CombatUI its z=-10 in the SCENE so no script says it, and the first build inherited the omission (11 correct cards, correct order, correct flips, nothing on screen). NO LAYOUT UNIT TEST CAN SEE THIS -- every position it asserts was right -- which is why a screenshot was the acceptance instrument. MEASURED: TurnQueueHudTest 7 arms green with FIVE seeded defects each reddening only its own arms (collapsed repeat -> arms 1+7, no flip -> 3, no gate -> 4, no cap -> 5, right-to-left layout -> 2); TurnQueueTest, TurnDirectorTest, GambitBattleTest, CombatCameraMountTest green unchanged. SOFT SPOTS: the readability calls rest on ONE captured frame and on S6's wording, and are what #939 settles; max_shown 16 has never been reached in a real battle, so dec. 9's warning path is exercised only by its test; and NavigatorMain does not mount this yet (#898), so 'the HUD rides the camera, not the host' is an argument and not yet a demonstration. |
| [0249](0249-the-picker-comes-in-on-one-vsync-clock-and-an-animation-with-no-mid-flight-question-has-no-assertion.md) The picker comes in on one vsync clock, and an animation with no mid-flight question has no assertion | `engine` | · | · | 2 | `0068`, `0084`, `0137`, `0161`, `0162`, `0172`, `0247` |
| | | | | | THE PICKER COMES IN ON ONE VSYNC CLOCK, AND AN ANIMATION WITH NO MID-FLIGHT QUESTION HAS NO ASSERTION. ADR-0247 landed the picker's subtractive dim and stopped there: it SNAPPED on and the grid APPEARED in its cells, against a user ask that named one gesture -- darken the background so it moves into the foreground, especially if the units slide in from the side. dec. 1: the dim's fade and the units' slide are ONE gesture on ONE vsync clock (FormationPickIn), because the background receding IS the cast arriving and two accumulators drift apart under a stall. dec. 2: counted in vsyncs, NEVER tweened on delta -- ADR-0161's rule, and not hypothetical, since the map host runs uncapped at ~1,100 fps and a delta tween would finish the gesture in about one frame and be indistinguishable from the snap it replaces. dec. 3: every value is a PURE function of the tick (dim_fraction_at, slide_frame_at), FormationScreenIn.value_at's shape, so a rig can assert the SHAPE of a ramp without a screen. dec. 4: the lengths are borrowed from measurements and the borrow is named -- DIM_TICKS 30 is WorldMapTownPage.OPEN_VSYNCS (@0x801533B8), STEP_TICKS 2 is the quantisation three console tables agree on and also this screen's own _EQUIP_TICK; replacement condition is a console capture of the picker opening. dec. 5: the units enter from the RIGHT (EQUIP_EXIT_X, the one measured off-screen X), DECELERATING (the time-reversal play_equip_unslide already uses), staggered by ROW (the split unit begin_changejob_slide uses). dec. 6: the picker slide carries its OWN latch, unlike begin_changejob_slide's reuse of the _equip_* dicts, because a Status overlay can open over a picker cell; the body-holder carry is copied verbatim. dec. 7: an animation gets a MID-FLIGHT question, not a rest-state one -- pick_animating, pick_in_ticks, pick_dim_strength (which reads the pushed uniform BACK off the material) and pick_sliding/pick_slide_offset_px (which reads the BODY holder). dec. 8: not a UI3Beat and not an ADR-0084 recipe -- ADR-0161 s5's reason, a beat needs a reverse driver and end_pick frees the dim outright. REJECTED: a delta tween; reusing FormationScreenIn (same shape, opposite direction, and a Node that owns its own quad); reusing the _equip_* latches; keeping a snap-home in end_pick_slide (a seeded deletion reddened NOTHING -- at the landing tick position_at_frame reads frame 0, which returns start exactly, so the last eased sample already IS the origin; the ORDERING is what carries the consequence); ramping the dim back down on close. BUILT: rig 45 -> 70 assertions; 7 seeded defects, 6 reddening only their own arms and the 7th finding the dead code above. UIVitalsBand.build returns its ShaderMaterial for exactly this and _raise_pick_dim was DISCARDING it -- that discard WAS the snap. formation.map.pick_dim is still the unscrubbed 0.35 against the stripe's oracle 0.47. |
| [0255](0255-the-gambit-surface-is-three-levels-of-one-list-and-the-action-menu-dispatches-by-name.md) The gambit surface is three levels of one list, and the action menu dispatches by name | `engine` | · | · | 4 | `0005`, `0016`, `0023`, `0048`, `0062`, `0084`, `0088`, `0137`, `0242`, `0244`, `0247`, … |
| | | | | | THE GAMBIT SURFACE IS THREE LEVELS OF ONE LIST, AND THE ACTION MENU DISPATCHES BY NAME. SS4 makes all four adjustment types legal on an open turn; ADR-0252 built the turn and three of them work today -- gambits did not, because there was no screen. UIGambitDisplay/UIGambitEditor3 were the only UI in the tree that could show or edit a GambitList and both went dark at ADR-0137 Amendment 2 / GPUArena._demolish_legacy_combat_ui, whose own comment names this ticket. dec. 1: the two UI2-era components are NOT re-hosted -- UIGambitEditor3 is a mouse-clicked UIModalWindow (UIClickableField rows, ESC the only key, placed by a UIWindowHost the formation screen does not have) while every neighbour on that screen is a glove-cursored O/X list, so re-hosting it puts THE ONE SCREEN YOU CANNOT REACH WITH THE CONTROLLER in the middle of the turn loop. dec. 2: the surface is SLOT -> PART -> CHOICE and all three levels are ONE class (GambitSurfaceMenu), because what differs between them is the STRINGS. dec. 3: a PART is edited, not a field -- a Gambit is an ActionKind, an ability_id and two TargetSelectors whose cross-product is mostly nonsense, so the screen edits the sentence the unit already speaks (Do/To/When/If plus a named Clear) and each choice is a whole selector built by the domain's own constructors. dec. 4: the part rows ARE the readout, so no second display window. dec. 5: the reachable set is smaller than the legal set and that is the trade -- it is the set a player can read back off the row, and #895's mutation operators write the same list without passing through here. dec. 6: ROWS_ADJUST (Item/Ability/Change Job/Gambit) is game-original, the ROM's five byte-untouched, the two roster verbs dropped because mid-battle they name nothing a turn can do. dec. 7 THE LOAD-BEARING ONE: the coordinator dispatches BY ROW LABEL -- ADR-0247 named the hazard ('an index means nothing across two row sets') and index 3 is Gambit here and Remove Unit there, so an index dispatch reaches a roster verb; a NAME means the same thing in both, and an unknown label still leaves through action_row_chosen so ADR-0247's rule stands for the rows it was written for. dec. 8: the two chosen-row handlers became one (they carried the same body and comment twice, and #941 proved a rule stated in only one of them does not hold). dec. 9: a four-row list gets a four-row home -- ADJUST_HOME_FOR pairs each five-row home with its rows*16+16 twin, asked AFTER the side-flip policy so the menu inherits 'open opposite the unit' for free. dec. 10: X pops exactly one level on BOTH hosts (ADR-0137 Am. 7's map-only rule has no second case here -- this surface is never reached from a roster). dec. 11: the surface does NOT mark the turn touched; unit_act_requested already did, and there is no route here that skips that press. dec. 12: the title is FONT.BIN text -- the atlas carries only Eqp and Ability tabs, FFT has no gambit screen to sample, and reusing the Ability cell would title this window with a DIFFERENT screen's name one press away. REJECTED: re-hosting UIGambitEditor3 (dec. 1); deleting the two UI2 components (they are the record of the full sentence builder and cost one UICompileTest line each); appending Gambit as a sixth ROM row (byte-faithful set, and six rows overflow every proven container at rows*16+16 = 112 vs 96); a ROWS_ADJUST that is a strict SUPERSET so indices agree (keeps two dead verbs to buy index compatibility dec. 7 makes worthless -- the index dispatch WAS the defect); letting GambitBattle dispatch all four rows itself (the host reaching through the coordinator's front door, and 'Item opens Equip' stated in two places that can drift); a five-dropdown editor over the raw struct; a separate UIGambitDisplay readout above the editor (two surfaces showing one list is two places for it to be stale); seeding the gambit lists here (SS7's playbook is still fog on map #886, and the map's own note says which of screen-or-generator fills them is not decidable until #895's operators exist). CONSEQUENCE: AdjustmentTurn.commit's second write (set_unit_gambits) finally has something to carry -- before this it landed an unedited list on every commit. IT OPENS ONTO FOUR EMPTY SLOTS and the rig asserts that rather than assuming it (#892: UnitSpawn mints a GambitList and nothing in the boot path authors into it), because an editor opening onto a seeded list would be reading somebody else's fixture. GambitDeploymentPickerTest moved one assertion (a pick ending now hands the rows back to ROWS_ADJUST, not to empty); GambitBattle.NO_ROWS is gone with no assignment left to guard. NOT HERE: SS5's imperative gambits (#1006), and Gambit.enabled is deliberately not exposed -- nothing in the tree reads it, and a toggle for a field the kernel ignores is a lie the player can press. PLACEMENT IS AUTHORED, NOT ORACLE-DERIVED -- there is no ROM gambit screen to prim-scan; the window rides one Tune key location (gambitsurface.loc.window) and ADR-0244 applies in full, so a SCREENSHOT is the acceptance instrument, not a layout test. |
| [0261](0261-the-back-grammar-is-one-table-and-the-claims-belong-to-the-stack.md) The back grammar is one table, and the claims belong to the stack | `engine` | · | · | 6 | `0084`, `0137` |
| | | | | | The screen STACK's grammar, so UI owns it, even though the defect was played on GambitBattle: the claims it moves onto the stack (camera takeover, pad ownership, the battle pause) are each RELEASED by a host. Supersedes ADR-0137 Amendment 7's CHANGE_JOB exemption. |
| [0268](0268-the-gambit-row-is-a-sentence-read-across-the-screen-and-a-screen-that-owns-the-pad-re-means-the-action.md) The gambit row is a sentence read across the screen, and a screen that owns the pad re-means the action | `engine` | · | · | 8 | `0016`, `0023`, `0062`, `0084`, `0097`, `0137`, `0172`, `0247`, `0252`, `0255` |
| | | | | | THE GAMBIT ROW IS A SENTENCE READ ACROSS THE SCREEN, AND A SCREEN THAT OWNS THE PAD RE-MEANS THE ACTION. Supersedes ADR-0255 dec. 2 (SLOT->PART->CHOICE drill-down) and dec. 4 (the part rows are the readout); dec. 3's whole-values rule, dec. 5's reachable-set trade and dec. 6-12 stand. dec. 1: the surface is ONE ROW PER GAMBIT read across -- Enable/Do/To/If -- with left-right walking the parts and circle opening that part's list under its own column; every entry is a domain-constructed whole value, so dec. 3's nonsense cross-product is never offered. dec. 2: WHEN GETS NO COLUMN BECAUSE 256 PX SAYS SO -- four columns measure 306 px against the ROM frame (content 256 + chevrons 24 + gutter 14 + gaps 12), three measure 232; the condition's subject folds into its named value so If reads 'Ally HP < 50%'. dec. 3: the screen edits condition index 0 and NEVER destroys indices 1+, which renders as +N -- A3 and D5 stay true and #895's operators keep writing multi-condition slots. dec. 4: THE ENABLE FLAG IS A PART, NOT A BUTTON, and ADR-0255 was WRONG that nothing reads it: Gambit.enabled -> GambitEncoder.gd:223 -> GPUCombatPacker.gd:1014 -> stage_compute.glsl:37/652/668, and rule A2 specifies it. dec. 5: THE RULE IS ONE ACTION PER BINDING, NOT ONE INTENT PER BUTTON -- ui_left/ui_right already carry cursor-walk and job-ring-rotate with the guard green; ADR-0137 Amendment 4's hazard was a second action squatting a binding at the SAME depth where the loser silently dies, which a screen owning the whole pad cannot produce. dec. 6: pad 9/10 join rotate_camera_cw/ccw (L1/R1 rotate the camera on the real hardware and were bound to no pad button at all) and the surface re-means them as raise/lower slot -- THE ONLY InputMap CHANGE. dec. 7: square STAYS UNBOUND, preserving Amendment 6's ruling after the player's verdict 'M is crazy'. dec. 8: the option lists are DERIVED from encoder support (rule E1 / ADR-0023), because a screen offering an unencodable choice silently bricks the row it just authored. Rejected: four columns (306 px); two lines per gambit (kept as the playtest fallback); Tab/Shift+Tab as the column cycle (Tab is triangle, triple-bound, and is this surface's own door -- and is_action_pressed defaults exact_match=false so Shift+Tab matches a plain-Tab action); new actions on Q/E (reds _test_one_intent_per_button, the exact mistake Amendment 4 section 4 records); square plus a fresh key; collapsing conditions to one in the domain; left-right cycling values in place; re-hosting UIGambitEditor (ADR-0255 dec. 1 untouched -- the input model was the reason, not the width). Found not fixed: PlayerCamera._input tests KEY_F raw through no action, so the one-intent guard is structurally blind to it. |
| [0269](0269-the-turn-queue-strip-is-a-row-of-framed-cards-on-a-band-up-for-the-whole-battle.md) The turn queue strip is a row of framed cards on a band, up for the whole battle | `engine` | · | · | 7 | `0010`, `0068`, `0084`, `0088`, `0097`, `0137`, `0182`, `0236`, `0242`, `0244`, `0260` |
| | | | | | Turn queue forecast HUD, round 3 (#893 design S6). THE TURN QUEUE STRIP IS A ROW OF FRAMED CARDS ON A BAND, UP FOR THE WHOLE BATTLE. Supersedes ADR-0244 dec. 6, 7 and 9; 0244 dec. 1-5 and 8 stand, and its rejection of EMPHASISING THE ACTING CARD is REOPENED (see the soft spot). The user's five-item review named: no friend/foe read, always-visible-but-only-relevant-on-a-turn, wrong resolution, unclear what it IS, nothing framed. Four are one shape -- the strip is the only thing on screen that is not a WINDOW. The user then reviewed the ANSWER twice more and reversed five parts of it, folded in here rather than stacked beside it. dec. 1: every part is a UI3Element (bar + each card), so it inherits aperture, beats, criteria and clip; the BAR survives dec. 7 taking its frame because it is the BAND'S CARRIER -- it owns the OWN_APERTURE + BOX_OPEN that sweep the backdrop across the screen, and its rect IS the band's extent. It does NOT clip the cards: dec. 3 gave each card its own aperture and made it the bar's SIBLING under the strip root. dec. 2: a TEAM-COLOURED UNDERLAY is the side-marking, working off the portrait shader's magic-0 discard so it tints the SILHOUETTE'S SURROUND; the mirror (0244 dec. 7) stays but is COMPARATIVE and goes silent on three same-team cards in a row. The ROM's baked 'Enemy' word cell was BUILT AND THEN REMOVED -- the sheet does carry the word, but a card does not need the marking twice, and the first build had already recorded that as a soft spot. Rejected AGAIN (premise repaired by dec. 7): a per-card team-colour frame CLUT -- the underlay tints the space you are looking AT, a border tints the space BETWEEN cards where two adjacent enemies merge. Rejected: two lanes, which destroys the left-to-right order 0244 dec. 1 rests on. dec. 3: A CARD ENTERS AND LEAVES BY ITS OWN APERTURE, and the three verbs are SERIALISED -- head closes, THEN survivors shuffle, THEN the new tail opens. This reverses the first build, which FADED and started the shuffle in the same pass. The FADE argument was mechanically right (BOX_OPEN's precondition is inert without OWN_APERTURE, and a private scissor inside the strip's scissor is nonsense) and its PREMISE went: a card needed cropping only because it slid in from off the right edge, and dec. 7's screen-wide band made that entrance untenable anyway. So a card is built AT its slot, declares OWN_APERTURE, and leaves the bar's subtree. The order is a chain on the elements' own closed/moved signals -- there is no barrier facility in the engine and no clock is available (charter clause 14 forbids create_timer under tests/). Only the PLAYS are serialised: the DIFF is immediate, so an entrant is a real element at its real slot with a SHUT aperture and the redraw gate keeps meaning what it meant. A refresh mid-chain REPLACES the pending step (generation stamp), and the chain is ARMED before the retirements because close() can settle synchronously. Transition.FADE keeps its enum member and its registration WITH NO CALLER: the int is unremovable (persisted overrides) and an unremovable member resolving to no beat would SNAP rather than error. The ROM's prayer-dismiss ramp (0x80->0x38 by -8, fiber 0x80131628) stays recorded here as UI3FadeBeat's provenance; the FLOOR to 0x00 is a FORCED divergence because a UI3 close has no window handle to free, and it multiplies ALPHA not BRIGHTNESS. No FAST cadence -- the ROM has no stride flag over this fiber. dec. 4: a computed home answers WHERE with derived() and walks with move_to_answer(); place_at is for KEY LOCATIONS and sixteen slot slugs would be a lying knob sixteen times over, while a literal rect mints a first-write-wins <id>.rect that strands every card on a later scale scrub. dec. 5: BACK is a slide cadence with its OWN overshoot s=1.9 (+12.1%, ~3.9px on a ~32px pitch); inheriting the hover's 0.7 is +1.76% = 0.6px, sub-pixel at the one place the cadence was added for. Its own length too (8 frames vs the table's 6). dec. 6: the refresh DIFFS the queue (greedy, stable, first unmatched (index,team)) so cards ENTER, MOVE and LEAVE; 0244 dec. 5's redraw gate is kept unchanged. dec. 7: EVERY CARD WEARS A FRAME, THE STRIP WEARS NONE, THERE IS NO TITLE, and the chrome is a UIVitalsBand behind the row -- a THIRD position neither prior ADR held. 0244 rejected per-card frames for a row of EMPTY boxes (a card with a face is not empty); the first build rejected them for one bar frame; the user has seen both. Card rect GROWN by UIFrame's own 9-slice margins (4/4/5/4, read not restated) and CEILED to whole display px, or the pitch drifts a third of a pixel per slot and every third gap picks up a stray dark column; spacing 2 -> 0 because the border IS the separation. The title is gone: three words on a 16px band every frame forever to inform a player once -- and its 'the font shader has no clip_world' soft spot stops existing rather than being solved. The band is the SHARED producer at the oracle's base_rgb 120, NOT the nameplate's cheap alpha quad, because it must SUBTRACT from a lit battlefield through the engine fold; mounted as the BAR'S OWN PAYLOAD so the clip engine pushes its aperture uniforms and it rides the box-open for free, keeping the 'no _process' claim. IT RUNS THE WHOLE WIDTH OF THE SCREEN: sized to the cards it stopped at the last portrait, which reads as a bug rather than a layout. Widening the band means widening the BAR, so _bar_rect is the screen's span and its x0 is NEGATIVE. That span is DERIVED (_screen_span_px), never the ~373px literal: under an ortho host it is REFERENCE_CAMERA_SIZE*aspect/ppu and the camera SIZE cancels against UIWindowHost's own counter-scale, leaving the viewport ASPECT. It cost the cards their parent -- authored_home is FROZEN, so a bar at negative x would put every card a screen-margin left of its slot -- and they owed the bar nothing but placement once dec. 3 took the aperture away. _bar_slots is gone with the per-turn reshape; a viewport is not a Tune slug, so TurnQueueHud._relayout re-derives on the host's own resize poll (a DIFFERENT override from the deleted clip-basis half-fix, which relayout could not see). Reshaped never rebuilt (update_extent) -- freeing an enrolled fold member mid-composite corrupts the 4.8 fork's heap. dec. 8: UP FOR THE WHOLE BATTLE, reversing this ADR's own first build. ADR-0260's 8-stops-a-battle measurement is right and the inference from it was wrong: those ten seconds between stops are when the player DECIDES, and a forecast that appears only as its answer arrives has nothing left to forecast. Opens/closes on NavigatorRunner's existing PRE_BATTLE->BATTLE state_changed (no signal invented); GambitBattle says NOTHING and is right to -- no runner, no GameState, the default is LIVE. Hides while a screen covers the battlefield via each host's existing pause_battle hook -- deliberately NOT unit_activated + dismissed, which is a HIDE WITH NO MATCHING SHOW because the MAP host is PERSISTENT and never emits dismissed. The hide PLAYS THE BEAT both ways rather than writing visible, and the strip's open is TWO STAGES: the band sweeps out, THEN the cards scissor open on it. Found by LOOKING -- played together, the band's screen-wide centre-out scissor has not reached the outermost cards while those cards are already open, and they stand on the bare battlefield for four frames. turnqueue.show_only_on_turn is DELETED, not left as an escape hatch: it would name a lifecycle no code implements. turn_opened/resumed are KEPT as CONTENT edges. Not visible during deployment (asked, declined). dec. 9: overflow is CAPPED at eight (turnqueue.max_cards, live) and simply not drawn, and the portraits do NOT shrink -- 0.3646 is the other exact sampling ratio and is the size the user already called unreadable. Replaces 0244 dec. 9's max_shown 16 belt AND its warning: this cap is MEANT to bite on an ordinary round-robin, so a line printed every battle is a line nobody reads. No conflict with 0244 dec. 1 ('an entry is a TURN' is about MEANING, not count) -- the cap trims the TAIL, the least reliable end of the projection. CONSIDERED: critique 3 is ARITHMETIC -- the host counter-scales by camera.size/14 so a UI px is 2.743 screen px; at scale 0.45 a texel lands on 1.23 screen px and is undersampled UNEVENLY, which is what reads as 'high-res'. 0.7292 lands exactly 2.0. The global fix (ppu 0.04375 = a flat 3.0 for all of ui3) is NOT taken -- it moves every window and is its own ticket. PAR is READ, never written. The UI3 page grows an OWNER-KNOB SECTION derived BY NAMESPACE (not a hand list, which is schema living in a view): one row per class-owned slug, plus the ui3 engine namespace, minus anything already editable as a criterion, driver or key location -- the gap was REACHABILITY, since spacing/portrait_scale were reachable only as a driver row repeated once per element inside its own fold. turnqueue.screen_pos joins it and is deliberately NOT a <ns>.loc. key location: no element is homed at it and place_at cannot take it, so a re-home dropdown there would be an affordance that does nothing. FOUND: PORTRAIT_BASE had its two axes TRANSPOSED (an 18px portrait on a 21.6px pitch) -- now derived from UIPortrait's own constants. FOUND: tearing the cards down must clear the redraw gate's memory or the strip re-opens onto no cards. FOUND: the bar must boot SHUT or the first open flashes a full box for one vsync. FOUND: A CAMERA-CHILD HOST LEAVES EVERY CLIP BASIS STALE, AND A STALE BASIS DISCARDS EVERYTHING -- and it had a TAIL that always-on exposed. UIWindowHost._process skipped _relayout on camera TRANSLATION; its comment was half right (screen-local POSITION is invariant under a pan, GLOBAL TRANSFORM is not, because the host is a camera CHILD), so the strip vanished on every map pan and came back full on close/reopen. MEASURED across a pan: the live basis origin moved (6.869333,-6.579999) -> (3.536,-7.691111) while all three payload shaders still carried the pre-pan value -- exactly the camera translation over the host's own counter-scale. Fixed with refresh_clip_basis in UIWindowHost, not in the HUD: it is a property of BEING a camera child. UI3OwnerColorMap._debug_material carried the same defect's other half (it copied clip_world and never clip_basis_inv), which is why the ownership map drew no colour on those screens. The SYMPTOM is untestable twice over -- no layout assertion can see an absent fragment -- so both are guarded by MECHANISM assertions folded into UI3ClipEngineTest and UI3OwnershipMapTest. MEASURED: TurnQueueHudTest 11 arms / 86 assertions, four seeds each reddening only their own arms (band width, close barrier, shuffle barrier, PARENT_APERTURE). FOUND: an ordering claim needs step INDICES, not settled states -- a build that plays all three verbs at once reaches the same settled positions and passes every endpoint assertion, so arm 8 hand-cranks ONE walk and asserts the first-step indices are ordered. FOUND: close() can settle SYNCHRONOUSLY, so a chain must be armed before it is started. FOUND: landing a rect on a SHUT own-aperture element re-opens it (UI3Element._apply_rect re-derives when _settled, and a settled close is settled), so both rect-landing paths re-close IMMEDIATE. TurnQueueHudShot.tscn's mid-open frame is the only thing that can tell a working aperture from an absent one, and since dec. 7 it also shows the band being cropped with the cards, which is what proves the backdrop rides the aperture. All three of this widget's invisible defects were found by LOOKING, none by a test. SOFT SPOTS: the acting-card question is REOPENED with nothing in its place (head-marking is the next ticket, with the declined deployment visibility); dec. 2's underlay was judged against a FRAMELESS card and every card now has a border; the band's strength (turnqueue.band_sub) was judged over Gariland's sky at the OLD card-row width and there is now much more of it; max_cards 8 and spacing 0 are capture calls on one queue shape; each card slot mints ~8 Tune binds so the F3 tree grows ~100 rows. AMENDMENT 1 (2026-09-09): the first dial after the card/portrait split moved four defaults, materialized via tools/materialize_tunables.py -- portrait_scale 0.7292 -> 0.85, portrait_offset (4,5) -> (2,2), spacing 0 -> 3, screen_pos (0.04,0.03) -> (0.015,0.03). 0.85 ABANDONS this ADR's exact-2.0 sampling argument on purpose (it lands 2.33): card_scale is a knob now and STAYED at 0.7292, so portrait_scale no longer sizes the card and 'make the face fill the frame' became dialable -- 0.7292/0.3646 remain the two values that answer the arithmetic. spacing 3 CLOSES the 'capture calls on one queue shape' soft spot: the 0 was a prediction and looking overturned it (two abutting borders read as one thick divider). The PAR soft spot is CONFIRMED, not closed -- live_ui_par is 1.0 and nothing sets 1.25 -- so dec. 9's width is restated at square PAR: a card is 32px, eleven span 11*32+10*3 = 382 of ~373 and do not fit, eight span 277 and do. The CAP of 8 survives its re-measurement; the 'about 353 at PAR 1.25' figure did not. |
| [0270](0270-the-safety-net-is-the-last-row-on-the-gambit-surface-dim-and-inert.md) The safety net is the last row on the gambit surface, dim and inert | `engine` | · | · | 5 | `0048`, `0255`, `0268` |
| | | | | | THE SAFETY NET IS THE LAST ROW ON THE GAMBIT SURFACE, DIM AND INERT. Supersedes ADR-0048 dec. 2 (invisible in the UI editor) and the second half of that ADR's title; ADR-0048 dec. 1, 3, 4, 5, 6 stand. Invisibility was free when there was no editor -- UIGambitEditor3 had been dark since ADR-0137 Amendment 2 -- and there is one now, which on a scenario-booted cast opens onto FOUR ROWS THAT ALL READ '---' and then the unit attacks. That is ADR-0268 dec. 8's defect from the other direction: not a row that reads right and never fires, but a rule that fires and is on no row. dec. 1: the net is the LAST row, after the four slots and after the imperative's, reading Attack / Nearest Foe / Always -- slot order IS priority (A1) and the net evaluates last. dec. 2: DIM under the glove, BLANK enable cell, every verb refused -- inert BEATS focus in the shade band because lighting a row the glove cannot act on promises a press that is refused, and the enable cell is blank rather than the 'x' ADR-0268 dec. 4 chose for a DISABLED slot because there is no state here to toggle. dec. 3: nothing is editable because there is nothing on the other side of the verbs -- the net is in no GambitList (ADR-0048 dec. 3 keeps it a buffer concern) so an edit has nowhere to land; the refusals are structural, not policy. dec. 4: the row is DERIVED from GambitEncoder.safety_net_gambit() (made public for it) and rendered through the surface's own part readouts, never three literal strings -- and 'to' reads the CONDITION target because the net's action_target is triggering(), which renders as 'Them' and names no pool (the imperative row took this decision first). dec. 5: the four authored slots are NOT seeded to match it -- seeding puts a rule in a slot the player never wrote and forces ensure_fixed_size to tell 'seeded' from 'authored'; the net stays reachable through a blank list because authored_gambits filters on Gambit.is_empty() before encoding, the invariant ADR-0048 Amendment 1 found. REJECTED: leaving it invisible and explaining it elsewhere (the screen is where the belief forms); making it a fifth EDITABLE slot (a different feature -- the net stops being invariant and the encoder stops being able to cache one template; revisit once the player can SEE it); seeding slots 1-4; rendering it in the authored shade (a row the glove lights and then refuses teaches the refusal by pressing); growing the window to six rows (GAMBIT_CONTAINER is Rect2(12,134,232,104) on a 256x240 screen -- 134+104=238, and a sixth row lands at 254). CONSEQUENCE: on a battle host the list is six rows and the window shows five, so the net is one down-press away; on the roster host the five fit exactly. The imperative's row sits BETWEEN the slots and the net so reading order does not match priority for it -- pre-existing (#1006), left alone rather than fixed silently. FOUND AND FIXED to make dec. 2 observable: GambitSurfaceMenu._on_selection_moved rebuilt row meshes only when the window SCROLLED and the LIT/DIM shade is baked into them, so up/down moved the glove and left the band on the row the player had LEFT -- an ADR-0268 dec. 1 violation, photographed. MEASURED: GambitSurfaceTest 146/0 -> 164/0; seeded break (delete the row == safety_net_row_index() early return) 134/15. |
| [0276](0276-a-sensible-default-is-the-aim-the-rom-cannot-fault-and-the-preference-is-still-open.md) A sensible default is the aim the ROM cannot fault, and the preference is still open | `engine` | · | · | 5 | `0023`, `0048`, `0049`, `0062`, `0255`, `0268`, `0275` |
| | | | | | A SENSIBLE DEFAULT IS THE AIM THE ROM CANNOT FAULT, AND THE PREFERENCE IS STILL OPEN (#1125 (b)). A player got `Move / Self / Foe HP<25%` and `ThrowStone / Self` from the gambit surface's DEFAULTS -- both read back correctly, both encode with no E1 skip, both reach the kernel, and one spends the turn standing still while the other throws a stone at the unit that threw it. dec. 1: the space is (verb x aim x If-subject) and every cell is GRADED -- 5,058 cells (3 verbs + 278 skillset-reachable abilities, x3 subject classes, x6 aims); enumerating rather than patching the two reported cells, which are instances. dec. 2: FIVE verdicts -- sensible 4,059 / no_op 4 / forbidden 797 / ignored 14 / unnamed 184, and a cell that is more than one is counted as the GRAVER (Move/Them is unnamed AND a no-op, booked as the no-op); `unreachable` is deliberately absent because ADR-0268 dec. 8 and GambitEncoderTest's round-trip already own rule E1. dec. 3: `Move / Self` is the no_op class, ROOTED IN THE KERNEL -- MOVE inherits action_target as its DESTINATION, execute_move_to_unit_gambit's `manhattan_distance(my, my) <= 1` passes on the first evaluation, the slot COMMITS with VERDICT_FIRED so every lower slot is unreachable, and the unit has not moved. dec. 4: `forbidden` is ADR-0049's dont_hit_* triple read as an AUTHORING-TIME classifier for the first time -- 186 of 368 records forbid the caster, and Self is exactly what the screen left an ABILITY slot aimed at. 🔴 dec. 5: FORBIDDEN DOES NOT NO-OP, IT LANDS -- hit_policy_allows has exactly two call sites (stage_damage.glsl:128, stage_spell.glsl:637) and BOTH are inside AoE walks gated on effect_area > 0; ThrowStone's effect_area is 0, so the single-target path applies damage without ever consulting the policy and the caster hits itself. A live ADR-0049 conformance gap, filed separately, xfail-witnessed by scenarios_K_defaults K2. dec. 6: `Weakest Ally` is CONTINGENTLY forbidden for the 186 and is NOT graded -- find_unit_by_criteria skips the caster on NEAREST and ONLY on NEAREST (mode == 0), so Nearest Ally can never be the caster and Weakest Ally (MOST_CRITICAL) IS the caster whenever the caster is the most-hurt friendly, which is exactly the tick a heal gambit fires; the gate removes only what it can PROVE, and the contingency bears on #1144's choice of what a forbidden aim does. dec. 7: `unnamed` is `Them` under a SELF subject and it is #1125's OPENING LINE -- Them forwards to whatever the If column picked, and on `Always` that subject is the actor, so Them IS the Self row under the one name that does not say so; the row is dropped and comes back the moment the If column gives it a subject to forward to. The surface already treated Them this way in exactly two places (safety_net_row and imperative_row both read the CONDITION target for their `to` column because triggering() "says nothing about the pool it aimed at"). dec. 8: `ignored` is Wait with any aim but Self -- execute_gambit_action exempts WAIT from the no-final-target bail and never reads a carrier for it. dec. 9: the `To` list offers only `sensible` cells (GambitOptions.targets_for), extending ADR-0268 dec. 8's derived-list gate one step past the encoder; the gate never empties a list because no record carries all three flags, asserted over the catalogue rather than assumed. dec. 10: the seed onto an empty slot is the HEAD of the gated list -- not a preference, since the head is where the cursor already sits and every row it can land on is one the grader could not fault; Attack keeps dec. 12's explicit Nearest Foe so the column can NAME the safety net's own value. dec. 11: a CONFIGURED slot is re-aimed only when the press made its aim broken (Cure/Self -> ThrowStone strands the row on a value the To list no longer offers); the If column re-seeds too because the subject is half of what `Them` MEANS. dec. 12: the readout probes the UNGATED catalogue -- the offer list is now a SUBSET, and naming an aim out of it would miss and fall back to "Self", printing an aim the slot does not hold. 🔴 dec. 13: ADR-0268 dec. 12's "this screen has no classifier" IS FALSE AND WAS MERGED -- the ROM ships one, already extracted, encoded and consumed. The correction is NARROWER than "so use the flags": dont_hit_* is a HIT POLICY, answering *may it* and never *should it* (Cure legally hits an undead enemy), so dec. 12's restraint was under-justified rather than wrong. 🔴 dec. 14: dec. 12's "Move and Wait mean Self" is also false for Move -- that is dec. 3's no-op, kept pointed at on purpose. NOT DECIDED: the effect FAMILY. After the gate, 153 of 249 reachable abilities still seed Self and 130 of those carry target_reaction_type taking_damage (Fire, Bolt, every summon) -- FFT canon is that summons hit allies, so the hit policy is RIGHT to permit it and USELESS as a default. Three findings recorded so the next session does not re-derive them: the family lives in `formula` and the space is 82 values not ~180; formula alone does not separate buff from debuff (f11 buffs vs f10 debuffs looks clean until f56 holds Heal and Seal together -- the direction is in inflict_mode); and THREE partial family predicates already exist and CONTRADICT each other (AbilityDatabase.is_damage lists f10 as damage while NON_DAMAGE_FORMULAS lists it as not-damage; is_healing is formula==12 while the shader's is_ability_healing reads ABFLAG_HEALING from target_reaction_type, covering 12 AND 13; is_damage has no callers). The vocabulary is player-facing, so it is being ASKED rather than inferred. A SECOND candidate verdict class was found while enumerating and is DELIBERATELY NOT GRADED: `range == 0`, carried by 41 of the 249 reachable abilities (every Draw Out, every Song and Dance, SpinFist, Chakra, StigmaMagic). Range 0 is not "self only" -- the effect is CENTRED ON THE CASTER and radiates, so the To column picks a target for something that does not take one. Statically spell_pre_validate checks MP and NOT range, and the range gate lives on the cast-position path which defers a tick rather than falling through, so the candidate reading is "walks toward a target it can never be in range of, forever" -- NOT VALIDATED, and grading it off the static reading alone would be the a-mechanism-that-could-explain-it-is-not-evidence-it-did mistake. SpinFist is the sharp case: range 0 AND dont_hit_caster, so this ADR's gate removes Self and every row it leaves standing may be a row that never fires. REJECTED: fixing the two reported cells (2 of 801 broken cells); deriving from `formula` now and skipping the hit policy (inverts the evidence); reviving ADR-0255's five-dropdown editor (this ADR is the opposite move -- it makes the offered set SMALLER by proving cells meaningless). CONSEQUENCES: the audit rides in GambitEncoderTest, an existing GPU-free process, per TEST-CHARTER clause 13; it PRINTS ITS SUBJECT and asserts a NON-ZERO count of both broken classes, because a classifier gone blind would grade every cell sensible and pass every "nothing is broken" assertion; rule group K joins tests/gambit_scenarios/ with a control arm, since K1 asserts two absences; 797 cells stop being offered and are REMOVED rather than greyed. |
| [0278](0278-the-seed-is-the-familys-pool-and-the-family-is-ours-because-the-rom-records-a-hit-policy-and-not-a-purpose.md) The seed is the family's pool, and the family is OURS because the ROM records a hit policy and not a purpose | `engine` | · | · | 6 | `0023`, `0049`, `0167`, `0251`, `0268`, `0273`, `0276` |
| | | | | | THE SEED IS THE FAMILY'S POOL, AND THE FAMILY IS OURS BECAUSE THE ROM RECORDS A HIT POLICY AND NOT A PURPOSE (#1125 (b), second pass). ADR-0276 shipped the hard gate and left the effect FAMILY open because the vocabulary is player-facing; the player answered it -- damage/debuff default to nearest foe, healing/buff to nearest friendly. dec. 1: four families onto TWO pools, and four names for two answers is deliberate -- the seed needs two, the AUDIT needs four, because a classifier that booked everything as damage satisfies 'every seed matches its family' perfectly. Measured 88 damage / 126 debuff / 29 healing / 34 buff over the 278 skillset-reachable abilities. dec. 2: THE GATE RUNS FIRST AND THE PREFERENCE RUNS INSIDE IT -- a hit policy says *may this aim*, a family says *should it*; targets_for removes what the ROM or the kernel can fault and the family picks from what is LEFT, never the other way round. It never has to: the family's pool survives the gate on all 278, and 59 of those records carry dont_hit_allies or dont_hit_enemies, so the zero is the classifier's positive control and not a blind one. dec. 3: ADR-0276 dec. 10 IS NARROWED, NOT DELETED, AND ITS ARGUMENT IS WHAT CHANGES -- dec. 10's case was that the head of the gated list 'involves no new opinion at all', and a family preference IS a new opinion, so the justification moves from 'no guess' to 'a guess the player authorised' (ADR-0023 faithful-or-explicit). The head survives for Move (its aim is a DESTINATION), for Wait (the kernel never reads it) and for the one reachable ability with no family. Attack keeps ADR-0268 dec. 12's explicit Nearest Foe. dec. 4: FIVE ROUTES AND THE ORDER IS THE DECISION -- 6 named overrides, then target_reaction_type == receive_heal (20), then the status XOR (114), then formula (121, 43 rulings), then ability_type (16 Throw/Jump records carrying no formula). receive_heal is taken FIRST because it is the field the KERNEL reads (ABFLAG_HEALING), so host and GPU cannot disagree about what a heal is, and because Raise answers routes 1 and 2 both. dec. 5: ROUTE 2 IS AN XOR over (mode, polarity) -- inflicts&good -> buff, inflicts&bad -> debuff, cancel&good -> debuff, cancel&bad -> healing; cancel does NOT mean helpful (Esuna cleanses an ally, DispelMagic strips a FOE, both cancel). INFLICTS IS `mode != cancel`, NOT `mode == all`: the field has FOUR values and random (6) and separate (11) inflict too, so the mis-spelling inverts 17 records in BOTH directions. StasisSword (separate+Stop) is the one that REACHES the player -- no dont_hit_allies, so a family of buff survives the gate and seeds Nearest Ally onto a Stop-sword; NamelessSong (random+good) is masked by dont_hit_enemies, which is why one witness would not have been enough. dec. 6: 23 OF THE 30 STATUS POLARITIES ARE THE ROM'S OWN -- its dispel set (Despair/DispelMagic: Transparent, Reraise, Float, Haste, Shell, Protect, Regen, Reflect, Faith) and its cleanse set (Esuna/StigmaMagic/Deathspell2/DragonCare/Raise/Revive/Heal: 14 bad ones) are disjoint and partition them, which settles Faith, Transparent, Berserk, Oil and BloodSuck without a judgement call. SEVEN are ours (Charm, Crystal, DeathSentence, Innocent, Invite, Slow, Undead, all BAD) and are marked as ours; five are corroborated by dont_hit_allies. The table reads statuses[0] and that is only sound because NO record mixes good and bad -- asserted, not assumed. dec. 7: FORMULA 42 IS WHY THERE IS A PER-ABILITY ROUTE -- Praise and Threaten are both f42, both taking_damage, both carry no statuses, and they move Brave in opposite directions; the ROM records the MAGNITUDE without the sign (Praise/Preach are both x=50,y=4 and Threaten/Solution both x=90,y=20, the catalogue corroborating the pairing and still not naming the direction). ADR-0276 named f56 (Heal/Seal) as formula's first counterexample; f42 is the second and route 2 cannot answer it. dec. 8 (THE PRICE): NEAREST ALLY EXCLUDES THE CASTER and this decision makes 47 abilities worse for it. find_unit_by_criteria skips self on NEAREST and ONLY on NEAREST (stage_compute.glsl:247), so Cure moves Self -> Nearest Ally and a lone wounded healer heals NOBODY. 15 of the 47 carry range == 0 (centred on the caster) and two of those, Accumulate and Scream, are range 0 / effect_area 0 -- they affect the caster and nobody else, so Accumulate / Nearest Ally raises somebody else's PA. Built as `Nearest Ally` because the player said nearest four times; `Weakest Ally` (MOST_CRITICAL, which DOES include the caster) is a one-line alternative recorded in Considered options with its numbers. This is where ADR-0276's still-ungraded `range == 0` class stops being independent. dec. 9: the classifier is an ALMANAC MEMBER and it is a `rule` -- it is a fact about an ABILITY not about a screen, it COMPUTES rather than stores, and ADR-0273 dec. 5's tie-break points the same way at zero cost (36 arm-5 debt lines under either word, run both ways). It cannot live in AbilityDatabase.gd, which is AUTO-GENERATED. Live register 32 -> 33 published, 11 -> 12 rules. dec. 10: THE THREE CONTRADICTORY PREDICATES ARE RECONCILED BY DELETION AND REALIGNMENT, NOT BY A FOURTH -- is_damage is DELETED (it listed formula 10 as damage while NON_DAMAGE_FORMULAS two lines below listed 10 as not-damage, and it had ZERO callers); is_healing now reads target_reaction_type == receive_heal, the same field as ABFLAG_HEALING. That disagreed with the kernel on SIXTEEN records (Raise/Raise2 are f13; the fourteen Item records carry no formula key at all so the old predicate read 0 and called every potion not-a-heal) and the realignment changes NO behaviour today -- MEASURED: its one caller is inside an effect_area > 0 branch and not one of the sixteen has a non-zero effect_area. Both edits land in generate_ability_database.py too. FOUND WHILE RECONCILING THEM: is_healing's ONE CALLER IS THE BUG SHAPE docs/context/07-ability-hit-policy.md ALREADY RETIRED, surviving where nobody looked -- CombatLoop.gd:2175's AoE effect spray does `if is_healing and unit_team != caster_team: continue`, a FAMILY question answered with an HP-write flag. Measured over the 193 records that branch can reach, the gate disagrees with is_ally_side on 32 (Protect, Shell, Haste, Esuna, Carbunkle, Murasame, every Song, Chakra, StigmaMagic...) -- it sprays the effect on FOES and skips the caster's own party. Reachable and visible. NOT fixed here (ADR-0167); filed as #1148. dec. 11: the residual is ONE reachable ability and it is NAMED, not counted -- Move-GetJp is a MOVEMENT support ability in a skillset's actions list; it lands on nobody, so there is no pool to prefer. One-entry allowlist with BOTH arms (#424). The other residual is 42 records that carry an unruled formula and EVERY ONE IS UNREACHABLE (f1's sixteen monster attacks, a few monster specials, six records named `(Nothing)`); ratcheted, not tolerated. dec. 12: THE AUDIT IS PROVED BY MUTATION, because a green audit reads the same whether it consults the register or ignores it. Six mutations, each reds where predicted. THE FINDING IS THE FIFTH: making every formula rule DAMAGE leaves the per-family counts at 137/26/20/94 -- all four still NON-ZERO -- so the census control does NOT catch it and only the six named witnesses (Chakra, Accumulate, Yell, CheerUp, Wish, Scream, with ThrowStone as the control) do. REJECTED: Weakest Ally for the ally side (dec. 8's alternative, one line, recorded not taken); seeding Self for the fifteen range-0 ally abilities (right answer the day a rule-group-K scenario GRADES range == 0, and building on the unvalidated static reading would be the a-mechanism-that-could-explain-it mistake); keying on formula alone (f56 and then f42, which is not separable by any field at all); inferring from target_reaction_type (the six traps read as strikes; Wish spends the caster's HP to heal an ally); putting the table in GambitOptions (two of the three predicates it reconciles are in the addon and the shader); ruling the 42 unreachable monster formulas to reach a zero (a zero bought by guessing); declaring the member a `table`. SOFT SPOTS: S1 the seven polarity rulings are ours and only a reader can falsify them; Crystal, DeathSentence and Undead rest on FFT canon alone and Crystal is not even reachable. S2 range == 0 is still ungraded and this ADR raised its stakes on a NAMED set of fifteen. S3 the six f42 rulings guard a branch our kernel does not run. S4 route ORDER is a decision no test can falsify where two routes agree (Raise). S5 the zero-fallback control is a property of THIS tree -- a re-extraction that flips a dont_hit_* bit turns a correct family into a printed fallback rather than a red. |
| [0283](0283-the-gambit-rows-subject-is-its-own-column-and-the-slot-number-shares-a-lane-to-pay-for-it.md) The gambit row's SUBJECT is its own column, and the slot number shares a lane to pay for it | `engine` | · | · | 4 | `0023`, `0048`, `0049`, `0244`, `0255`, `0268`, `0270`, `0276`, `0278` |
| | | | | | THE GAMBIT ROW'S SUBJECT IS ITS OWN COLUMN, AND THE SLOT NUMBER SHARES A LANE TO PAY FOR IT (#1125 (c)). The ticket asked for `If` two levels deep; the player specified FOUR COLUMNS instead -- Action / Target / Subject / Condition -- with the subject a TWO-WAY SWITCH (My / Their), not the pool selector ADR-0268 dec. 2 priced. THE FINDING THAT MAKES IT MORE THAN COSMETIC, RECORDED NOWHERE BEFORE: the fold made a sentence the kernel can already run UNSAYABLE. evaluate_gambits_up_to is two-pass and Pass 2 walks find_nth_nearest at rank 1,2,3 RETESTING the condition, for exactly three condition targets (NEAREST_ENEMY, NEAREST_ALLY, NEAREST_ALLY_OR_KO) and writes VERDICT_NOT_RETRYABLE for every other; every folded `Ally HP<X%` row bound its subject to MOST_CRITICAL friendlies, which _target_selector_to_gpu maps to TARGET_LOWEST_HP_ALLY -- one of the rejected types. So the screen offered the one ally subject that switches the rank walk OFF and 'the nearest ally whose HP is below half' could not be authored. NOT an E1 skip: it encodes cleanly, which is why no guard saw it. dec. 1: Subject is a part again; `Their` is a COPY of the aim so cond_target_type == action_target_type, which is what the rank walk requires, and the copy is re-derived on a To press rather than shared (a shared selector rewrites the subject of a condition the player is not looking at). dec. 2: `Always` LEAVES the screen's vocabulary -- blank IS the absence of a condition (check_gambit_conditions returns true at zero), Type.ALWAYS stays on the enum for #895's operators and old saves, condition_label reads either spelling as `-`, and Gambit._init writes an EMPTY conditions array so the domain has ONE spelling. `-` and not the empty string, because an empty column is one the chevron points at nothing beside. dec. 3: Subject and Condition are blank TOGETHER, and a blank condition leaves condition_target MIRRORING the aim rather than cleared -- cond_target_type still GATES the slot, so cleared to SELF an `Attack / Nearest Foe / - / -` row would gate on the ACTOR existing (always true) and fire at a pool it never checked was there. dec. 4: THE CHEVRON GAP IS 13 PX BECAUSE 11 WAS PHOTOGRAPHED AND IS WRONG -- what matters is gap-9 blank px (FONT.BIN's arrow carries one empty column on its left; every other glyph in the row inks its FULL advance, so two letters inside a word already touch). 13 gives 4 blank px = a word space; 11 gives 2, and at 2 a full-cap `Weakest Ally` followed by the subject's chevron renders `Weakest Ally>Their`, an arrow BETWEEN two values rather than a cursor on one. A wrong meaning, not a tight fit, and no layout assertion could see it (ADR-0244). dec. 5: THE SLOT NUMBER AND `Do`'S CHEVRON SHARE ONE LANE, which is what pays for the fourth column -- the row wants 228 px against 218, and the 10 px is available nowhere else: COL_DO_CAP has a CLIFF between 48 and 46 px where 25 of 228 action-ability names become 102; the window is pinned to the ROM lower panel's margins matched ON INK (ADR-0255 Am. 1); `+1` measures 12 px and dec. 3 of 0268 says the cap needs the affordance; and the glove's ink stops at x=18, measured on a capture, so 22 is the leftmost start (x=18 was tried and photographed with the glove over the number). The two share because the number is a PER-ROW readout and the chevron appears on one part of one row -- never wanted at the same x at the same time; the cost is the focused row's number while its Do is focused, the one row the glove is already on. dec. 6: is_empty() REWRITTEN and DIRECTION-TESTED BOTH WAYS, because authored_gambits filters on it BEFORE encoding: too permissive drops an authored rule (row reads back, unit is not under it), too strict lets a blank slot occupy a priority (rule A1). Four things at once -- WAIT, aim is the actor, subject is the actor, and NOTHING TESTED (no condition whose type is not ALWAYS, so both spellings of blank read blank). THE ARM THAT MATTERS: `Attack / Nearest Foe / - / -` is an unconditional RULE and a blank-means-empty predicate DELETES it silently; it survives on action_kind, checked first and alone. dec. 7: the safety net's row reads `Attack / Nearest Foe / - / -`, re-deriving the string ADR-0270 dec. 1 states -- off GambitEncoder.safety_net_gambit() through the same label function, so it changed without the row being edited. TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY RE-READING, NOT BY A RED TEST: (1) _seed_aim is the SECOND place action_target is written and did not re-mirror, so pressing Attack on an empty slot left condition_target at SELF -- seeded control reds exactly one assertion; (2) `To = Them` + `Subject = Their` encodes cond_target_type = TARGET_THEM and select_target answers `case TARGET_THEM: return -1`, so VERDICT_NO_CANDIDATE every tick forever while the row reads back perfectly -- the cell this shape CREATED, cut in TWO places (mirror_of refuses to copy a TRIGGERING aim; aim_verdict grades AIM_NEVER_RESOLVES so targets_for withholds the row), with `Them` kept in targets() so a save holding it reads back as itself rather than through _target_text's Self fallback. aim_class's docstring asserted the opposite ('a subject is never itself TRIGGERING, so there is no cycle to guard against') -- true of the folded catalogue, false the moment Their became a copy. REJECTED: the two-level `If` drill the ticket asked for (cheaper, and it hides the subject in the place the ticket complains it is hidden); TWO LINES PER GAMBIT, ADR-0268's own recorded fallback, unused because the row fits; dropping the slot number outright (-6 px, tiled and CAPTURED and it works with Do at 52, but dec. 5 gets the px for free, and the shot showed two right-pointing cursors adjacent); shortening `To` to Near/Weak Ally (-12 px, closes the gap alone -- a player-facing vocabulary change bought for pixels); `Self`/`Them` as the subject words (`Them` already means something else one column left, and the subject is a POSSESSIVE); reclaiming the 4 px of left pad (the glove is there -- only the 2 px at the right were taken, PLUS_RIGHT_X 238 -> 240, captured 2 px clear of the border). CONSEQUENCES: Do elides 21 of 228 where it elided 14 (and the prose '17 of 265' both 0268 and GambitSurfaceMenu carried mixed two sets -- 265 is abilities.json, the screen renders the almanac's ROM spellings, of which 228 are skillset-reachable); Part.IF renumbered 2 -> 3 and the widget MIRRORS the enum, so GambitSurfaceTest's mirror equality is what keeps the chevron on the column it names; the (verb x aim) audit walks 6,744 cells where it walked 5,058 -- sensible 5,245 / no_op 5 / forbidden 1,011 / ignored 19 / unnamed 184 / never_resolves 280 -- and the extra axis value is a raw `Them` subject the SCREEN cannot produce, carried because it is the only input reaching the new grade and a grade that only reads zero is indistinguishable from one nothing examined; the census now prints EVERY grade plus anything its list does not name and asserts its rows SUM to the cell count, because never_resolves was counted-and-unprinted for one run and the only symptom was five rows that no longer added up; pre-ADR-0283 saves read as sentences (subject_label reads the POOL, not a catalogue probe) but keep their non-retryable behaviour until re-authored, and this ADR does not migrate them. VERIFIED: GambitSurfaceTest 252/0; GambitEncoderTest PASS with two new arms (the rank walk, whose NEGATIVE CONTROL is the fold's own subject still encoding cleanly and still not retryable, so the defect was never a skip; and the circular cell through both cuts); GambitScenarioRunnerTest 91 scenarios green (85 PASS / 5 XFAIL / 1 pre-existing XPASS); GambitVerdictCellsTest 80/0; GambitSafetyNetTest, GambitEncodeSchemaTest, AdjustmentTurnTest PASS; project-wide parse sweep clean. SOFT SPOTS: S1 `+N` is still flush against a max-width condition (`MP<50%+1`) -- dec. 3 of 0268's geometry unchanged in kind but now reachable with a 6-character condition instead of an 11-character one. S2 `Their` over a `Self` aim is `My` under another name; left offered because a subject list that changed length with the aim moves its row indices under the cursor, and unlike the Them pair it RESOLVES. S3 the 4-blank-px requirement is a reading of two captures, not a threshold -- 2 px was judged wrong and 4 right, and 3 was never photographed because 13 px fit. |
| [0291](0291-dont-target-self-is-the-cursor-rule-and-dont-hit-caster-is-the-splash-rule-and-the-wiki-named-a-third-that-the-engine-never-reads.md) `dont_target_self` is the CURSOR rule, `dont_hit_caster` is the SPLASH rule, and the wiki named a third the engine never reads | `engine` | · | · | 1 | `0013`, `0023`, `0049`, `0268`, `0276`, `0283`, `0285` |
| | | | | | `dont_target_self` IS THE CURSOR RULE, `dont_hit_caster` IS THE SPLASH RULE, AND THE WIKI NAMED A THIRD THE ENGINE NEVER READS. The ask was "enforce more of the ROM's targeting rules in gambit selection"; 13 targeting flags are extracted and the gate read 3 (ADR-0049's dont_hit_* triple). The flag NAMES come from the FFHacktics wiki and ADR-0049 carries no ROM address either, so every decision here is rooted at an instruction instead. dec. 1: the attribute table is SCUS 0x8005FBF0, 14 bytes x 368 records, and BATTLE.BIN forms a record pointer in exactly 13 places -- enumerating every mask applied to a byte loaded from one of them is a CLOSED enumeration, not a name search. dec. 2: `dont_target_self` (flags1 & 0x01) removes a TILE FROM THE CURSOR'S REACH -- FUN_8017A290 @0x8017A290 builds the selectable-tile table at 0x80192DD8, marks the caster's own entry selectable at 0x8017A410-0x8017A418, then tests the bit at 0x8017A444 and `sb zero`s that entry at 0x8017A450. A different enforcement point from ADR-0049's AoE distribution walk, and the repo had been treating one rule as if it were both. dec. 3: THE AUTHORABLE GAP IS FOUR ABILITIES AND SAYING FOUR IS PART OF THE FINDING -- 156 records carry the flag, 152 also carry dont_hit_caster and keep ADR-0049's grade, leaving Revive(107)/Invitation(116)/Wish(152)/BloodSuck(200), all skillset-reachable, all effect_area 0. Heal(149) is NOT in the set: it was the opening example, the requester withdrew it, its record carries neither flag (confirmed in live RAM) and the gate is right about it. dec. 4: the grade is AIM_UNTARGETABLE, ordered AFTER the hit-policy line so `untargetable` counts exactly the gap and nothing else; per ADR-0276 dec. 12 it lands in the GRADER and the offer list follows. dec. 5: `targeting_ai_only` (flags4 & 0x01) IS INERT -- 58 records carry it and BATTLE.BIN never reads the bit; the zero is not blind because the SAME BYTE's other bits are found by the same enumeration (direct 0x20 @0x8017B8E4, requires_sword 0x08 @0x801819FC, blade_grasp 0x10 @0x8018D05C, evadeable 0x02 @0x8018583C), and Draw Out / Sing / Dance / Chakra / Punch Art all carry it and are all player-commanded. NO GATE IS BUILT ON IT -- a rule from that name would be a rule invented. dec. 6: `auto_target` (flags1 & 0x02) is real and means NO TARGET IS SELECTED AT ALL -- FUN_8017A8C0 @0x8017AA6C returns mode 2, the same code as the action kinds that take no target; left ungated deliberately, as grading 45 aim columns AIM_IGNORED is a separate behaviour change. OPEN AND NAMED: the KERNEL still has no dont_target_self counterpart and all four gap abilities are effect_area 0, the same single-target hole #1144 / ADR-0276 dec. 5 and K2's XFAIL open on -- THIS IS THE SURFACE HALF ONLY; and AIM_FORBIDDEN may be over-strict for effect_area > 0, where the ROM lets the cursor land on your own tile and the splash simply skips you. EVIDENCE: static met and mechanistically complete; dynamic PARTIALLY met -- the extracted table is byte-exact against live PSX RAM for all 368 records, the four gap abilities read flags1=0x09 and Heal reads 0x08 in live RAM, and the engine's working copy at 0x801938F0 carries a set bit for the action in flight, but the cursor REFUSING the tile is not shown live (no savestate exists with a targeting cursor open). |
| [0296](0296-the-to-list-is-ordered-by-the-abilitys-family-and-the-rom-stores-the-polarity-we-hand-wrote.md) The `To` list is ordered by the ability's family, and the ROM stores the polarity we hand-wrote | `engine` | · | · | 1 | `0048`, `0276`, `0278`, `0283`, `0285`, `0291` |
| | | | | | THE `To` LIST IS ORDERED BY THE ABILITY'S FAMILY, AND THE ROM STORES THE POLARITY WE HAND-WROTE. Asked for "common sense sorting based on the spell -- healing spells have ally above foe, damage the opposite... self should be on top if it's self targetable and positive". The taxonomy did NOT need building: AbilityFamily (ADR-0278) already classifies damage/healing/buff/debuff and `is_ally_side` IS that polarity -- it was wired to the DEFAULT aim (seed_aim_for) and to nothing else, while targets() returned one hardcoded Self-then-foe-then-ally order for every ability. dec. 1: the order is the family's and it is the SAME decision as the seed, deliberately not a second classifier -- sorted by one rule and seeded by another, the head of the list and the default could disagree in front of the player. healing/buff -> Self, ally block, foe block; damage/debuff -> foe block, ally block, Self, which SINKS below ally because `Fire / Self` is worse than `Fire / Ally`. dec. 2: it REORDERS and can never add or remove -- runs after the gate on what the gate left, so ADR-0276 dec. 12 is untouched and the one-decision guard (a SET comparison) is unaffected; ties keep targets() order via an index-decorated sort so nearest/weakest cannot shuffle on a sort-stability accident. dec. 3: `Self` rises only when POSITIVE and SELF-TARGETABLE and NEITHER half is re-asked -- positive is is_ally_side, self-targetable is whether the Self ROW IS STILL IN THE LIST, which ADR-0291's gate already answered. Wish is healing and therefore positive and still never floats Self, because Self is not there to float; if it ever reports Self first then either the gate stopped withholding or the sorter started resurrecting. dec. 4: an UNKNOWN-family ability keeps targets() order untouched (42 records, plus Move/Wait which have no family) -- ordering them would invent a claim the classifier declined to make, AND that row is the test's CONTROL, because "Cure leads with an ally row" is satisfied by a sorter that does it for everything. M-pM-^_M-^TM-4 dec. 5: THE ROM STORES THIS POLARITY AND ADR-0278 SAYS IT DOES NOT -- that ADR built AbilityFamily as "a rule and not a table BECAUSE NOTHING IN THE ROM STORES IT", but ability_data.json's 4-byte AI block carries ai_target_allies (72) / ai_target_enemies (315) / ai_only_allies (17) / ai_only_enemies (41) over 512 records, agreeing 12 of 12 with our hand-written families on a Cure/Protect/Fire/Poison spot check. The player's hunch ("I'm sure the enemy AI implements something") was right and the data is extracted but NOT SHIPPED. NOT used here: ordering on a second source re-opens the disagreement dec. 1 avoids, and these are FFHacktics WIKI names -- ADR-0291 dec. 5 is what that is worth, where `targeting_ai_only` reads like a rule and is read by NOTHING. 12/12 is corroboration, not a decompile. Root it first (#1227). OPEN: `Attack` has no family so it STILL opens on Self (#1228) -- a verb's polarity is a different claim from an ability's and was not smuggled in. |

(1) THE TESTS LEVER IS SUBLINEAR -- a published name is freed only by its LAST namer, so moving 41% of the tests frees 24% of the names; the decision is worth 11 names TODAY, not 45 (45 is the value at an unreachable limit). (2) THE MOVE IS NOT GATED -- ADR-0212 dec.2 already forbade deferring a facade because another channel is open, and dec.4 deferred it because the TEST channel was open. Nothing about the git mv changes with the tests decision; only the LENGTH of the facade const list, and a row deleted later is free. FACADE IS 56 ROWS under the achievable move (22 published + 34 test-only names pinned by a stuck test), not 22. Of 126 tests naming a UI member, 52 are MOVABLE and 74 STUCK; the bar (zero host classes, zero host autoloads) is STRUCTURAL -- the stranger rig has no host scripts and no autoload block -- and all 20 existing addon-owned tests obey it (that zero is controlled: the same pass returns 74 on UI). The 74 are ONE CORRELATED CLUSTER, not 74 problems: 52 of them name nothing outside {CharacterCatalog 35, PromotedRosterSeeder 33, Tune 23, UI3Registry 12}, and unsticking ONE is worth ~0 (PromotedRosterSeeder alone frees 0 tests, CharacterCatalog alone 2, the three together 43). Tune was ONE OF SEVEN -- a member may reach NO autoload identifier at all, because all EIGHT stranger rigs declare an EMPTY [autoload] block and an addon cannot ship project.godot entries (ADR-0262 dec.6). 49 reaches stand in 16 of the 121 members: PSXDisplay 25 (#1263), DebugConfig 8 (#1271), CharacterCatalog 6 (#1272), SfxRouter 5 (#1273), EventBus 4 (#1274), UI3Registry 1 (dissolves at the move). Tune is 0 -- the positive control, same instrument returned 107 before step 2. WHY ONLY ONE WAS ON THE BOARD: #1263 measured PSXDisplay correctly at pass 3 and framed it as ONE PORT LACKING TWO VERBS, so nobody enumerated the other 26 autoloads; a ticket naming one instance HIDES the category. THE GATE CRITERION (missing from ADR-0307 dec.4): a prerequisite GATES when the deliverable cannot be PRODUCED, and is a scope decision when it is produced at a different SIZE -- ask what the artifact looks like if the decision goes the wrong way; a 67-row facade is an artifact, an addon calling an undefined DebugConfig is not. CharacterCatalog.gd ALREADY lives in addons/exmateria_catalogue and the HOST declares the autoload: the script travels, the registration does not, and 7 of 27 autoloads are already this shape. Instrument is a RATCHET with THREE failure directions (new/grown, PAID-DOWN-but-still-listed, empty subject), identifiers PARSED from project.godot never listed. |
